# Decisions

Every assumption and design choice these docs make beyond the literal [seed spec](../seed-specs.md), in one place, so any of them can be revisited deliberately.

> [!NOTE]
> **TL;DR.** 90 decisions. D-01 to D-44, D-56, D-57, D-73, D-78 to D-80, D-84, D-85, D-87, D-89, and D-90 are settled by the owner. D-45 to D-55, D-58 to D-72, D-74 to D-77, and D-83 are proposals (D-81, D-82, and D-88 are superseded by D-90) that stand as the plan until changed. **D-56/D-57**: a strict, layered design system, and an interface defined entirely as data, with no HTML or CSS in the app. The newest owner decisions: **D-41/D-42**: private sharing with named audiences through magic links and passcodes, in the first release. **D-43**: download first, browser second. **D-44**: agent-friendly through MCP, a command-line tool, deep links, and AGENTS.md. Key owner decisions: **D-14**: 20 px corners on Windows (and Linux) by drawing the window and rebuilding window management; macOS uses its native window. **D-16**: Spotlight is <kbd>Ctrl/⌘</kbd>+<kbd>/</kbd>, and <kbd>Ctrl/⌘</kbd>+<kbd>K</kbd> is the link key. **D-19**: support every platform possible. **D-21**: no OAuth app, no registration, and no hosting by the project. Sign-in goes through Git's own sign-in tools that the user owns (with device codes), and each user's Focus Notes is an isolated instance in their own GitHub account (**D-38**). **D-90**: the browser keeps its own notes and reads the instance's notes; only the desktop app writes to GitHub, and private notes reach the browser sealed, as a share with an audience of one. The owner accepted every remaining proposal on 2026-10-13.

## Status legend

| Status | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| **Decided (owner)** | Stated or confirmed directly by the project owner. |
| **Decided (accepted)** | Proposed in these docs and accepted by the owner ("go with what you proposed", 2026-10-13). |
| **Proposed** | Proposed in these docs to close a gap. Treated as the plan until the owner changes it. |
| **Superseded** | Replaced by a later decision, kept for history. |

## Implementation updates

**Compact backup-card icon and spacing, 17 October 2026 (D-132 implementation; owner request).** The status tile inside the backup card is 36 px square at the default UI scale, using a compact `StepMark` variant. Internal padding is 16 px on all sides, using the popover's comfortable-padding variant. Setup's larger hero marks, their responsive sizing and other popovers' padding are unchanged. Affects [User can](../product/spec/user-can.md) and [Main UI](../experience/main-ui.html).

**Save indicator, 17 October 2026 (D-132 and D-161 implementation; owner request).** Remove the desktop corner's username, retain an always-visible circular save control, and show backup details in an accessible hover card. Its border spins while backup runs, with live status in the card; reduced motion keeps the ring still. Reuse the existing recorded backup timestamp without inventing a historical time when it is unavailable. The control follows the pinned Files edge, falling back above the sheet when the window has no side room. Browser vault controls remain unchanged. Affects [Requirements](../product/requirements.md), [User can](../product/spec/user-can.md) and [Main UI](../experience/main-ui.html).

**Viewport interaction, 17 October 2026 (D-160 implementation; owner request, built; corrected by the owner the same evening).** Treat the visible document canvas as a click target, not only its narrow text column. A plain left-margin click selects the aligned row's block in the existing block-selection mode, the caret at that row's start, as Word's selection bar does; a right-margin click places the caret at the aligned row's end and selects nothing; a click below the note goes to its end without adding content; Shift+click extends text selection. A drag that starts in a margin or above or below the note selects text from where the press landed to the row under the pointer, scrolling the note past its edges. (First built with the right margin selecting the block; the owner said they'd mixed up the sides.) Controls, scrolling and touch gestures are excluded. Affects [Editing](../experience/editing.md), [User can](../product/spec/user-can.md) and [Requirements](../product/requirements.md).

| Update (17 October 2026) | Status | Implementation | Affected pages |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Window input (D-147) | Proposed; built | Window move and resize pointer events are captured before a surface can stop them from bubbling. Tests cover moving and all eight resize handles while an account check is pending. | [Perceived performance](../architecture/perceived-performance.md) |
| Bounded account checks (D-149) | Proposed; built | Desktop GitHub API requests have a 10-second deadline, including response bodies, while preserving caller cancellation. A stalled account check shows the existing offline problem and can be retried, instead of waiting indefinitely. | [Desktop and Git](../architecture/desktop-and-git.md), [Perceived performance](../architecture/perceived-performance.md) |
| Portable IPC identity (D-147, D-149) | Proposed; built | The portable's short temporary path exposed a raw URL comparison: Chromium's literal `~` did not equal Node's `%7E`, so both setup and window IPC were rejected. Compare the decoded path with the exact renderer entry file, preserving Windows path case rules and refusing every other file or protocol. Verify packaged setup and frame commands from a tilde-containing path, not just development journeys or CLI smoke tests. | [Desktop and Git](../architecture/desktop-and-git.md) |

## Owner decisions

| ID | Decision | Status | Affects |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| D-01 | **Editor engine: Tiptap (ProseMirror).** Only MIT-licensed Tiptap packages are used. The paid Tiptap bundles (Collaboration, Content AI, Conversion, Documents/Cloud) are excluded. Markdown comes from either `@tiptap/markdown` (MIT) or our own remark bridge, whichever passes the round-trip corpus in the M0 spike. | Decided (owner) | [Editor engine](../architecture/editor-engine.md) |
| D-02 | ~~Desktop sign-in through the app's own GitHub OAuth device flow.~~ The wish for device sign-in at the CLI level is kept, delivered through Git's own sign-in tools (D-21). | Superseded by D-21 | [First run and sign-in](../experience/first-run.md) |
| D-03 | **Trigger size: a 10 px *diameter* red dot inside a 30 px *diameter* frosted circle.** | Decided (owner) | [Main UI](../experience/main-ui.html) |
| D-14 | **Window corners by platform.** Windows (and Linux, where a compositor allows it): a transparent, frameless window with 20 px corners, and **all window management rebuilt by the app** (moving, resizing, maximize and restore, edge and corner snapping, a Snap Layouts picker, the system menu, the shadow, and multi-monitor scaling). **macOS: the system's native window**, with no 20 px requirement. | Decided (owner) | [Desktop app](../experience/desktop-app.html#window-management-on-windows-and-linux) |
| D-16 | **Spotlight opens with <kbd>Ctrl/⌘</kbd>+<kbd>/</kbd>.** <kbd>Ctrl+K</kbd> in the original request was a typo. <kbd>Ctrl/⌘</kbd>+<kbd>K</kbd> is therefore the link key everywhere, as in other editors. <kbd>Ctrl/⌘</kbd>+<kbd>Shift</kbd>+<kbd>P</kbd> also opens Spotlight on every keyboard layout. | Decided (owner) | [Keyboard shortcuts](../experience/keyboard-shortcuts.md), [Spotlight](../experience/spotlight.html) |
| D-19 | **Support as many platforms as possible:** macOS, Windows, Linux, and iPadOS and iOS with a hardware keyboard (web build). | Decided (owner) | [Keyboard shortcuts](../experience/keyboard-shortcuts.md#platforms) |
| D-20 | **License: the app is MIT.** Every shipped dependency must be under an MIT-compatible permissive license (policy below). | Decided (owner) | [Editor engine](../architecture/editor-engine.md#licensing) |
| D-21 | **No OAuth app, no registration, no hosting by the project.** Each user's Focus Notes must work with only things the user owns. Desktop sign-in goes through **Git's own sign-in helpers**: Git Credential Manager or the GitHub CLI, both with device codes, or a **fine-grained token** the user creates from a pre-filled GitHub link. The credential lives only in Git's helper, so the app and the terminal share it. The only hosting is GitHub Pages (each instance's own site) and GitHub Releases (D-87). | Decided (owner) | [Desktop and Git → Sign-in](../architecture/desktop-and-git.md#sign-in), [First run](../experience/first-run.md) |
| D-24 | **Desktop window:** a custom title bar on every platform, with 20 px rounded corners where D-14 allows. | Decided (owner) | [Desktop app](../experience/desktop-app.html) |
| D-25 | **Themes:** Light, Dark, and System (auto). | Decided (owner) | [Theming](../experience/theming.md) |
| D-26 | **Toolbar:** at most 6 entries. Anything beyond that goes into a ⋯ (more) menu. | Decided (owner) | [Main UI](../experience/main-ui.html#the-minimal-toolbar) |
| D-27 | **Keyboard:** a thorough shortcut strategy that follows the most popular apps, from basic to niche pro shortcuts. | Decided (owner) | [Keyboard shortcuts](../experience/keyboard-shortcuts.md) |
| D-28 | **Find, find and replace, and a Spotlight-style search-and-act panel** (on <kbd>Ctrl/⌘</kbd>+<kbd>/</kbd>, see D-16). | Decided (owner) | [Find and replace](../experience/find-and-replace.md), [Spotlight](../experience/spotlight.html) |
| D-35 | **Every UX and UI decision is grounded in a chain:** customer promise → scenario (job to be done) → user can → view or component → design decision → requirement. | Decided (owner) | [The articulation chain](../product/spec/chain.md) |
| D-36 | **Tables are edited the way Microsoft Loop tables are, and text is formatted with a simple contextual toolbar.** Theme management is designed around when and why people theme, what they bring, and presets, randomizing, or manual editing. | Decided (owner) | [Tables](../design/components/tables.html), [Text formatting](../design/components/text-formatting.html), [Theme manager](../design/views/theme-manager.html) |
| D-38 | **Every user gets an isolated instance in their own GitHub account:** a public notes repository (created from the project's template), a private repository as its submodule, their own GitHub Pages site, and their own share-link keys. The app creates all of it on their computer. The project's repository holds only the source, docs, releases, the template, and the reusable publish workflow. | Decided (owner), amended by D-87 | [Repository and publishing → Who owns what](../architecture/repository-and-publishing.html#who-owns-what), [Creating a workspace](../architecture/desktop-and-git.md#creating-a-workspace) |
| D-41 | **Sharing with audiences.** A private note or folder is shared with an *audience* identified only by a **name**, through a **magic link**, a **custom passcode**, a generated passcode, or a combination. This follows the access model of the owner's personal site. Readers enter their name and passcode, or open the link. The content stays in the private repository and is published only as ciphertext. | Decided (owner) | [Sharing and visibility](../design/views/sharing.md) |
| D-42 | **Private sharing is part of the first release (P0)**, not a later addition, because the owner asked for it as a core capability. | Decided (owner) | [Roadmap → M5](roadmap.md) |
| D-43 | **Getting started: download first, browser second.** The product site's Get started page leads with the desktop download, and the app runs setup. *Start in your browser* (M6) is a secondary path that uses only GitHub's own pages and a token. | Superseded by D-80 | [Get started](../design/views/get-started.md) |
| D-44 | **Agent-friendly by design:** a local MCP server, a command-line tool with JSON output, `focus-notes://` deep links, an `AGENTS.md` in every workspace, published JSON Schemas, and `llms.txt` on the product site. | Decided (owner) | [Agents and automation](../architecture/agents.md) |
| D-56 | **A strict, layered design system:** tokens, primitives, layouts, compound components, fragments, and views, each using only the layers below it. Every visual value is a token. | Decided (owner) | [Design system](../design/system/overview.html) |
| D-57 | **The app's interface is data.** Every view is a JSON document mapped onto design-system components, so the app has no HTML or CSS of its own. Markup and styles exist only in the design system's primitives and layouts. | Decided (owner) | [Views as data](../design/system/views-as-data.md) |

## Accepted proposals

| ID | Decision | Status | Affects |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| D-04 | **Focus mode default:** on for new installs, since it's a signature feature. | Decided (accepted) | [Focus mode](../experience/focus-mode.md) |
| D-05 | **New notes are private** by default, so nothing gets published by accident. | Decided (accepted) | [Files and sharing](../experience/files-and-sharing.md) |
| D-06 | **Save state UI:** saving is invisible. Only failures or unsynced offline edits show, as a status arc on the trigger plus a toast the first time. | Decided (accepted) | [Feedback and dialogs](../design/components/feedback-and-dialogs.md#save-and-sync-status) |
| D-07 | **Submodule pointer cadence:** the notes repository's pointer to the private repository moves only for publishing-relevant private changes (share grants, shared notes), plus once a day. Since D-90 every private note is in the vault, so every private change is publishing-relevant: the desktop app moves the pointer after each private push, batched so a writing session makes at most one public commit every few minutes, and the browser's vault follows within one deploy. | Decided (accepted), amended by D-90 | [Repository and publishing](../architecture/repository-and-publishing.html) |
| D-08 | **Public URL stability:** renaming or moving a public note leaves a redirect at the old URL, found through the note's front-matter `id`. | Decided (accepted) | [Content model](../architecture/content-model.md#public-urls) |
| D-09 | **Private mount path:** `private/` (the reference `personal-site` uses `private-pages/`). | Decided (accepted) | [Content model](../architecture/content-model.md) |
| D-10 | **Desktop first.** The Electron app is the primary writing client and ships in the first release. In that release the web build serves public pages and share links, and browser editing follows in M6. | Superseded by D-80 | [Roadmap](roadmap.md) |
| D-11 | **Component syntax:** generic Markdown directives (`::name{}` / `:::name`) for future components. | Decided (accepted) | [Content model](../architecture/content-model.md#components) |
| D-12 | **Markdown-only formatting:** no underline, color, or highlight, because GFM can't represent them. | Decided (accepted) | [Editing](../experience/editing.md#inline-formatting) |
| D-13 | ~~**Browser editing (MW) uses a fine-grained token the user creates**, limited to their notes repositories and kept in the browser (see D-81).~~ The browser holds no GitHub credential and never writes to a repository; only the desktop app does (D-90). | Superseded by D-90 | [Architecture overview](../architecture/overview.html) |
| D-15 | **Windows and Linux window buttons** are drawn by the app and shown on hover. Hovering maximize opens the app's own Snap Layouts picker, because the native one isn't available to transparent windows. | Decided (accepted) | [Desktop app → Title bar](../experience/desktop-app.html#title-bar) |
| D-17 | **Focus mode shortcut:** <kbd>Ctrl/⌘</kbd>+<kbd>Shift</kbd>+<kbd>D</kbd>, with <kbd>F8</kbd> as an alternative (Typora). <kbd>Ctrl/⌘</kbd>+<kbd>Shift</kbd>+<kbd>F</kbd> is kept for "find in all notes" (VS Code convention). | Decided (accepted) | [Focus mode](../experience/focus-mode.md#turning-it-on-and-off) |
| D-18 | **The six toolbar actions:** New note, Open, Search, Save, Focus, Theme. Everything else is in ⋯. | Decided (accepted) | [Main UI](../experience/main-ui.html#the-minimal-toolbar) |
| D-22 | **System Git on the direct downloads; the Store package carries its own (amended by D-228).** The installer and the portable run the user's own `git`, so configuration, hooks, commit signing, and credentials are the same as in the terminal; their first run checks for Git and helps install it. The Microsoft Store package brings its own Git and sign-in helper and never offers to install anything (D-228). | Decided (accepted), amended | [First run and sign-in](../experience/first-run.md), D-228 |
| D-23 | **Save and sync:** edits are written to disk within 1 second. A commit and push happens when the writer presses <kbd>Ctrl/⌘</kbd>+<kbd>S</kbd>, switches or closes a note, quits, or stops typing for 60 seconds (configurable). | Superseded by D-133 (when it backs up) and D-132 (what saving shows, and Save to cloud); writing within 1 second stands | [Desktop and Git → Save and sync](../architecture/desktop-and-git.md#save-and-sync) |
| D-29 | **Fonts may use the SIL Open Font License (OFL-1.1).** Bundled fonts: Inter, Source Serif 4, JetBrains Mono, Atkinson Hyperlegible, OpenDyslexic. The licenses are confirmed in CI. | Decided (accepted) | [Design foundations](../design/foundations.md#type) |
| D-30 | **A note's file name follows its title until the note is first published**, then stays fixed unless renamed on purpose (with a URL warning). | Decided (accepted) | [Files panel](../design/views/files.html#names-and-titles) |
| D-31 | **Appearance has a light slot and a dark slot.** System swaps between the two chosen themes. Reading settings are separate from color themes. | Decided (accepted) | [Theme manager](../design/views/theme-manager.html#the-model) |
| D-32 | **One icon family: Fluent UI System Icons (MIT).** | Decided (accepted) | [Design foundations](../design/foundations.md#icons) |
| D-33 | **Undo instead of confirmation**, except for changes of audience, deletes at scale, deleting public notes, and discarding text in a conflict. | Decided (accepted) | [Feedback and dialogs](../design/components/feedback-and-dialogs.md) |
| D-34 | **Recently deleted is built from Git history** (30 days shown), not a trash folder in the repository. | Decided (accepted) | [Files panel](../design/views/files.html#deleting-and-restoring) |
| D-37 | **Working personas** (Writer, Power user, Tinkerer, Comfort-sensitive writer, Reader) are used as stated, and are checked against real users once there are any. | Decided (accepted) | [The articulation chain](../product/spec/chain.md#personas-used-in-scenarios) |
| D-39 | **Cheat sheet key:** <kbd>Ctrl/⌘</kbd>+<kbd>Shift</kbd>+<kbd>/</kbd> (<kbd>Ctrl/⌘</kbd>+<kbd>?</kbd>), because <kbd>Ctrl/⌘</kbd>+<kbd>/</kbd> is now Spotlight (D-16). | Decided (accepted) | [Keyboard shortcuts](../experience/keyboard-shortcuts.md#app-and-window) |
| D-40 | **This documentation is published on the project's own GitHub Pages site** (with the download page), not inside each user's notes site. | Decided (accepted) | [Repository and publishing](../architecture/repository-and-publishing.html#who-owns-what) |

## Proposed

| ID | Decision | Status | Affects |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| D-45 | **Agent permissions start closed.** Agents can read public notes. Reading private notes, editing, publishing, and sharing each need the writer to turn them on in Settings → Agents, optionally limited to folders. Agents can never change visibility or create audiences. Every agent edit is a separate commit with an agent trailer, and can be undone. | Proposed | [Agents → Permissions](../architecture/agents.md#permissions) |
| D-46 | **Synced settings and custom themes live in the private repository** (`private/.focus-notes/`). Storing them in the public notes repository would publish preferences. Without a private repository they fall back to the notes repository, and the app says so. | Proposed | [Security and privacy](../architecture/security-and-privacy.md), [Content model](../architecture/content-model.md) |
| D-47 | **`private/` tracks `main` and syncs as its own repository**, with `submodule.recurse` off. Otherwise a recursive pull would reset private notes to the older pointer the publish workflow uses (D-07). | Proposed | [Desktop and Git](../architecture/desktop-and-git.md), [Repository and publishing](../architecture/repository-and-publishing.html) |
| D-48 | **Users' publish workflows are pinned by commit SHA** and verify the reader bundle's checksum and attestation. Updates arrive as pull requests the user merges. | Proposed (restored by D-87) | [Repository and publishing](../architecture/repository-and-publishing.html) |
| D-49 | **This repository becomes a pnpm monorepo** (desktop, web, core, publishing, MCP and command-line tool, template source, schemas, docs), built with TypeScript, Vite, electron-builder, Changesets, Vitest, and Playwright. | Proposed | [Distribution](distribution.md#layout-of-this-repository) |
| D-50 | **Use established standards before inventing anything:** CommonMark and GFM, YAML front matter, W3C DTCG tokens, WCAG 2.2 AA, WAI-ARIA APG, JSON Schema 2020-12, SemVer, Keep a Changelog, Conventional Commits, OpenSSF Scorecard, SLSA provenance, CycloneDX, MCP, AGENTS.md, and llms.txt. | Proposed | [Standards](../architecture/standards.md) |
| D-51 | **Distribution:** GitHub Releases first, then winget and Homebrew (P1). Stable and Beta channels. Windows Authenticode and macOS notarization when a certificate is available. Until then, builds are labeled unsigned and ship checksums and attestations. | Proposed (restored by D-87) | [Distribution → Signing](distribution.md#signing) |
| D-52 | **Generated passcodes use the EFF long wordlist** (CC BY 3.0 US), allowed with attribution in `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES`. | Proposed | [Sharing and visibility](../design/views/sharing.md) |
| D-53 | **Theme files use the W3C Design Tokens (DTCG) format** (`$value`, `$type`), so they can be exchanged with design tools. | Proposed | [Standards](../architecture/standards.md), [Theme manager](../design/views/theme-manager.html) |
| D-54 | **The web app is hosted on the product site at `/app/`**, not in each user's notes site, so one build serves everyone and users' sites stay read-only. | Superseded by D-80 | [Distribution → Product site](distribution.md#the-product-site) |
| D-55 | **No telemetry.** Diagnostics stay on the computer. *Copy diagnostic report* and *Report a problem* (a pre-filled GitHub issue the user reviews) are the only ways information leaves. | Proposed | [Performance and quality](../architecture/performance-and-quality.md), [Security and privacy](../architecture/security-and-privacy.md) |
| D-58 | **Design system implementation:** React (MIT) with React Aria Components (Apache-2.0) for accessible behavior, vanilla-extract (MIT) for build-time, token-typed styles, Style Dictionary (Apache-2.0) for tokens, Ajv for view validation, and Storybook for the gallery. The rules don't depend on these choices. | Proposed | [Design system → Implementation stack](../design/system/overview.html) |
| D-59 | **Compound components never render elements or declare styles.** Only primitives and layouts do. | Proposed | [Design system → The layers](../design/system/overview.html) |
| D-60 | **No logic in views:** a restricted JSON Logic subset for conditions, and everything else in typed view-model selectors and commands. | Proposed | [Views as data](../design/system/views-as-data.md) |
| D-61 | **Views ship inside the app.** No remote, user-supplied, or agent-supplied views in this version. The format is designed so that could be allowed safely later. | Proposed | [Views as data → Versions](../design/system/views-as-data.md#versions-and-migration) |
| D-62 | **Stages are usable vertical slices.** M0 runs, M1 writes, M2 is a local daily driver with the real window, M3 syncs through GitHub, M4 adds Spotlight and the theme manager, and M5 publishes, shares, and is 1.0. Every stage ends in an installable preview build and a demo script that becomes a journey test. | Proposed | [Roadmap](roadmap.md), [Implementation plan](implementation-plan.md) |
| D-63 | **A five-level definition of done** (ready, work package, stage, release, project) with measurable bars and named evidence. The full quality bar applies from the first stage, scoped to what exists. | Proposed | [Definition of done](definition-of-done.md) |
| D-64 | **Implementation runs as an agentic loop over a machine-readable work graph**: orchestrator, builder, verifier, and four review roles, with at most three fix rounds before escalating to the owner, who accepts every stage gate. | Proposed | [The agentic loop](agentic-loop.html) |
| D-65 | **Measure locally, never through telemetry.** Every wait a person feels has a named performance measure, and the main process records named spans for Git and content work. They stay in memory and appear only in local diagnostics. `pnpm bench` measures the release build on a throwaway Git copy of a fixture workspace, against a committed budgets file with noise allowances, `--compare`, and `--check`. Published pages are measured with lab runs in CI. Real-user monitoring of the app or of readers' browsers was considered and **rejected** under D-55. | Proposed | [Performance and quality → Measuring](../architecture/performance-and-quality.md#measuring), [Requirements](../product/requirements.md) (QLT-05, PRF-12) |
| D-66 | **One cached Git status per repository, off the path of every view.** One porcelain v2 `git status` per repository, both in parallel with `GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0`, cached under the content generation, the times and sizes of Git's own files, and a 30 s maximum age. Commits always ask fresh, especially when the `private/` pointer might move. Views render with the cached state or "Checking changes…". | Proposed | [Desktop and Git → Asking Git what changed](../architecture/desktop-and-git.md#asking-git-what-changed) (PRF-07) |
| D-67 | **Less work per change:** view models declare the content they show so a change redraws only what shows it, heavy parts of views load when first shown, parsed notes are kept in an immutable snapshot keyed by path, size, and modification time, and a warm-up after start loads the first views and the search index without delaying typing. | Proposed | [Views as data → Rendering](../design/system/views-as-data.md#rendering), [Performance and quality → Doing less work](../architecture/performance-and-quality.md#doing-less-work) (PRF-10, PRF-11) |
| D-68 | **Structural changes show before they're saved.** Moving, deleting, creating, and duplicating notes and folders change the screen first, then write and commit. Only real commits are confirmed, and refusals put everything back with a reason. Edits in place keep the normal save. | Proposed | [Desktop and Git → Changes shown before they're saved](../architecture/desktop-and-git.md#changes-shown-before-theyre-saved), [Files](../design/views/files.html) (PRF-09) |
| D-69 | **One task tracker explains every wait a person starts.** Nothing under 300 ms, then a sign at the affected place and words that say what's happening, held at least 500 ms, announced after 1 s, with elapsed time after 3 s. The timings are `wait.*` tokens. Background saving stays silent. | Proposed | [Feedback and dialogs → Waits](../design/components/feedback-and-dialogs.md#waits), [Tokens](../design/system/tokens.md) (PRF-08) |
| D-70 | **Markdown bridge (M0 spike result for D-01):** a remark-based bridge (unified, remark-parse, remark-gfm, remark-frontmatter, mdast-util-to-markdown, all MIT) that keeps each top-level block's source and writes unchanged blocks back verbatim. `@tiptap/markdown` re-serializes the whole document, so it can't keep untouched text byte-identical (EDT-10). Evidence: the 20-file corpus round-trips byte for byte, both directly and after ProseMirror loads and normalizes it, and unsupported constructs stay verbatim as raw blocks (EXT-05). | Proposed | [Editor engine](../architecture/editor-engine.md) |
| D-71 | **Implementation choices made while building M0:** the boundary check (`tools/check-boundaries.mjs`) replaces ESLint and dependency-cruiser for the design-system rules; the gallery is the app's own runtime rendering contract fixtures, not Storybook; view expressions use a small evaluator of the allowed JSON Logic subset, not json-logic-js; TypeScript is limited to erasable syntax so Node tools run the sources directly; Electron is pinned per release (42.4.1 for M0). | Proposed | [Design system](../design/system/overview.html#implementation-stack) |
| D-72 | ~~**M0 window:** until the app-built window management lands in M2 (WP-2.1), Windows and Linux use a hidden title bar with the system's window controls overlay. The transparent, frameless 20 px window from D-14 is behind `--custom-window`, and its spike confirmed it opens and draws.~~ Since WP-2.1 the transparent window is the window on Windows and Linux (D-104). | Superseded by D-104 | [Desktop app](../experience/desktop-app.html) |
| D-73 | **Minimal scrollbars (owner, 2026-10-14):** custom scrollbars everywhere, with no arrow buttons, hidden when idle, shown only while scrolling or on hover, and with their space always reserved so nothing moves when they appear (UI-09). Built with `::-webkit-scrollbar` styles only, because Chromium ignores those when the standard `scrollbar-width` or `scrollbar-color` is set. | Decided (owner) | [Foundations](../design/foundations.md#scrollbars) |
| D-74 | **Keymap implementation (WP-1.8):** every key binding lives in the command registry with its layer and cheat-sheet group. Editor commands carry `handledBy: "editor"`: the editor's keymap is built from the registry (passed to the editor surface as view data), and menus or Spotlight run them through an editor-action event. App keys are resolved first (a capture-phase handler), then the editor's. Key matching tries the typed character first and the physical key second, except Ctrl+Alt on Windows and Linux (AltGr). `quiet` chords run without being listed (Mod+Plus for larger text). Commands named before their milestone (`planned`, such as Spotlight) keep their keys reserved but stay out of the cheat sheet. The cheat sheet's layer and platform filters are segmented controls (radio groups), not chips. | Proposed | [Keyboard shortcuts](../experience/keyboard-shortcuts.md) |
| D-75 | **Appearance before the theme manager (WP-1.9):** until the theme manager arrives (M4, WP-4.5), the light theme, the dark theme, and the content font are chosen from ⋯ submenus (Theme → Light theme ▸ / Dark theme ▸, View → Font ▸), each a command so Spotlight and agents can run it. The custom font stack (THM-03) and the UI scale setting get their controls with the theme manager's Reading tab and Settings; the UI scale already works as a token mode (`--fn-ui-scale`) with commands. Built-in palettes for Sepia, Dusk, Harbor, Forest, and High contrast are new reference tokens; the Paper and Ink placeholder grays were darkened to pass 4.5:1. | Proposed | [Theming](../experience/theming.md) |
| D-76 | **Find and replace build (WP-1.10):** the find bar is composed in the canvas view from existing components, not a FindBar compound, and its keys (Enter, Shift+Enter, Esc, ↑/↓ history, the option toggles, replace all) are registered commands with *key scopes* (`findBar`, `findField`, `replaceField`) set on a Surface and SearchFields. Matching folds accents and case with an accent-aware pattern over prosemirror-search; plain-text replacements are always literal. "Replaced N matches" is a toast. Searching waits 60 ms after typing; find actions always apply the latest query first; replace keys work only while the replace row is open and there are matches. The scroll-edge tick marks are deferred to P1, and read-only raw blocks are skipped without a count. | Proposed | [Find and replace](../experience/find-and-replace.md) |
| D-77 | **Waits and feedback build (WP-1.4):** the task tracker (`packages/core/src/tasks.ts`) reads the `wait.*` timings from the token build's resolved `waits` export (they're delays, so no mode changes them; `wait.toastDuration` 6 s and `wait.toastUndoDuration` 10 s were added). Save (<kbd>Mod</kbd>+<kbd>S</kbd>) is the first tracked wait; a failed save shows an error toast with [Retry]. Screen-reader announcements go through one LiveRegion. New components: Spinner, Toast, LiveRegion, ResultList (listbox with aria-activedescendant, so focus stays in its owner), Tabs, and the Field layout. Toasts are a primitive rather than a compound because they need a live-region container and hover and focus handling. Known gap (S3, owner WP-1.11): with find open, every keystroke rebuilds all match highlights (about 0.45 s for a one-letter query in a 50,000-word note); highlights will be capped or limited to the visible range. | Proposed | [Feedback and dialogs](../design/components/feedback-and-dialogs.md#waits) |
| D-78 | **The canvas follows the owner's earlier editor (owner, 2026-10-13; reference: prabinpebam/markdown-focus-editor):** note text is **Roboto Mono** (OFL, bundled) by default, at 16 px with 1.5 line height and 1.5em between blocks; headings are bold at 2.5, 2.0, 1.6, 1.3, 1.1, and 1.0 times the text size, with 1.5, 1.2, 1.0, 0.8, 0.6, and 0.5em above them. The trigger sits 40 px from the top edge; on hover its dot grows to fill the circle (200 ms), and the circle morphs into the toolbar (300 ms, buttons fading in 150 ms later) and back. The **reading zone** keeps text between 25% and 65% of the window height: 25% padding above the note, 35% below its end, and the edited line scrolled back into the band (UI-10, UI-11). Supersedes the 18 px Inter default and the 1.75/1.4/1.15 heading ramp in Foundations. | Decided (owner) | [Foundations](../design/foundations.md), [Main UI](../experience/main-ui.html) |
| D-79 | **Serif preset (owner, 2026-10-13):** the other preset is **Fraunces for headings and Newsreader for body text** (both OFL, bundled), replacing Source Serif 4. Content font presets now set a body font and a heading font together (`font.content`, `font.contentHeading`); every preset but Serif uses one font for both. Sans, Hyperlegible, OpenDyslexic, and System stay as further options, for reading needs (THM-03). | Decided (owner) | [Theming](../experience/theming.md#fonts) |
| D-80 | **Web first, on each user's own GitHub Pages (owner, 2026-10-13).** The primary way to run Focus Notes is the web app: a user makes their own copy of the Focus Notes repository (*Use this template* or fork), turns on GitHub Pages with GitHub Actions, and opens their site. Nothing to download and nothing hosted by the project. The desktop app is for people who want a dedicated desktop experience. Supersedes D-43 (download first) and D-54 (one web app at the product site's `/app/`). The browser work moves from M6 to a new stage, **MW**, right after M1. | Decided (owner), amended by D-90 | [Web app](../architecture/web-app.md), [Roadmap](roadmap.md) |
| D-81 | **Web sign-in is a fine-grained token, created once from a pre-filled GitHub link.** GitHub's OAuth and device-code endpoints don't allow calls from a web page (no CORS), so a static site can only use them through a server or a registered app, both ruled out by D-21. The GitHub REST API does allow browser calls with a token. The first run opens `github.com/settings/personal-access-tokens/new` pre-filled (name, description, *Contents: read and write*, expiry), the user chooses *Only select repositories* and their notes repository, and pastes the token. It's checked, then kept in IndexedDB encrypted with a non-extractable WebCrypto key; it's sent only to `api.github.com`; the page has a strict CSP and no third-party code. Signing out deletes it. github.dev and Codespaces can't be driven from another site, so they're offered only as *Open in github.dev* links. | Superseded by D-90 | [Web app → What the browser never does](../architecture/web-app.md#what-the-browser-never-does) |
| D-82 | **The browser is local first and syncs through the Git Data API.** IndexedDB holds notes (path, text, the blob SHA last synced), settings, an outbox of changes, and the encrypted token. Pushes batch the outbox into one commit (blobs, tree, commit, then a fast-forward ref update); a rejected update pulls and retries. Pulls compare the branch's tree with the last synced one and fetch only changed blobs. A note changed in both places keeps the local text and saves the remote one beside it as `Name (conflict YYYY-MM-DD).md`. *(Corrected by D-84: there is no separate notes repository; public notes sync to the instance repository and private notes to its `private/` repository.)* | Superseded by D-90 | [Web app → Storage](../architecture/web-app.md#storage) |
| D-83 | **Web app build (WP-6.1, WP-6.2):** `apps/web` is the same app on a browser bridge (IndexedDB stores `notes` and `meta`; the outbox is the notes marked unsynced plus deletion markers), the sync engine is `packages/github` (tested against an in-memory GitHub), and `.github/workflows/pages.yml` deploys the app at the site's root with the docs at `/documentations/`. Open… in the browser is an in-app dialog (a SearchField driving a ResultList). The welcome note stays local until edited. A push that changes nothing makes no commit. Known gaps, each with an owner: in the browser the keymap uses <kbd>Ctrl</kbd> even on macOS (the `web` platform; S3, WP-6.2 follow-up); browser journeys run in Chromium only so far (Firefox and WebKit join in CI with WP-6.2's exit); Trusted Types wait for sanitized paste (WP-1.3). | Proposed, amended by D-90 | [Web app](../architecture/web-app.md) |
| D-84 | **One repository is the whole instance (seed spec, owner 2026-10-13).** The seed spec says the whole thing is managed as a GitHub repository, the app itself is hosted on GitHub Pages, and private content lives in a private repository that is a submodule of the parent, as in the owner's personal-site. So a user's copy of the Focus Notes repository (*Use this template* or fork) holds the app's source, its *Deploy app* workflow, the docs, **public notes in `notes/`**, and the **private repository at `private/`** (private notes in its `notes/`, settings in `.focus-notes/`). Its own Pages site serves the app at `/` and public notes as pages. The project's repository is an instance too, and is the template. Supersedes the split in D-38 (a source-only product repository plus a notes repository made from a template), D-48 (a pinned reusable publish workflow: the workflow now lives in the repository and updates arrive by syncing with upstream), and D-82's separate notes repository. The web app reads its repository from its own address (`<owner>.github.io/<repository>`); the desktop app clones the same repository. | Decided (owner), amended by D-87 | [Web app](../architecture/web-app.md), [Repository and publishing](../architecture/repository-and-publishing.html) |
| D-85 | **Resequenced for the seed's priorities (owner, 2026-10-13).** The hosted web app and the seed's main workflows come first: M1 finishes Notion- and Loop-style editing (the contextual toolbar, the slash menu, tables), **MW** delivers the instance on GitHub Pages with public and private notes and files and folders in the browser (WP-2.3, WP-2.4, WP-2.5, WP-3.4, WP-6.1, WP-6.2), then M4 (Spotlight, theme manager), M5 (public links, sharing, release), and only then the desktop app's own work: M2 (the desktop window) and M3 (desktop sign-in and Git). Stage IDs are kept so references stay valid; their order is in the roadmap. | Decided (owner), amended by D-90 | [Roadmap](roadmap.md) |
| D-86 | **Nothing is hosted for others (owner, 2026-10-13).** The owner's GitHub Pages site is the owner's own app, not a service, and others aren't meant to use it. Everyone else copies the repository (*Use this template* or fork) and runs their own instance on their own Pages; anyone who wants the desktop app builds it from their copy (their own Actions, or `pnpm dist` locally). So there's no product site, no Get started page for visitors, no downloads or store listings for others, and no reusable workflow or reader bundle fetched from the project: every copy builds everything from its own source. The README is the only getting-started guide. Supersedes D-51 and the "project's own site for docs and downloads" clause of D-21, and rewrites DST-01, DST-03, DST-04, DST-06, DST-07, SEC-05, PKG-06, and DSK-07. | Superseded by D-87 | [Distribution](distribution.md), [Web app](../architecture/web-app.md) |
| D-87 | **Two audiences, one model (owner, 2026-10-13; corrects D-84 and replaces D-86).** **People who want to improve Focus Notes** fork the project repository and build their own app. **People who just want to use it** copy the small **template repository** (*Use this template*, or clone it), not the source repository, and never build the app: the template is their instance (their public notes in `notes/`, the private repository at `private/`, and a *Deploy app* workflow that deploys the released web app bundle at a pinned version, verified by checksum and attestation, to their own Pages), and they can download the desktop app from the project's **GitHub Releases**. The **project repository is the owner's own instance**: its Pages site is the owner's app, built from source, with the owner's public notes. On any instance, someone without the owner's token is a **visitor**: they see only public notes, read-only, plus **Use this app for yourself**, a dialog that explains how Focus Notes works and gives the steps to get their own instance or download the desktop app. Nothing runs on a server; the only hosting is GitHub Pages and GitHub Releases. Restores D-38's template (amended: the instance's Pages serves the app itself), D-48, and D-51. | Decided (owner) | [Web app](../architecture/web-app.md), [Repository and publishing](../architecture/repository-and-publishing.html) |
| D-88 | **Visitors and owners on an instance's site.** On an instance's GitHub Pages site, a browser that hasn't connected is always a visitor: it shows the public notes read-only, with **I own this site: Connect GitHub** and **Use this app for yourself**. Writing starts once a token that can write to this repository is connected in this browser, which makes it the owner's; local first and offline then apply. Outside a Pages site (the desktop app, local development) there are no visitors: you can write at once and connect later. The released **web app bundle** is one artifact: the app plus the public-page reader, the sealer, and the leak check (D-38's reusable workflow and D-48's reader bundle now mean this bundle). The template repository is `focus-notes-workspace`. | Superseded by D-90 | [Web app → What the browser never does](../architecture/web-app.md#what-the-browser-never-does) |
| D-89 | **Account pill (owner, 2026-10-13).** When a GitHub login is detected, the name shows at the bottom left in a small pill, muted and hardly noticeable (caption size, muted text, no fill until pointed at). Pressing it opens a small menu: the sync status, *Sync now*, and *Sign out of GitHub*. When nobody is signed in, the same spot shows a quiet *Sign in* link that opens Connect GitHub. Hidden where there's no GitHub connection (UI-12). | Decided (owner), amended by D-90 | [Main UI](../experience/main-ui.html), [Web app](../architecture/web-app.md) |
| D-90 | **The browser keeps its own notes; the desktop app is the only GitHub editor (owner, 2026-10-14, adapting an independent study of local-first editors).** There's no simple, consumer-grade way for a static web app to save to GitHub: a token pasted into a page is hard to make, can't be limited to the right repositories from a link, expires, and sits on an origin (`<user>.github.io`) shared by every Pages site the user has. So: **(1) The web app** on each instance's Pages site keeps **your notes in this browser** in IndexedDB (no account, offline, never sent anywhere), shows the instance's **public notes** read-only, and, after you **unlock** with your passphrase, its **private notes** read-only, decrypted in the browser. It never signs in to GitHub and never writes to a repository; *Copy to my notes* makes an editable copy here. **(2) The desktop app** is the only client that writes to GitHub: it signs in through Git's own helpers (D-21), works on a clone with `private/`, and commits and pushes both repositories. **(3) The vault is a share grant with an audience of one.** The owner's private notes are sealed by the instance's own *Deploy app* workflow with the share-link scheme (D-41): a built-in grant for the owner whose scope is every private note and whose passcode is the vault passphrase. `private/access.json` stores only the passphrase's derived key, never the passphrase. Ciphertext exists only in the deployed site, never in Git history, with fresh keys every build; the keyring opens a sealed manifest (paths, titles, note keys), so file names stay readable in the private repository and opaque on the site. **(4) Moving notes:** *Export my notes* saves the browser's notes as one bundle; the desktop app imports it into the instance (private by default), and nothing is deleted on either side. **(5) Disclosures:** a forgotten passphrase can't be recovered (the notes stay in the private repository); *Remember on this browser* is exposed to every site on the same `github.io` origin, so the app warns and recommends a custom domain. Kept from the seed spec, unlike the study: public notes and public links, and the private repository as a submodule of the instance (two repositories, not a separate encrypted mirror). Supersedes D-81, D-82, and D-88; amends D-80 (the web app is how you reach your instance, not how it's written to GitHub), D-83 (the browser sync engine is removed; `packages/github` keeps the REST client for the desktop setup), D-85 (desktop sign-in and Git, M3, now comes right after MW, then the desktop window, M2), and D-89 (the browser's pill unlocks instead of signing in). | Decided (owner) | [Web app](../architecture/web-app.md), [Repository and publishing → The vault](../architecture/repository-and-publishing.html#the-vault), [Security and privacy](../architecture/security-and-privacy.md), [Roadmap](roadmap.md) |
| D-91 | **The formatting toolbar, link editor, and hover card as built (WP-1.2).** They're composed in the canvas view document from catalog parts, like the find bar (D-76), rather than as FormattingToolbar, LinkEditor, and HoverCard compounds: a new **Positioner** layout anchors them to the selection or a link, and the editor surface reports the selection through a `format` event to `vm.format`. Where the build differs from [Text formatting](../design/components/text-formatting.html): **(1)** controls are 32 px, the same `IconButton` as the trigger's toolbar, not 40 px, and a pressed toggle shows a fill rather than a filled icon; a mixed one shows a lighter fill rather than a half-fill. **(2)** No caret on the toolbar's edge when it slides sideways, no exit fade, and a selection that starts above the window flips the toolbar below its last line instead of anchoring to the first visible line. **(3)** <kbd>Alt+F10</kbd> returns to the control used last (React Aria's toolbar behavior). **(4)** The link editor isn't pre-filled from the clipboard (reading it would need a clipboard-read permission the shells deny), and it doesn't search note titles yet: that waits for Spotlight's index (M4), so the invalid-address message drops "or pick a note". Only http, https, and mailto links open, through the shell (a new `link:open` channel on the desktop, a new tab with `noopener` on the web); links to other notes don't open in the app until the workspace model (MW). **(5)** One undo after pasting an address over text leaves the address, which links to itself as a typed address does. **(6)** Copying a link's address goes through the shell (a new `clipboard:write` channel on the desktop; the renderer still can't read the clipboard). **(7)** The <kbd>Mod</kbd>+<kbd>U</kbd> underline toast (UNSUP-FMT-01) isn't built; no work package owns it yet. **(8)** Edited italic text is saved as `*text*` as [Content model](../architecture/content-model.md) says (it was `_text_`). | Proposed | [Text formatting](../design/components/text-formatting.html), [Components](../design/system/components.md), [Keyboard shortcuts](../experience/keyboard-shortcuts.md), [Desktop and Git](../architecture/desktop-and-git.md), [Security and privacy](../architecture/security-and-privacy.md) |
| D-92 | **The local workspace model (WP-2.3, proposed).** (1) **Layout before setup:** the desktop's notes folder is the workspace. With `.focus-notes/workspace.json` it has the repository's roots (*Private* `private/notes/`, *Public* `notes/`, D-84); without it, it's one root of its own, so everything written through M2 stays where it is until workspace setup (M3). The browser's root is its own notes, with the instance's `public:` and `private:` notes as read-only roots (D-90). (2) **Format:** a missing `workspace.json` is writable and isn't created by the app; a newer or unreadable one makes the workspace read-only with a message and a *Read only* pill, and the desktop's main process and the browser refuse such writes too; an older one is read-only until the person chooses *Update* (in the message or the pill), then migrates one version at a time. The browser keeps its notes' format in IndexedDB `meta` → `workspaceFormat`. (3) **File names:** letters, marks, and numbers in any language are kept with `.` and `_`; any other run of characters (spaces, punctuation, symbols, emoji) becomes one hyphen, so names read well in links and URLs; reserved Windows names get `-note`; at most 80 characters and 240 UTF-8 bytes (Linux allows 255), cut at whole characters. A name follows its title when it's the title's name, a numbered clash of it, or untitled, decided when the note opens: a name chosen by hand isn't replaced, and a note without an H1 keeps its name. Only notes in the workspace's roots are renamed. *Published* is a per-note flag that sync sets from M3 (pushed at least once); until then desktop notes aren't published, and the browser's own notes never are. (4) **Watching:** Node's built-in recursive `fs.watch` instead of chokidar (no dependency; recursive on Windows, macOS, and Linux in the Node that Electron ships), batched for 30 ms. An open note with unsaved edits keeps them and a message says it changed; choosing between versions is the conflict resolver (FIL-12). (5) **Redraw (PRF-11):** view models declare `reads` (state paths, or `"*"` for command states, kept while equal) and `shows` (workspace content); the renderer re-renders per region (each child of a view's top node), not per node, and a hidden region reads only its `when` until it first shows. Splitting view code per view and `VirtualList` paging arrive with the Files panel (WP-2.4). (6) **Watching on Linux (CI, 2026-10-14):** Node has no recursive watch on Linux and emulates one by polling each file's details, which missed a note saved by renaming a temporary file over it and then replaced by Git (`git reset --keep` when a sync conflict is settled), so the editor kept showing the version that was given up. On Linux each folder now has a watch of its own (inotify reports every name created, deleted, or written in a folder at once), hidden folders other than `.focus-notes` and `node_modules` aren't watched, and folders that come and go get and lose theirs (`apps/desktop/src/main/watch-tree.ts`); Windows and macOS keep the system's recursive watch. (7) **Typing not yet handed over is unsaved too:** a change from outside that arrived before the editor handed its latest typing over found the note saved and replaced it, losing what was typed. The app now asks the editor for that text first (`FLUSH_EDITOR`), so the change goes to the resolver with both versions, as (D-92) promises. A file the watcher reports is a change from outside only when it differs from what the app knows is on disk (its last save of the note, or the text it opened), so the app's own writes, such as a new note's empty file, never are. A change the watcher reports more than once is offered once. | Proposed | [Content model](../architecture/content-model.md#the-workspace-model), [Desktop and Git](../architecture/desktop-and-git.md#external-changes), [Files panel](../design/views/files.html#names-and-titles), [Web app](../architecture/web-app.md), [Views as data](../design/system/views-as-data.md), [Security and privacy](../architecture/security-and-privacy.md#data-inventory) |
| D-93 | **The slash menu, paste, and Trusted Types as built (WP-1.3, proposed).** **Slash menu** (EDT-06): composed in the canvas view document like the formatting toolbar (D-91): a Positioner with a new `caret` anchor and `align` start, a Surface, and a ResultList with a new `width` menu (320 px). The editor reports the `/query` and the rows valid there through a `slash` event to `vm.slash`; the block registry's rows reach the app when it mounts (`slash.register`), so a block registered later appears by registration alone. Where it differs from [Text formatting → Slash menu](../design/components/text-formatting.html#slash-menu)'s first design: **(1)** no group headings (rows are ordered Text, Lists, Insert), and matching letters aren't bold, because ResultList has neither yet. **(2)** Icons are ResultList's 20 px, not 16 px. **(3)** *Insert* lists Code block before Divider (registry order); Table joins with WP-1.5. **(4)** It opens at the start of any block, not only an empty one, and after a line break as well as a space; a space right after `/` closes it, a space when nothing matches hides it until a match comes back, and a query over 40 characters ends it. **(5)** Validity is checked in the `/`'s own block, so a heading isn't offered on a list item's first line. **(6)** ResultList is now also a tab stop that takes the arrow keys, Home, End, and Enter itself, so a scrolling list is reachable from the keyboard on its own (axe: scrollable-region-focusable); that applies to every ResultList. **Paste** (EDT-09): **(7)** HTML is sanitized (DOMPurify, Apache-2.0 or MPL-2.0, taken under Apache-2.0) before ProseMirror parses it, then read in an inert document and converted to Markdown (mdast) and through the Markdown bridge into blocks, so a paste can only bring in what a note can hold. Word's list paragraphs become nested lists and Google Docs' styled spans become marks. HTML with no structure or formatting at all (Markdown copied from a code editor) is read as its text, as Markdown. A copy from ProseMirror keeps its exact structure. Dropped text and HTML take the same path. **(8)** What the editor can't represent yet degrades to plain text, one paragraph per line: a table becomes its GFM text until WP-1.5 (TBL-10). Images keep their alt text and a toast says they were left out; an image file alone only shows the toast. **(9)** Tiptap's paste rules are off, so <kbd>Mod</kbd>+<kbd>Shift</kbd>+<kbd>V</kbd> keeps `**text**` as typed. On macOS the desktop's main process maps <kbd>⇧⌘V</kbd> to Paste and Match Style, which Chromium binds only to <kbd>⌥⇧⌘V</kbd> (not verified on a Mac yet). **Sanitizing** (SEC-02): **(10)** links keep only http, https, mailto, and relative addresses (Tiptap's default also allowed ftp, tel, and others): unsafe links in a paste become text, and in a note they render without an address while the file keeps them. **(11)** Both shells' CSP adds `require-trusted-types-for 'script'; trusted-types default dompurify`. The app writes no HTML, but libraries do: React Aria clears its live region with `innerHTML`, the Markdown parser decodes entities such as `&copy;` through `innerHTML` in browsers, and ProseMirror reads clipboard HTML. So `mountApp` installs a `default` policy that passes text without `<` as it is and sanitizes anything else with DOMPurify; there's no script or script-URL policy. **(12)** DOMPurify returns unsanitized output under happy-dom, so the sanitizer's and paste's unit tests run under jsdom (MIT, development only). The journeys hand the clipboard's data to a real paste event because the system clipboard isn't reachable where they run (as in WP-1.2); <kbd>Mod</kbd>+<kbd>Shift</kbd>+<kbd>V</kbd> is held while it's dispatched. | Proposed | [Text formatting](../design/components/text-formatting.html#slash-menu), [Editing](../experience/editing.md#paste-and-drop), [Editor engine](../architecture/editor-engine.md#block-registry-contract), [Components](../design/system/components.md), [Keyboard shortcuts](../experience/keyboard-shortcuts.md), [Security and privacy](../architecture/security-and-privacy.md#threats-and-controls), [Web app](../architecture/web-app.md) |
| D-94 | **The Files panel as built (WP-2.4, proposed).** (1) **Components:** `Sheet` is a layout, not a compound, because it needs its own positioned, frosted element and its closing behavior (<kbd>Esc</kbd>, a click on the note). `FileTree` is one primitive that draws its rows (no separate FileTreeItem yet) on the WAI-ARIA tree pattern, a flat list of rows with `aria-level` and `aria-multiselectable`, rather than React Aria's Tree, which renders a treegrid and doesn't host an inline name field. Its code loads when the panel first opens (PRF-11). Move to… is composed in the canvas view from Dialog, SearchField, and ResultList, like D-76 and D-91. (2) **Opening:** *Open…* (<kbd>Mod</kbd>+<kbd>O</kbd>) is the Files panel wherever there's a workspace, in both apps; the browser's in-app list of notes becomes *Quick open* (<kbd>Mod</kbd>+<kbd>P</kbd>) until Spotlight (M4). The system's open dialog stays only for a shell without a workspace. (3) **Batches:** each operation, however many items it covers, is one `workspace.apply` batch (create folder, move, delete): all or nothing (the desktop's main process undoes earlier steps; the browser writes one IndexedDB transaction). It shows at once, the toast comes only after the write, and a refusal puts the tree and selection back and gives the reason (PRF-09). From M3 each batch is one commit. (4) **Deleting before Git:** the desktop sends deleted items to the system trash (`shell.trashItem`) and says so; the browser keeps them, marked `deleted`, for Recently deleted. There's no Undo until commits (M3); when to ask first is as designed. (5) **Visibility:** a move between the Public and Private roots is refused with a reason until WP-3.4 makes it a confirmed move between repositories; Move to… shows those folders as unavailable. (6) **Titles:** the desktop's scan and watcher read each note's first H1 from its first 16 KB, and the browser from its stored text, so rows show titles. Renaming a note in the panel edits (or adds) its H1, and the file name follows unless the note is published, even a name chosen by hand, because Rename is on purpose. (7) **Keys:** key scopes `files` (the sheet), `filesTree`, `filesEdit` (a name field, which hides the scopes around it through `data-key-scope-stop`, so <kbd>Delete</kbd> and <kbd>F2</kbd> edit text there), and `moveField`; New folder is <kbd>Ctrl+Alt+Shift+N</kbd> in browsers. (8) **Later:** sort and Duplicate (FIL-11), each row's time and ⋯ menu and the context menu, cut and paste, the drag ghost's count, Recently deleted and Undo (FIL-10), link updates (FIL-09), remembering open folders, the full-width compact sheet, VirtualList paging, and writing a new note only once it has text. | Proposed | [Files panel](../design/views/files.html#whats-built), [Components](../design/system/components.md#compound-components), [Desktop and Git](../architecture/desktop-and-git.md#changes-shown-before-theyre-saved), [Files and sharing](../experience/files-and-sharing.md#the-files-panel), [Keyboard shortcuts](../experience/keyboard-shortcuts.md#files-panel), [Web app](../architecture/web-app.md), [Security and privacy](../architecture/security-and-privacy.md#data-inventory), [Information architecture](../design/information-architecture.html) |
| D-96 | **Loop-style tables as built (WP-1.5, proposed).** **(1) Model:** a table is GFM's shape and nothing else: a header row of header cells, body rows, every row as wide as the header, each cell **one paragraph** of inline content, alignment per column. The schema allows only that (Turn into, lists, and splitting are refused in a cell), and a normalizer turns any line break that reaches a cell into a space and rebuilds a table that lost its shape, so a table can always be written as a pipe table (TBL-06). A table always has a body row: deleting the last one empties it, and a header-only table gets an empty one when it opens. Deleting the last column deletes the table. Untouched tables keep their source byte for byte; edited ones are written padded and aligned, with a backslash before every pipe in a cell, code included. A table with something the editor can't hold in a cell (inline HTML, an image) stays verbatim and read-only (EXT-05). **(2) Components:** `TableControls` is one **host** component, placed in the canvas view and fed by `vm.table`, that draws the handles, the + insertion point, the New row and New column bars, the column tint, the range outline, and the drop line from geometry the editor surface publishes (like the anchors behind Positioner), rather than a compound over Positioner, IconButton, and Menu: the controls follow the table's rows and columns, sit above the focus mask, and drag. Its menus reuse MenuButton's menu (`CommandMenu`). Tables render through the editor surface's own styles rather than a separate TableBlock, like the other blocks so far. Tokens: semantic `table.*` sizes and `cmp.tableControls.*`. **(3) Interactions, as built:** hover a cell for its row and column handles; the + appears after 150 ms within 8 px of an inner boundary on the left or top edge (the handles' 40 px gutter); New row and New column show while the pointer is over the table or within 24 px of it. Clicking a handle selects its row, column, or table and opens its menu; dragging moves it (the header row doesn't move, nothing drops above it; <kbd>Esc</kbd> cancels). A moved row or column stays selected. The column menu's **Align** is a submenu with Left, Center, and Right (TBL-08); sorting stays with WP-8.1 (TBL-04). <kbd>Enter</kbd> in the last row leaves the table onto a new line below it. <kbd>Esc</kbd> toggles cell selection mode (a second <kbd>Esc</kbd> or <kbd>Enter</kbd> goes back to typing); arrows move the selected cell there. Handles show for the caret's cell on touch screens; with a mouse they show on hover, and the keyboard path is the **cell menu** (<kbd>Shift</kbd>+<kbd>F10</kbd>, the Menu key, or right-click), which holds every row and column action plus Copy, Cut, Clear cells, Select row, column, or table, and Delete. **(4) Clipboard:** copying a range writes tab-separated text, HTML, and `text/markdown` (a GFM table whose first copied row is the header). A range pasted into a table fills from the active cell (or the range's top-left) and grows the table in one undo step, then stays selected, with a toast ("Pasted 5 × 4. Table grew by 2 rows."); one pasted on a line becomes a table. A pasted range is a spreadsheet's HTML table, or text whose lines all have the same number of tabs, not all leading (so indented code stays text). The cell menu has no **Paste**: reading the clipboard from a menu needs a permission the app doesn't ask for, so <kbd>Mod</kbd>+<kbd>V</kbd> is the way. **(5) Not yet:** a table wider than the reading column scrolls sideways inside its frame, without growing out to `layout.wideContent` or the edge fade; handles don't hide under the trigger band; destructive menu items aren't in the danger color (menu items have no tone yet); a dragged row or column is tinted rather than lifted to 60% opacity; on touch, long-press dragging and the insertion points on the focused cell's edges; announcements cover structural changes ("Row inserted below row 2") but not every selection change. **(6) Typing budget:** typing in a 200 × 10 table of short entries (tasks, owners, numbers) measures p95 about 21 ms keystroke to two frames on the reference machine, inside the journey's 33 ms; a table whose every cell wraps over two lines measured about 42 ms, all in Chromium's table paint and layerization (a whole table repaints when a cell changes). Fixed by D-100 (6): each cell paints on its own (about 18 ms p95 even with every cell wrapping). | [Tables](../design/components/tables.html), [Editing](../experience/editing.md#tables), [Keyboard shortcuts](../experience/keyboard-shortcuts.md#tables), [Content model](../architecture/content-model.md), [Components](../design/system/components.md) |
| D-95 | **Link updates as built (WP-2.5, proposed).** (1) **Planned in core, on the parser's positions:** `packages/core/src/links.ts` plans link updates as pure functions over a note's text. Core now depends on `@focus-notes/markdown`, whose `linkDestinations` reports where each inline link, image, and reference definition destination is written, from the parser's own tokens, so nothing in code, HTML, or front matter is touched. The rewrite is text-level: only the characters of a changed destination change, and the note is never written again from the editor's document. (2) **One batch:** `WorkspaceOp` gains `write`, a note's new text at its path after the batch's moves. Moves and folder renames in Files, renaming a note in Files, and a file name that follows its title as it's typed (FIL-07) send the move and the link writes as one `workspace.apply`. The desktop writes each note atomically and undoes it by writing the old text back; the browser applies the batch in its one IndexedDB transaction. Renaming a note in Files now sends its new title in the same batch, instead of a write followed by a rename. (3) **Which notes:** every note in the writable roots is read (the open note's text comes from the editor, after pending edits are written). A note that isn't moving is parsed only if its text names something that is, after undoing escapes and percent-encoding and ignoring case, because a link into a moved file or folder always spells its name. The instance's read-only notes are never read. (4) **Matching and spelling:** paths match ignoring case and Unicode form, as the stores and file systems do. Segments that didn't change keep their spelling; new ones are percent-encoded where a destination needs it (spaces and parentheses stay as they are inside `<…>`). Left alone: links that climb out of a root, site-root links (`/…`), absolute URLs, and `#…`. Not handled yet: character references (`&amp;`) inside a destination, HTML links, and wiki links. (5) **The open note** reloads with its new links; text typed while the batch was written is kept, with the same rewrite applied to it. (6) **Saying so:** the move's or rename's toast adds "Updated links in N notes." A renamed note that changed no other note is only announced, as before. (7) **Later:** Undo (M3), and a shell-side index so a rename in a large workspace doesn't read every note. | Proposed | [Files and sharing](../experience/files-and-sharing.md#links-that-follow), [Files panel](../design/views/files.html#whats-built), [Content model](../architecture/content-model.md), [Desktop and Git](../architecture/desktop-and-git.md#changes-shown-before-theyre-saved) |
| D-97 | **Save and sync as built (WP-3.3, proposed).** (1) **Where:** the Git adapter is in the desktop's main process (`apps/desktop/src/main/git/`), not a package, because only the desktop runs Git (D-90). It runs the `git` on `PATH`, at least 2.31. The workspace folder must be its repository's top level (never a parent repository it sits in), and `private/` is the private repository when it's its own top level; otherwise, or without Git, saving stays local with no Git UI. Git opens after the caret is ready (or after 3 s), and a note written before then is still committed. (2) **Running:** argument lists, `-C`, `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`, `GCM_INTERACTIVE=never`, `LC_ALL=C`, `GIT_LITERAL_PATHSPECS=1`, and `GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0` for reads, with `GIT_DIR` and its kin removed. Paths go through standard input (`--pathspec-from-file=-`) and the message through a file in the Git folder, so no note name, message, or credential is ever a process argument (SEC-03). (3) **Cadence (D-23):** the main process keeps the 60 s timer, restarted by every note write (writes follow typing within a second). <kbd>Mod</kbd>+<kbd>S</kbd> and switching notes ask through `sync.now`, after the editor hands over text it hasn't sent yet; quitting syncs once more after the window has saved, waiting at most 15 s per network call. `FOCUS_NOTES_IDLE_COMMIT_MS` and `FOCUS_NOTES_POINTER_GAP_MS` set the delays until Settings does. (4) **Commits:** a save commits only the paths the app wrote or renamed since their last commit (`git add --all` and `git commit --only` on those paths), leaving the person's own staged changes and other programs' files alone. Each Files batch is one commit per repository it touches, made before `workspace.apply` resolves, with pushing in the background; deletes still go to the system trash as well. A batch whose commit fails (a hook, signing, no name and email) isn't refused, because its files have changed: they stay, wait for the next commit, and the problem is shown. (5) **Messages:** one change says what it is (`Update`, `Create`, `Delete`, `Rename`, `Move … → …`, `Create folder …`); several are counted (`Update 3 notes`, `Move 2 items to archive`, `Delete 2 items`, `Change 2 items`) and listed below, with "Update links in N notes" for a batch's link updates; the pointer is `Update private notes to <commit>`. (6) **Pointer (D-07):** moves only to a pushed private commit (or when neither repository has a remote), at once the first time in a session, then at most every 5 minutes, in any notes commit already being made, and on quit whatever the gap. The app sets `push.recurseSubmodules=check` when it opens a workspace with `private/`. (7) **Push and pull:** `push.default=upstream`; no upstream means local only. A rejected push fetches (`--no-recurse-submodules`), rebases the app's commits without an automatic stash, and pushes once more. Fetches run when the window becomes active (at most every 30 s) and every 5 minutes, and pull only when tracked files are clean; saves wait while a rebase runs. A rebase that stops is aborted: the app's commits and files stay, the remote's version stays in its remote-tracking branch, and the problem shows until the resolver (WP-3.5). Offline, commits stay and a retry runs after 30 s, doubling to 5 minutes. (8) **Lock:** `focus-notes.lock` in the notes repository's common Git folder holds `{"pid": …}`, taken when Git opens and released on quit; a dead or unreadable owner is replaced in one rename. While another running process holds it, nothing is committed and the problem shows. (9) **Status cache (PRF-07):** as specified, with each repository's first status asked when Git opens so the key knows its refs; the benchmark records 2 status processes per refresh and a cached answer in about 0.5 ms (budget 5 ms). (10) **Shown:** only problems, as the trigger's problem dot ("Couldn't sync") and one toast when a problem first appears, with [Retry]; <kbd>Mod</kbd>+<kbd>S</kbd> says "Saved and synced" or why not. Problems: offline, auth, conflict, locked, identity, signing, hook, failed. (11) **Later:** the arcs and the toolbar's status row, the last-sync tooltip, [Details], [Sign in again] (WP-3.2), the conflict resolver, Recently deleted and Undo (WP-3.5), moves between Public and Private (WP-3.4), Settings for the delays, and logs kept per device. | Proposed | [Desktop and Git → Save and sync](../architecture/desktop-and-git.md#save-and-sync), [Repository and publishing](../architecture/repository-and-publishing.html), [Feedback → Save and sync status](../design/components/feedback-and-dialogs.md#save-and-sync-status), [Files panel](../design/views/files.html), [Files and sharing](../experience/files-and-sharing.md#links-that-follow), [Security and privacy](../architecture/security-and-privacy.md#data-inventory), [Performance and quality](../architecture/performance-and-quality.md) |
| D-100 | **The trigger toolbar, the ⋯ menu, and writing hardening as built (WP-1.6, WP-1.11, proposed).** **(1) The ⋯ menu** has five groups: *View* (text size, Font ▸, Full screen), *Theme* (System, Light, Dark, Light theme ▸, Dark theme ▸), *Edit* (Find, Find and replace), *Note* (Share and visibility…), and *App* (Settings, Keyboard shortcuts, Help and docs); the browser adds its *Notes* group before *App*. Items whose feature belongs to a later work package are there, unavailable, and say why under their label: *Share and visibility…* (sharing, WP-5.3; Public and Private moves, WP-3.4), *Settings* (the Settings view; <kbd>Mod</kbd>+<kbd>,</kbd> is reserved and not yet in the cheat sheet), and *Help and docs* (the product site, WP-5.6), each "…arrive in a later preview". An unavailable item stays focusable (APG: focusability of disabled controls), so the arrow keys reach it; it's `aria-disabled`, choosing it does nothing and keeps the menu open, and its accessible name is everything it shows, reason and shortcut included (WCAG 2.5.3). *Find in all notes*, the *Note* group's other items, and *About* aren't in the desktop menu yet. Menu items' focus ring is now 2 px (it was 1 px). **(2) The toolbar:** the trigger says it opens a dialog (`aria-haspopup="dialog"`, the toolbar's frosted panel), not a menu. Opening it focuses the first action the first time, then the action used last (React Aria's toolbar, as the formatting toolbar does, D-91 (3)); <kbd>Home</kbd> is always the first. **(3) Accessibility audit (A11Y-01):** every M1 and MW surface, opened from the keyboard, in Paper, Ink, and both High contrast themes, has no axe violations, 24 × 24 px targets or the spacing 2.5.8 allows, and a visible, uncovered focus; at 320 CSS px (400% zoom) nothing scrolls sideways; with reduced motion nothing animates; in forced colors every surface keeps contrast and focus. What it found and fixed: a tooltip opened by keyboard focus sat outside every landmark (it now renders in its control's outermost landmark); an empty table header named nothing for its column (it now shows "Column 3", muted, which isn't part of the note and is never saved); the delete confirmation focused itself (it now focuses *Cancel*, through a new Button `autoFocus`); a selected Files row and the link editor's held selection were dark text on Highlight in forced colors (new semantic `color.onSelection` and `color.onSelectionMuted`, HighlightText there); at 320 px the formatting toolbar, link editor, and slash menu overflowed (positioned panels are at most the window less its edges, toolbars and the link editor wrap, the slash menu narrows) and submenus opened off screen (beside the menu where there's room, else below their item). A link or code inside a find match was 3.9:1 on the highlight (a match's text is now the text color, HighlightText in forced colors). **(4) Races found on slow runners (CI run 38484016446) and fixed in the product:** a selection made by keys or a drag just before the editor dispatched from an effect, a timer, or Tiptap's one-frame-late focus was put back (the editor now reads the DOM selection before such dispatches and before clipboard events, and focuses directly); a hover card kept for a pointer on a panel that then closed stayed up, with a stale address; <kbd>Shift</kbd>+<kbd>F10</kbd> pressed as the caret entered a table was lost; a menu that ran an editor command could leave focus on its button. Pressing a button in the formatting toolbar, link editor, or hover card no longer takes focus from the note (it used to blur and refocus it, and ProseMirror then put back its older selection 20 ms later over a new drag); a click in the note is read before that restore too. The caret starts at the note's end, scrolled into view, at once. The trigger's panel clips rather than scrolls, so focus moving to an action while it still grows doesn't shift the buttons or close their tooltip. The journeys use each platform's caret keys (macOS: <kbd>⌘</kbd> with arrows) and wait for states rather than time; `FOCUS_NOTES_E2E_CPU_THROTTLE` slows the renderer to reproduce such races. **(5) PRF-03:** the app records `fn:note.open` (from the request to the first frame showing the note with the caret ready) and a `fn:note.saved` mark at each write; `pnpm bench` measures opening a 10,000-word note from Files (about 50 ms, budget 150) and a burst's write after its last keystroke (about 560 ms, budget 1,000: serializing waits 150 ms for a pause, saving 400 ms more), and the journeys check both, with typing timed while saves run. **(6) Tables:** each cell has paint containment, so typing repaints one cell: in a 200 × 10 table whose every cell wraps, keystroke to paint fell from about 430 ms p95 (42 ms with two-line cells, D-96 (6)) to about 18 ms, and the short-entry table from 21 to 13 ms. **(7) The M1 demo** is two journeys (pointer and keyboard only, `m1-demo.spec.ts`). Two steps stand in for what a test can't do: the OS appearance change is sent as the main process's `nativeTheme` event, and a spreadsheet range is a paste event, as in the other paste journeys. The screen-reader passes are a scripted manual checklist ([Performance and quality → Accessibility audit](../architecture/performance-and-quality.md#accessibility-audit)). **(8) After the merge with M3 and WP-2.1:** the *App* group's *Settings…* is D-103's: available on the desktop, and in the browser unavailable with its reason ("Settings manage your GitHub account, so they're in the desktop app"), so (1)'s "Settings arrive in a later preview" no longer applies. The audit covers the new surfaces too: the window controls, Snap Layouts, and the system menu (Windows and Linux), Settings, and the first run's Welcome, Git, and Sign in steps, in the four themes, in forced colors, and at 320 px. Settings now opens with focus on its selected category (Tabs' new `autoFocus`), as its view document's `focus` says; it used to focus the dialog itself, which shows no indicator in forced colors. The focus check counts a control as covered only by something that paints: the drawn window's title bar band is an invisible box over the note's top that takes the pointer (D-104) and hides nothing. At 320 px it's the window as drawn that is 320 CSS px wide: the transparent shadow margin zooms with the page, and at 400% it left a 288 × 168 window, where the selected word sat under the 110 px top band and the formatting toolbar, rightly, hid. **(9) After CI on Linux and Windows (2026-10-14):** React Aria counts a key as keyboard use only without Ctrl, ⌘, or Alt, so once the pointer had moved (a CI runner's pointer rests in the middle of the screen, over the window), <kbd>Ctrl</kbd>+<kbd>.</kbd> opened the toolbar with *New note* focused and no ring. A key that runs an app command now sets the keyboard as the way of interacting (`keyRanCommand`), so the focus it moves shows its ring. The first run's audit uses a fake Git Credential Manager, so its Sign in step is the same whatever the computer has installed. The keyboard-only M1 demo counts a click a key made (Enter or Space on a control: detail 0, no pointer type) as the keyboard's, which macOS's controls make where Windows' and Linux's don't; any other click, press, or context menu still fails it and names its target.
