(7) **An error belongs to what produced it:** a lookup's error ("Can't reach GitHub to look for your notes.") shows only on the lookup panels, never while setup runs or on *Your notes are ready*, and every error clears when the step changes or the action is retried; the catalogue checks that no state that went well shows an error. | Proposed | [First run → Principles](../experience/first-run.md#principles-of-the-desktop-first-run), D-98, D-124, D-131, D-135 |
| D-155 | **The notes folder is named after the notes' repository (the owner: "Folder name should be the same as the repository name, else it's confusing"; their `prabins-notes` went in `Documents\Focus Notes`).** (1) **Creating:** the folder is `Documents/<instance repository name>` as entered in setup (default `notes`), for example `Documents/prabins-notes`; when it has other files and isn't already this clone, the first free `<name> 2`, `<name> 3` beside it (D-154's free-folder rule). A folder the app chose is decided when *Download to this computer* runs, so changing the name in the form changes the folder. (2) **Opening existing notes** (*We found your notes* → Open, *Open from GitHub*): `Documents/<repository name>`, the same rule. (3) **Just use this computer for now** has no repository and keeps `Documents/Focus Notes`, the app's own neutral name. (4) **Names Windows can't use:** GitHub already limits names to letters, digits, `.`, `_` and `-`; trailing dots are dropped (`notes.` → `notes`, only dots → `notes`) and device names get `-notes` (`con` → `con-notes`, `con.x` → `con-notes.x`). (5) *More options* shows the real folder for the name typed ("Saved on this computer in …"), and Settings → Workspace shows the folder in use. (6) No existing workspace folder is renamed or moved. | Proposed | [First run → Your notes](../experience/first-run.md), [Desktop and Git → The workspace folder](../architecture/desktop-and-git.md#the-workspace-folder), D-98, D-124, D-154 |
| D-156 | **The note scrolls smoothly, and the focus band follows a resize live (the owner asked for both, proposed).** (1) **Auto-scroll** that keeps the line being edited inside the reading band animates over 200 ms (`motion.autoScroll`) with a symmetric ease-in-out (`easing.inOut`, `cubic-bezier(0.42, 0, 0.58, 1)`), every time, never a jump. A new target while one runs continues from the note's current position and speed toward the new target (a cubic Hermite curve, at rest there) and finishes no later than one frame after the running scroll would have, so scrolls never queue or stack and never push their end out by a fresh 200 ms; held keys follow the caret continuously (while a key repeats, each step finishes within three frames, so the caret stays on screen even in a 360 px window); reduced motion makes it instant; a drag-selection still waits for the release. Owner-approved refinements: (a) only a scroll the person's own key, input, or click caused animates; opening a note and other moves the person didn't make place the note at once; (b) a move of under 4 px (or a target past the end the browser clamps away) is written at once, so typing at the end of a long note starts no animation; (c) held keys follow as above. Round 4 (CI): only a caret move from typing, a key, or a click in the note animates, with the note focused, find closed, and a caret rather than a range; every other scroll (a programmatic selection, focus returning from the link editor or Spotlight, find, a range the contextual toolbar opens on) is ProseMirror's own instant scroll, in the same task, as before D-156, so no overlay tied to the selection ever opens on a moving note. The animator only writes scrollTop. Also: a press in the note stops a running scroll where it is (the text stays under the pointer), and focus leaving the note ends it at once on its target (an anchored surface such as the link editor is hidden while its anchor is under the top band, which dropped the focus it was given). Round 5 (DEF-21, CI): a surface anchored to the note (the slash menu, a link card, the contextual toolbar, the link editor) opening ends a running scroll at once on its target, and while one is open every scroll is instant, so none opens or sits on a moving note; and anchored surfaces follow their anchor the way floating-ui's `autoUpdate` does: placed again on any scroll or layout change, once per frame, against the anchor read live in that frame (the slash menu had been placed against a rectangle published a frame earlier, so for a frame the scrolling caret slid under it). Round 6 (DEF-25): a scroll the reading zone writes from a frame's callback reaches scroll listeners only in the next frame, so the reading zone says it scrolled the note (`anchors.moved`) and every open anchored surface is placed again in that same frame. Round 7 (the owner, 21 October: selection auto-scroll, and deselecting, were jumpy): a selected range whose moving end leaves the band (Shift with an arrow, or a drag released there) uses the same 200 ms animation as a caret, and so does a range collapsing (deselecting) while its toolbar is still open. With a range selected, the formatting toolbar may stay open while the note glides, because it follows its live anchor in each written frame; a toolbar about to open waits until the scroll ends, so opening never cuts the animation short. Shift with the note's start or end key (Windows and Linux `Mod+Shift+Home/End`) selects to there through the editor rather than the browser, whose own reveal is an instant jump; where that shifted chord is already another command's key (macOS `Mod+Shift+Down` moves a block) the command keeps it. A fresh caret (the slash menu, a link) still scrolls at once while an anchored surface is open. Round 8 (the owner, 21 October: deselecting with a click past the band still jumped): a quick click's selection reaches the editor only after the release (the browser's `selectionchange` follows `mouseup`), when the release's own check had already run against the old range; a selection change within 150 ms of a release in the note now counts as that click's move, so it glides. (2) **The focus band follows a window resize in real time:** while the window is being resized it's re-measured every frame (one layout read per frame, FOC-05), so it stays on the caret's row as lines rewrap, never waiting for the resize to end or the next caret move (FOC-02). Cause of the owner's height-resize report: the band's transition list named its properties as `var()` values, so it fell back to `all`, and the note's 25vh/35vh padding glided after every height change while the band, measured once, stayed behind. The transition now names the band's properties; any resize (width, height, both, or the top edge) marks the band stale, and the band is then written without a glide (`cmp.editorSurface.focusLiveDuration`, `motion.none`). | Proposed | [Foundations → Motion](../design/foundations.md), [Focus mode](../experience/focus-mode.md) (FOC-02) |
| D-157 | **A template repository is never your notes, on any route (security P0, proposed).** Before D-147, *We found your notes* listed the public template; the owner opened it, and the app committed and pushed his notes to it (the template has since been cleaned). D-147 only filtered the list. Now one guard, `isTemplateRepo(owner, name)` (`TemplateGuard`: the Focus Notes template by name in any letter case, without the network, or GitHub's `is_template`, kept for the session), is asked at every way in: downloading or opening from GitHub, a folder on this computer (its `origin`), switching workspace, and setup's parts (copy, private repository, download, save to GitHub). **Before every push**, saving and setup read the remote the branch pushes to and refuse a template: the commits stay here, and the save state, the trigger, and a toast say "This is a template repository. Your notes weren't saved to it." with *Run setup again*. When GitHub can't answer, the last good answer stands, and a repository never known as a template saves as normal; the known template is always refused. After security review: every push URL is checked, before the push and before its retry; the known template's name is matched anywhere in a URL; and a remote that can't be read as owner/name (GitHub, `www.`, `ssh.` on 443, or an SSH alias) is refused (sync `unreadableRemote`). The fail-open cases are listed in [Security and privacy](../architecture/security-and-privacy.md). Problem codes: sync `template`, setup `template` (with *Start over*), and opening `template`. Open: a workspace without a private repository puts new notes in the public `notes/` folder (FRE-06); queued, not changed here. | Proposed | [Desktop and Git → Save and sync](../architecture/desktop-and-git.md#save-and-sync), [First run](../experience/first-run.md), [Security and privacy](../architecture/security-and-privacy.md), D-88, D-147 |
| D-158 | **A new note in a workspace without a private repository is marked Public and isn't backed up until the writer chooses (privacy, proposed: the orchestrator's default from the handoff, for the owner to confirm).** Found by D-157: in a clone without access to the private repository (FRE-06), *New note* fell back to the public `notes/` folder and the next backup published it, against private by default (D-05, SHR-01). (1) **Where it goes:** still `notes/` (there's no private folder to put it in), and the empty note says so: its placeholder starts *Public note* (`canvas.placeholderPublic`). (2) **Marked from creation:** the bottom-left corner shows a *Public · not backed up* pill beside the save state (the read-only pill's place, UI-12) while the note waits, and the Files panel describes it the same way. (3) **Held until answered:** before the note's file is first written, the main process adds its path to a block the app manages in the notes repository's `info/exclude` (in Git's common folder: local to this clone, never committed, never pushed). Git then never lists or stages it, so neither the app's backups nor a terminal's `git add -A` can publish it, and the hold survives quitting and crashes. (4) **Asked once:** the first time a backup would include it with the writer's words in it (*Save to cloud*, or switching to another note; an empty note left behind isn't asked about) the app asks once per note and session, *Where should "⟨title⟩" live?*, with two option cards: **Make it public** (anyone can read it once it's published) and **Keep it on this computer only** (never backed up or published). Dismissing leaves it waiting; the pill asks again. *Make it public* removes the line and the note backs up with the next save; *Keep it on this computer only* moves the line to the block's *this computer* list, and the pill then says *On this computer only* (pressing it offers *Make it public* again). (5) **Following the note:** a rename that follows the title, a move in the Files panel, and a delete update the line before the file changes, so a held note's new path is never committed. (6) **Where it applies:** only notes the app creates in the public root of a workspace with no private root (new notes, and the welcome note made when there's none); with a private repository, new notes stay private by default, and choosing the Public folder in Files stays the writer's explicit choice. Residual risk: a held note renamed or moved by another program loses its hold. | Proposed | [Requirements](../product/requirements.md) (SHR-01), [Desktop and Git → Save and sync](../architecture/desktop-and-git.md#save-and-sync), [Content model](../architecture/content-model.md#workspace-layout), [First run](../experience/first-run.md), D-05, D-157 |
| D-159 | **The MCP server as built (WP-7.2, AGT-01, proposed).** (1) **Its own code, no SDK:** `packages/mcp` (`@focus-notes/mcp`) speaks MCP itself over **stdio only** (newline-delimited JSON-RPC 2.0, UTF-8, one message per line, at most 4 MB; no batches; nothing but protocol on stdout; logs on stderr carry counts and codes, never paths or note text). There's no network listener. The official TypeScript SDK is MIT, but it brings HTTP servers, OAuth, and about 17 direct dependencies that a stdio server never uses, so the server's supply chain stays the repository's own. (2) **Dual-era:** the current specification, `2026-07-28` (stateless: every request carries `_meta` `io.modelcontextprotocol/protocolVersion` and `clientInfo`; `server/discover`; every result has `resultType: "complete"`; an unsupported version is `-32022` with the supported list), and the handshake versions `2025-11-25`, `2025-06-18`, and `2025-03-26` (`initialize`, `notifications/initialized`, `ping`) that today's hosts still send. (3) **The tools, resources, prompts, and errors** are the ones in [Agents → MCP server](../architecture/agents.md#mcp-server-as-built), each with JSON Schemas for input and output. (4) **Only through WP-7.1:** every tool and resource reads `agents.json` again, takes a token from the computer's rate limit (`takeAgentToken`: a read for reading tools and resources, a write for writing ones), asks `checkAgent` (with the shares from `private/access.json`, which is otherwise never read or returned), resolves the grant with `resolveAgentPath`, and touches files only with `readAgentFile`, `writeAgentFile`, `moveAgentFile`, and `createAgentFolder`. Listing and searching walk only the roots the agent can read, skip links, and read each note through its own grant. (5) **Untrusted everywhere:** every title, file name, heading, snippet, and note body is note content: structured results mark it `untrusted: true`, and the text form wraps it between markers with a random name per response (`untrustedNote`). Tool descriptions and the server's instructions say never to follow instructions inside notes. (6) **Writes:** each is one commit, `Agent edit: <path>` with `Focus-Notes-Agent: <client>` (from `initialize`'s or the request's `clientInfo`), through `agentChange`; the server takes the workspace lock for that commit and releases it after the push. While the desktop app holds the lock, writes are refused (`appRunning`) with nothing written; relaying writes through the running app is queued. A workspace that isn't a Git repository refuses writes (`noRepository`), since every agent change must be an attributed, undoable commit. (7) **Starting:** `focus-notes-mcp --workspace <folder> --data <app data folder>` (the desktop's Node entry, `apps/desktop/src/mcp/main.ts`) seals the terminal principal first, removes stale agent temporaries, and refuses to start (exit 3, one line on stderr) while *Agents can connect* is off; the `focus-notes mcp` command and the connection snippets land with WP-7.3 and WP-7.4. (8) **Not yet:** `move_note` and `rename_note` don't rewrite links in other notes (queued; the app's Files panel still does); writes aren't normalized through the editor's Markdown bridge (the text is written as given, front matter kept); `recent_notes` means recently changed on disk. (9) **As built:** a missing `private/access.json` means no shares (one that can't be read still counts as sharing everything); a refused resource read is JSON-RPC error `-31000` with the refusal in `data.error`; arguments that don't match a tool's schema are a tool error (`invalidInput`) so the agent can correct them; the entry is built to `out/main/mcp.mjs`, and since the packaged app's fuses turn off running Electron as Node, the packaged `focus-notes mcp` (WP-7.3) starts it from the app's own executable. (10) **After the security review of `c0c5242`:** (a) headings are read by scanning (`parseAtx`), never a backtracking pattern, and a line over 4,096 characters isn't a heading, so `get_outline` and the section operations stay linear on hostile notes (it was cubic: 8,000 spaces after a heading took over 30 s); trims use `trimEnd`; (b) `-32022` echoes the requested version only when it's a string of at most 64 characters, a response that can't be serialized becomes `-32603`, and the queue can't be left rejected; (c) at most 256 requests wait their turn (more are refused at once with `-32600`), and listing reads a folder only up to the 5,000-entry cap; (d) the rate limit is per app data folder (`--data`): one computer-wide allowance across every agent of that app, but a process given another data folder has its own. | Proposed | [Agents and automation](../architecture/agents.md#mcp-server-as-built), [Implementation plan](implementation-plan.md) (WP-7.2), [Desktop and Git → One writer at a time](../architecture/desktop-and-git.md#one-writer-at-a-time), D-44, D-45, D-145 |
| D-160 | **Editor pro as built: sorting, alignment, find in selection, and the Pro layer (WP-8.1, proposed).** (1) **Sorting (TBL-09):** `table.sortAscending` and `table.sortDescending`, in the column menu (Sort A→Z, Sort Z→A, a section after Move) and the cell menu (Sort column A→Z and Z→A, so <kbd>Shift</kbd>+<kbd>F10</kbd> reaches them), for the handle's or the caret's column. Natural order (`packages/editor/src/table-sort.ts`): numbers (signs, decimals, thousands commas) by value, then ISO dates by date, then text by `Intl.Collator` with numeric order and base sensitivity (no case, no accents); empty cells last in both directions; a stable sort. Rows move whole with their formatting; the header and the alignment stay; the caret's cell moves with its row, and from a handle the column is selected. One undo step; a table already in that order isn't changed and adds none. Announced as "Column 2 sorted A to Z" (the column's number, as the other table announcements do, rather than its header text). (2) **Alignment (TBL-04)** was built in WP-1.5 (D-96); this package adds its round-trip evidence: the corpus file `26-table-alignment.md` (every marker form, short, long, and without outer pipes) and a test that an edited table's alignment survives a save and a reopen. (3) **Find in selection (FND-03):** <kbd>Alt+L</kbd> (<kbd>⌥⌘L</kbd>) is global, not a find-bar key, so it works from the note: with the bar closed it opens it scoped to the selection without filling the field; with the bar open it toggles the option. The scope is whatever was selected as the bar opened (any size), kept in its own editor plugin that maps it through edits and tints it with `color.selectionGhost` only while the option is on; prosemirror-search's range does the matching, stepping, wrapping, and replacing. With nothing selected there's no scope, so the toggle (an `inSelection` icon, Fluent's Select All On) is disabled with the reason "Select text in the note, then open find"; closing the bar forgets the scope. (4) **The Pro layer (KEY-04):** built: expand and shrink selection (`edit.expandSelection`, `edit.shrinkSelection`: <kbd>Shift+Alt+→</kbd>/<kbd>←</kbd>, <kbd>⌃⇧⌘→</kbd>/<kbd>←</kbd>; word, sentence, text block, list item, its list, the note, stopping at a table cell, with shrink retracing the steps); the Spotlight Pro keys (`spotlight.run1` to `run9`, `spotlight.copyLink`, `spotlight.nextGroup` and `prevGroup`, in the `spotlightField` scope). The table's cell selection mode and cell menu, the find toggles, and <kbd>F4</kbd> were already Pro. `packages/core/test/keymap-layers.test.ts` now reads every Pro row too. **Block selection mode** (packages/editor/src/block-select.ts, lock.select on <kbd>Esc</kbd>, outside a table): sibling blocks, decorated with the selection color; ↑/↓, Shift+↑/↓, Backspace, Delete, and Mod+Shift+K (one undo step), Mod+C, Enter, and Esc as the spec says; with one block selected the block keys act on it and the selection follows it; typing or a click leaves the mode. Every editor journey was re-run with it (M1 editor, tables, find, keys, canvas, formatting, slash and paste, focus, accessibility, the demo, M4 power keys and find all). Each change in the count is announced politely ("Block selected", "2 blocks selected", through lock.selected), and Mod+C's copy is unit-tested. **Planned, not built:** *Copy the note as Markdown*; reveal in Finder or Explorer and the Files preview (the Files panel's own package); replace in all notes (WP-8.4). (5) **The block handle (EDT-07)** is specified on the editor's own block commands rather than Tiptap's DragHandle, so a drag, a menu item, and a key run the same move and a drag is one undo step; *Copy link to block* is offered for headings only, because Markdown links only to headings. **Built so far:** the editor's side, `block.moveTo` (a block, by its position, dropped at a boundary between its parent's blocks, a list item only within its list, as one transaction and one undo step, the caret at its start) and every `block.*` command taking `block` (the handle's block) to act on it rather than the caret's (`packages/editor/test/blocks.test.ts`). The handle is the **BlockHandle** host component (like `TableControls`): the editor surface's block tracker publishes the hovered block's box and first line (`block-geometry.ts`) in one animation frame per pointer move, never while typing, and BlockHandle draws a six-dot grip (`cmp.blockHandle.*`) 24 px outside the reading column. Pressing it turns it the accent color in the next frame; releasing without moving opens the block menu (`vm.block`: Turn into, Duplicate, Move up and down with their keys, Copy link to a heading, Delete) through `CommandMenu`, tinting the block it acts on without moving the caret; moving 4 px drags, with an accent drop line at the nearest boundary between its siblings, the note scrolling near its top and bottom edges, and <kbd>Esc</kbd> cancelling. The open menu calls `anchors.opening()`, so the note's auto-scroll ends and later scrolls are instant (DEF-21), and the grip it hangs from is placed again every frame the note moves. The keyboard path is a new Pro key, `block.contextMenu`: <kbd>Shift+F10</kbd> or the Menu key outside a table opens the same menu at the caret's block (a table's own cell menu keeps the key there). Moves, duplicates, and deletes are announced ("Block moved"). Two choices beyond the spec: the grip doesn't show beside a table, whose row handles sit in the same gutter (its table handle duplicates and deletes it, and the block keys move it); and the grip is out of the <kbd>Tab</kbd> order, because it shows only on hover. **Not yet:** the grip for the focused block on touch screens. | Proposed | [Tables](../design/components/tables.html), [Find and replace](../experience/find-and-replace.md#find-in-selection-p1), [Keyboard shortcuts](../experience/keyboard-shortcuts.md), [Text formatting → Block handle](../design/components/text-formatting.html#block-handle), [Editing](../experience/editing.md), [Implementation plan → M8](implementation-plan.md#m8), D-96, D-115 |
| D-161 | **Files can be pinned open, floating, and the note's text moves clear of it (the owner: "I should have an option to pin the files panel… I want it pinned and not docked", proposed).** (1) **The control:** a pin `IconButton` in the Files sheet's header (`pin` icon, then `pinOff`; its name and tooltip *Keep Files open*, then *Unpin Files*; `aria-pressed`), and the command `files.togglePin` (*Keep Files open*) in Spotlight's commands, the ⋯ menu's View group and the macOS menu bar's Go menu, shortcut <kbd>Mod</kbd>+<kbd>Alt</kbd>+<kbd>P</kbd> (proposed: <kbd>Mod</kbd>+<kbd>Shift</kbd>+<kbd>P</kbd> is Spotlight's commands mode). It replaces the footer's *Keep open* (P1) on the Files page. (2) **Pinned is the same sheet:** the floating frosted `Sheet` with its inset, radius and shadow, never docked edge to edge and never a split pane. Opening a note, a click on the canvas, and <kbd>Mod</kbd>+<kbd>O</kbd> don't close it: <kbd>Mod</kbd>+<kbd>O</kbd> and <kbd>Esc</kbd> move focus between the tree and the note (<kbd>Esc</kbd> in the panel returns the caret to the note and keeps it pinned); only the pin button or the command unpins, and then the panel closes as before. As built: the sheet publishes where it ends (`pinnedSheet` in `behaviors/pinned-sheet.ts`), the note's scroller holds the column's clearance in a registered, inherited length that glides (a window resize applies at once), and each frame of the glide the scroll follows the caret's line before the frame is drawn (measured locally: no movement of the caret's line over 120 frames while the text narrowed from 520 to 438 px). (3) **The text moves clear:** the reading column is laid out in the space right of the sheet (the sheet's right edge plus `space.l`), centred there when it's wide enough for the measure; narrower, the column narrows down to `cmp.editorSurface.pinnedColumnMin` (360 px); in a window where even that doesn't fit, the panel floats over the text as an unpinned one does. The column's move animates over `motion.sheet` (reduced motion: at once), and the caret's line stays at the same height in the window throughout; a shift the person caused by pinning is input-caused, so the DSK-09 layout-shift check still holds. The focus band, the top edge, the trigger and toasts are unchanged: they follow the column or the window, not the sheet. (4) **Kept per device** in `state.json` (`filesPinned`); a pinned panel opens at launch without taking the caret. (5) **Both apps:** the browser has the same panel, so it pins too. | Proposed | [Files](../design/views/files.html), [Information architecture](../design/information-architecture.html), D-111, D-148 |
| D-162 | **Every file and folder has its menu: right-click, the row's ⋯, <kbd>Shift</kbd>+<kbd>F10</kbd> and the Menu key (the owner: "I should be able to right click on a file or a folder and be able to delete, duplicate, move etc.", proposed).** (1) **Opening it:** right-click on a row, the row's ⋯ button (shown on hover, on the focused row, and on selected rows), or <kbd>Shift</kbd>+<kbd>F10</kbd> and the Menu key on the focused row. It's the app-drawn `CommandMenu` (IA-CMP-CONTEXTMENU, DD-FB-05) in a popover at the pointer or under the ⋯ button, themed and keyboard-reachable, never the native menu; Esc closes it and focus returns to the row. Right-click on an unselected row selects it first; on a selected row the selection stays. (2) **Items, by what's acted on:** a note: *Open*, *Rename* (F2), *Duplicate*, *Move to…* (Mod+Shift+M), *Make public* or *Make private*, *Copy public link* (a public note, SHR-03), *Delete* (Delete); a folder: *New note*, *New folder*, *Rename*, *Move to…*, *Make public* or *Make private*, *Delete*; a section: *New note*, *New folder*; several selected: *Duplicate* (notes), *Move to…*, *Make public* or *Make private*, *Delete*, acting on the selection. Each item shows its shortcut; an unavailable one stays reachable and says why (D-111's menu rule). A read-only section (the browser's instance notes) lists only what's allowed there. *Show in Explorer/Finder* waits for a shell bridge and is queued. (3) **Duplicate (FIL-11's duplicate, delivered here; sorting stays WP-8.4):** "⟨title⟩ copy" next to the original (then "⟨title⟩ copy 2"…), its H1 retitled and links unchanged, without the original's front-matter `id` (a share by ID never reaches the copy), in one batch and one commit; it shows in the tree at once (PRF-09), selected and focused, and the toast offers *Undo*, which deletes the copy. | Proposed | [Files](../design/views/files.html), [Files and sharing](../experience/files-and-sharing.md), [Feedback and dialogs → Context menu](../design/components/feedback-and-dialogs.md#context-menu), FIL-11 |
| D-165 | **The command-line tool and deep links as built (WP-7.3, AGT-05, AGT-06, proposed).** (1) **One entry, two ways in:** the commands are `packages/cli` (`@focus-notes/cli`); the desktop wires them to Git (`apps/desktop/src/cli/`). Released builds turn off running Electron as Node (the `runAsNode` fuse), so the packaged app's own executable dispatches: `focus-notes --cli <command> …` runs the command in the main process before anything else starts (no window, no single-instance lock, no workspace watcher) and exits with its code. A launcher in the app's resources, `bin/focus-notes.cmd` on Windows and `bin/focus-notes` on macOS and Linux, runs `<app executable> --cli %*`, so people type `focus-notes ls`. In development and tests the same commands run under Node (`out/main/cli.mjs`). (2) **On the PATH (decided in [Distribution](distribution.md)):** Settings → Agents → *Install command-line tool* (WP-7.4) adds the launcher's folder to the user's own `Path` on Windows (no administrator), and on macOS and Linux links `~/.local/bin/focus-notes` to it, saying when that folder isn't on the `PATH`. Nothing is added at install time. (3) **The person at the terminal:** a command claims the terminal principal (`claimTerminal`) once, in its entry, and acts as the person: both roots whatever the switches say, with the same path, disk and sharing checks as agents; deleting, visibility and sharing aren't commands. `focus-notes mcp` never claims it: it seals it first and runs the MCP server (D-159), so the server keeps the agent switches. The terminal isn't rate-limited (the limits stop agents' runaway loops), and its output isn't wrapped as untrusted: it's the person reading their own notes. D-145 (10)(e) stands: an agent that can run programs can run the tool and so act as the person; Settings → Agents says the tool acts as you. (4) **Commands:** `ls`, `cat`, `search`, `new`, `append`, `edit`, `mv`, `open`, `status`, `sync`, and `mcp`, each with `--json` and `--workspace`; writes are one attributed commit each through `agentChange`, named `command line`; while the desktop app holds the workspace, writes are refused (`appRunning`) and nothing is written. Exit codes: 0 done, 1 error (including `appRunning`, `notFound`, usage), 2 conflict (`changed`, `exists`), 3 not permitted (any refusal of the checks). With `--json`, standard output is exactly one object: `{"ok": true, …}` or `{"ok": false, "error": <code>, "message": …}`. (5) **Deep links are read-only:** `focus-notes://open?path=<notes/… or private/notes/…>[&heading=<anchor>]` and `focus-notes://search?q=<text>`. The spec's `new` link is dropped: making a note writes a file at once, and no link may write. `parseDeepLink` in core accepts exactly these: the scheme, `open` or `search` as the host, no user, password, port, path or fragment, only the named parameters, each once, a note path that `agentPath` accepts in one of the two roots ending in `.md`, a heading anchor of letters, digits, `-` and `_`, a query of 1 to 200 characters without control characters, and a link of at most 2,048 characters; anything else is ignored. The main process resolves the path inside the open workspace's roots and opens it only if the note is there; a link never names a folder outside them. (6) **Registered per platform** only in a packaged app (never in development or journeys): `app.setAsDefaultProtocolClient("focus-notes")` on Windows and Linux, which arrive as the first instance's arguments or a second instance's; `open-url` on macOS; electron-builder's `protocols` for the macOS bundle and the Linux desktop file. (7) **As built:** `--cli` counts only as the executable's first argument (after the app's path in development), and never when any argument is a `focus-notes:` link, so a link can't become a command; the MCP server and the tool skip a root that isn't there (a clone without its private repository), which WP-7.2 failed on with *Read private*; `open` refuses a path that isn't a valid note link before handing anything to the system. (8) **After the security review of `d8cdb0c`:** (a) a search link only ever searches notes: Spotlight opens in notes mode with the query as it is, never read for its `>`, `#`, `##`, or `?` prefixes, so a link can't put a command one Enter away; (b) a link's note is resolved under the agents' rules (every part spelled as on disk, no symbolic link, junction, or short name, no second hard link), and the path opened is the root's own with those checked names; (c) `focus-notes.cmd` runs with `setlocal DisableDelayedExpansion` and keeps CRLF line endings (`.gitattributes`). Batch files still re-read `%*`, so arguments with `"`, `^`, `&`, `|`, `<`, `>`, or `%` can be mangled: quote them, or call the executable with `--cli` yourself; a small native or PowerShell launcher is queued; (e) after the re-review of `0e44978`: a link's query shows results only, with no *Create note* or *Find* rows and no quick actions, so Enter on no match writes nothing; typing makes the query the person's own again; (d) known limitation: Electron's own guard against arguments that look like URLs may stop a command whose argument starts with a scheme (`focus-notes search https://x`); search for the rest of the text, or use the Node entry. | Proposed | [Agents and automation](../architecture/agents.md#command-line-tool), [Distribution](distribution.md), [Implementation plan](implementation-plan.md) (WP-7.3), D-44, D-145, D-159 |
| D-166 | **Settings → Agents and the published catalog as built (WP-7.4, AGT-02, DSY-13, proposed).** (1) **One page, from `agents.json`:** Settings gains *Agents* (desktop only). The main process reads and writes `agents.json` (`readAgentPermissions`, `writeAgentPermissions`); the window never names the file. *Agents can connect* is the first switch, then *Read public notes*, *Read private and shared notes*, *Create and edit notes* (which covers moving and renaming, D-145 (4)), and *Create public notes*; each is a toggle button with one line on what it allows, and the ones after the first are disabled while agents can't connect. Each change is written at once and read back, so the page shows what the file holds. *Limit to folders* shows the current limit (all folders, or the list); editing the list is queued. (2) **Changed outside the app:** when the file's check doesn't match, the page says so in a warning line, and the switches show the defaults the server uses; turning any switch writes a fresh file. (3) **Connection snippets:** for VS Code (`mcp.json`), Claude Desktop (`claude_desktop_config.json`), and a generic stdio command, each a code block with its own Copy (the shell's clipboard; journeys use the in-memory one). They start the app's own executable with `--cli mcp --data <the app's data folder>` (D-165); in development, Electron with the built main script. Copying says *Copied*. They carry no secret: the switches are what protect. (4) **Recent agent changes:** the last 50 attributed commits from both repositories (`agentChanges`), newest first, each as *⟨client⟩ (self-reported)*, the note's path, and when; *Undo* runs `undoAgentChange`, whose rules are D-145's (only an agent commit in this branch, by this computer's identity, refused when the note changed since); a refusal is said in a toast and nothing changes. (5) **Install command-line tool** (D-165 (2)), only in an installed app: on Windows the launcher's folder is added to the user's own `Path` by `[Environment]::SetEnvironmentVariable(…, "User")` in PowerShell, with the folder passed in an environment variable, never in the command's text, and only if it isn't there yet (compared without case); on macOS and Linux `~/.local/bin/focus-notes` becomes a link to the launcher, never replacing a file that isn't already that link, and the page says when `~/.local/bin` isn't on the `PATH`. Development builds and journeys never change the `PATH`: the button says it works in the installed app. (6) **The published catalog (DSY-13):** `ui-check --write` also writes `schemas/ui/catalog.v1.json`, generated from the contracts, tokens, icons, views, and fragments (each component's type, tier, description, props, events, slots, states, tokens, accessibility, and fixture names; every token group's names; each view's id, IA ID, and view models), checked for staleness by `pnpm check` like the other generated files (D-138). The *Pages* workflow publishes `schemas/` beside the docs, so the catalog is at `/schemas/ui/catalog.v1.json` with the contracts and the view schema. No HTML is generated: agents read JSON, and people read the gallery and these docs. (7) **As built:** the app's *Copy* (`clipboard:write`) now goes through the app's clipboard seam, so journeys copy into memory and never touch the computer's clipboard; the empty recent list says *No agent changes yet*. (8) **After the security review of `ba5dcb9`:** (a) the Windows install no longer pipes a script to `powershell -Command -` (which stopped before the multi-line block and still exited 0); two fixed scripts run from a temporary file with `-File`, by Windows PowerShell's absolute path under `%SystemRoot%\System32`; (b) the user's `Path` is read raw through `Microsoft.Win32.Registry` with `DoNotExpandEnvironmentNames`, the new value is worked out in the app, and it's written with the kind it had (`REG_EXPAND_SZ` stays expandable; a missing `Path` is made expandable), only if the value is still the one read, then `WM_SETTINGCHANGE` is broadcast and the value is read back: a write that didn't take is an error, never a silent success; (c) values travel base64-encoded in environment variables, so names outside ASCII survive; a folder with `;`, `"`, or a line break is refused, and a `Path` that isn't a string value is left alone; (d) the third snippet is quoted for this computer's shell (PowerShell on Windows, a POSIX shell elsewhere), and the JSON snippets keep the command and its arguments apart. | Proposed | [Agents and automation](../architecture/agents.md#settings-agents-as-built), [Views as data](../design/system/views-as-data.html), [Implementation plan](implementation-plan.md) (WP-7.4), D-138, D-145, D-159, D-165 |
| D-167 | **The project's GitHub Pages site becomes the product site, with help and a demo (owner, 2026-10-16; proposed).** The owner asked for support articles for people who aren't developers, a marketing page as the default landing page of the project repository's GitHub Pages site, and the demo hosted there. (1) **Addresses:** `/` is the landing page, `/help/` the help centre, `/help/whats-new/` the release notes, `/demo/` the demo, `/download/` the downloads, `/privacy/` the privacy statement. The specification stays at `/documentations/`, and the JSON Schemas and design-system catalogue at `/schemas/`. (2) **The owner's own Focus Notes** (D-87), which `/` served until now, moves to `/app/`. Public note pages stay at `/n/`, so their addresses and canonical links don't change. The owner's day-to-day instance is now `prabins-notes`, so whether this repository should keep publishing an instance at all is the owner's call. (3) **The demo** is the web app built from the same commit as the release it links to, with the template's starting notes plus about ten sample notes. It's kept in the visitor's browser, with no sign-in, nothing sent anywhere, a demo banner with *Download* and *Reset demo*, and it never offers to save to GitHub. The owner wrote the demo's address as `github.com/prabinpebam/focus-notes/demo`, which GitHub can't serve; this reads it as `prabinpebam.github.io/focus-notes/demo/` (the owner to confirm). (4) **Supersedes** the part of D-87 and distribution.md that made `/` the owner's instance, and revives D-54's product site at the project repository's own Pages, still with nothing hosted for others (D-86). (5) **As built (WP-10.1):** the share reader stays at `/s/`, and the notes and sealed files stay at the site's root, so no published or shared address changes; the app at `/app/` is a build of the same web app that reads them from the folder above it (`dist-app/`). | Accepted (owner, 16 Oct): "make this live". The owner wrote the demo's address as github.com/prabinpebam/focus-notes/demo; GitHub Pages serves it at prabinpebam.github.io/focus-notes/demo/ | [Product site and help](../product/site-and-help.md), [Distribution → The project's own site](distribution.md#the-product-site), [Requirements](../product/requirements.md) (HLP-01, HLP-05, HLP-08) |
| D-168 | **Help articles follow one content model and writing guide, and the app links to them by ID (proposed).** From the owner's brief and a research review of the Microsoft and Apple style guides, the GitHub Docs content model, Diátaxis, GOV.UK and plainlanguage.gov, NN/g and Carroll's minimalism. (1) **Seven content types** (getting started, how-to, concept, troubleshooting, reference, FAQ, what's new), one per article, each with a template ([Writing help articles](../design/help-writing.md)). (2) **Eight categories by user goal:** Get started, Write, Organize your notes, Save and back up, Private notes and sharing, Fix a problem, Reference, For developers and AI agents. (3) **Plain words:** about grade 8 or below, checked in CI (fails above 9); technical words explained on first use; the main path avoids commit, push, clone, token, credential, instance and CLI. (4) **Stable IDs:** articles live in `site/help/<category>/<id>.md` with front matter. The app's *Learn more* and *How to fix this* name `help:<id>#<anchor>`, mapped by `packages/views/help-links.json`. CI fails on an unknown ID, a missing anchor or a broken link, and a renamed article keeps a redirect. (5) **Troubleshooting** articles start from the message's exact words, and every problem message in the app links to one (HLP-06). (6) **Feedback without tracking:** *Was this helpful?* opens a pre-filled GitHub issue, with no counters, cookies or requests (PRV-01). (7) **Search** runs in the browser from an index built with the site (HLP-09). | Proposed | [Writing help articles](../design/help-writing.md), [Product site and help](../product/site-and-help.md#the-help-centre), [Definition of done](definition-of-done.md#help-article-definition-of-done) (HLP-02, HLP-03, HLP-06, HLP-07, HLP-09) |
| D-169 | **Screenshots on the site are captured from the release build in CI (proposed).** Hand-made screenshots drift from the app. (1) A `site` catalogue in `tools/review/catalogs.mjs` drives the release build with the demo workspace through each screen the site shows, at 1280 × 800 for the full window and 720 × 480 for details, in light and dark, at 2× scale. (2) Each is cropped to the window with its own corners and shadow on a neutral background, with no desktop or taskbar and at most one highlight. (3) Each has alt text and an optional caption, kept in the catalogue beside the capture. (4) Captures run in the screen-level CI job (D-141, never on the owner's computer) for each release and on demand, and are committed under `site/media/`. (5) A check fails when a view document a screenshot shows has changed since the screenshot was taken. (6) Focus mode's loop is a short capture with a still frame for reduced motion. | Proposed | [Product site and help → Screenshots](../product/site-and-help.md#screenshots) (HLP-04) |
| D-171 | **Nothing is public unless the person chose it (the owner's rule, after the app opened an empty public note at launch, proposed).** (1) **The note at launch:** only the note the person last opened (`lastNotePath` in the app's state) is reopened as it is, and never when it's an empty file under *Public*: an empty note holds nothing anyone chose to publish, and the template's `notes/` has such files (an empty `exploratory-conversation.md`), which an earlier launch could have left as the remembered note. Anything else (a fresh profile, that note deleted or moved, or empty and public) opens the app's own note in the Private root, the private welcome note, made there when it's missing, rather than a new untitled note each time: it's private, it's remembered like any note opened so a launch never makes another, and the workspace journeys know it. Never the first note found in `notes/`. After setup, the first note is a new note (2). (2) **Every way to create a note defaults to Private:** <kbd>Mod</kbd>+<kbd>N</kbd> and the toolbar's *New* with any note open or none, Spotlight's *Create note*, the note after setup, and the empty workspace: next to the open note only when it's known to be private (the Files tree has it under *Private*), else in the Private root, including before the tree has been read at launch, when the open note's visibility isn't known yet. Only a folder the person chose in Files puts it under *Public*. Agents already create only under `private/notes/` unless allowed (`createPublic`, D-145). There's no command-line `new` yet; it will default to Private. (3) **A workspace without a private repository** keeps D-158: the new note is under *Public*, held out of backups until the writer chooses. (4) **Not changed here:** a new note is still written at once, empty (Files → What's built), not at its first character as Files and sharing says; that's its own change. A last note in another workspace folder (after *Open another workspace*) is still reopened, because a note opened with *Open…* outside the workspace is a choice too; telling them apart needs the folder kept with `lastNotePath`, queued. (5) **Checked:** `packages/core/test/private-default.test.ts` (the launch note and every create path, including <kbd>Mod</kbd>+<kbd>N</kbd> from a public note before the tree is read, which created the note next to it under *Public* before this) and the journey `d171-private-default.spec.ts` (a fresh profile on a workspace whose `notes/` has an empty public note: the shown note is a new private note and nothing is created in `notes/`; <kbd>Mod</kbd>+<kbd>N</kbd> from a public note creates under *Private*). | Proposed | [Requirements](../product/requirements.md) (SHR-01), [Files and sharing](../experience/files-and-sharing.md), [Files](../design/views/files.html), [First run](../experience/first-run.md), D-99, D-158 |
| D-172 | **The note's top scroll edge is visible all the way to the window's edge (the owner: "I want the blur effect to be visible all the way to the top edge of the window. Right now it visibly disappears completely much before the top edge"; amends D-119).** (1) **Why it vanished:** the edge did start at the window's top, but its 40 px outermost blur under a 92% canvas veil (85% over the trigger) left the text about a thirtieth of its contrast in the top 16 px: nothing to see well before the edge. (2) **Now:** the top edge has its own, lighter blur, `layout.scrollBlurTop` (8 px at the window's edge, halving inward over the same eight layers), and a light veil: `layout.scrollVeil` 30% at the edge, `layout.triggerVeil` 25% at the trigger place's bottom, on the same eased curve to none at 130 px. Blurred text stays visibly there right up to the edge; under the trigger it's softened and quiet, and the dot keeps its own frosted ring. The bottom edge is unchanged (40 px, fading to the canvas). (3) **Without the blur** (reduced transparency, software compositing, D-121), unblurred text under the dot would read, so the veil keeps the old curve: fully opaque at the edge and at least `layout.triggerVeilPlain` (85%) over the trigger. (4) **Checks:** the visual quality check fails when the top edge starts below the window's drawn top, when its outermost blur isn't `scrollBlurTop`, or when the veil at the edge is more than `scrollVeil`; the canvas journey captures the app's own window (never the screen, D-141) with a text line right under the top and fails unless the top 16 px show at least an eighth of crisp text's contrast (old values: a thirtieth; 12 px under 45%: a tenth). (5) Values chosen by rendering the variants in the app; **for the owner to confirm in Preview.** | Proposed | [Foundations → Visual quality](../design/foundations.md#visual-quality), [Main UI](../experience/main-ui.html), D-119, D-121 |
| D-170 | **Toolbar and keybinding customization as built (WP-8.3, UI-08, KEY-07, proposed).** (1) **Pinning six:** *Customize toolbar…* (in ⋯'s app group, and in Settings → Keyboard) opens a dialog with the pinned actions in order and the actions that can be pinned. Each pinned row has *Move up*, *Move down*, and *Unpin*; each other row has *Pin*, disabled with its reason while six are pinned; *Reset to default* puts D-18's six back. Everything is buttons, so the keyboard does all of it; dragging is queued. The pinnable actions are the six defaults and ⋯'s plain commands (no submenus, nothing planned). The choice is kept per device in `state.json` (`toolbarPinned`), as other device settings are (D-46 syncs only what Settings → Appearance syncs). (2) **The rest go into ⋯:** a pinned action leaves ⋯; an unpinned default appears in a first ⋯ group, *Actions*, so nothing is ever unreachable and the toolbar never holds more than six. (3) **`keybindings.json`** lives in the app's data folder (per device), with `schemas/keybindings.schema.json`: a list of `{ "command": "<id>", "key": "<chord>" }`, optionally with `"platform": "macos" | "windows" | "linux"`; a leading `-` on the command removes that default chord instead. Chords use the cheat sheet's names (`Mod`, `Ctrl`, `Alt`, `Shift`, `Meta`, then a key). The `when` field of the earlier draft schema is dropped: contexts aren't configurable yet. (4) **Layered over the defaults:** user chords are added to a command and win over a default chord on the same key in the same context (they're matched first); removals drop a default chord. Menus, Spotlight, the cheat sheet, tooltips, and the editor's own keymap show the effective keys. (5) **Never a crash:** a file that isn't JSON or isn't a list, an entry that isn't an object, an unknown command, a chord that can't be read, a key the OS keeps (`OS_RESERVED`), or an unknown platform is skipped and reported by its position; the rest apply. (6) **Conflicts:** a key that runs more than one command in the same context is listed in Settings → Keyboard and at the top of the cheat sheet, saying which command wins. (7) **Live:** the main process checks the file twice a second (a poll, which also sees editors that replace the file), and a change applies within a second, without restarting. (8) **Settings → Keyboard:** the file's state (no custom keys, or how many apply), its problems, its conflicts, *Edit keybindings.json* (made with `[]` when missing, then opened in the system's editor; not in journeys, which mustn't take the screen), *Reset keyboard shortcuts* (writes `[]`), and *Customize toolbar…*. (9) **After the security review of `8f4e900`:** the file's size is checked before it's read (over 256 KB is reported and never read); *Edit keybindings.json* shows the system's reason when it can't open the file; *Reset keyboard shortcuts* asks first, with *Keep my keyboard shortcuts* focused; and a search link's query stays the link's (no *Create note* or actions) through a change of Spotlight's mode, until the person types (D-165 (8e)). | Proposed | [Keyboard shortcuts](../experience/keyboard-shortcuts.md#custom-keybindings-as-built), [Main UI](../experience/main-ui.html#the-minimal-toolbar), [Security and privacy](../architecture/security-and-privacy.md#data-inventory), D-18, D-46 |
| D-173 | **Custom themes, first slice: theme files read from the workspace (WP-8.2, THM-04, proposed).** (1) **Where:** the desktop reads `private/.focus-notes/themes/*.json` (or `.focus-notes/themes/` in a workspace without a private repository) once at launch, before a remembered theme is put back: at most 100 files of up to 64 KB, regular files only, through `themes:list`; the web app reads none yet. (2) **The file:** Theming → Custom themes' format: `name`, `kind`, `base`, `tokens`. Only color tokens its base has, with hex values (`#rgb` or `#rrggbb`), are kept, so a file can't inject CSS (UNSUP-THEME-01); a key color sets the tokens it leads (the accent sets the link and focus ring) unless the file sets them; a base of the other kind becomes that kind's default. Its ID is `custom:` and its name made a slug; two files with one name keep the first by file name. (3) **Showing it:** the root shows the base's styles (`data-theme`) with the theme's colors over them, through AppRoot's `themeColors` (the way an edited copy already shows, D-108), and Edit colors' changes go over those. (4) **The theme manager:** custom themes are listed first, by name, described as "Yours, light, AA"; their tiles are sampled in their own colors (the sample tokens set on the tile); one failing the contrast guard's pairs (THM-13) is badged *Low contrast* and still offered (DD-THEME-05). Keyboard focus previews, Enter chooses it for its slot (switching the appearance like a built-in), and it's remembered as `lightTheme` or `darkTheme`; Settings → Appearance names it. A remembered custom theme whose file is gone falls back to the slot's default. (5) **Saving edited colors (slice 2):** once a key color is edited, Edit colors offers *Save as a theme*: the copy is written as a new file there (`<slug>.json`, through `themes:save`, never over another file, inside the workspace only, and not in a workspace this app mustn't write, DST-05), named "Paper (custom)" (a copy of a custom theme keeps its name, numbered "Paper (custom) 2"), joins the gallery, fills its slot, and the edit ends. A copy failing the guard's pairs is asked about first ("This theme has 2 hard-to-read pairs. Save anyway?", in the danger tone and announced), and the button becomes *Save anyway* (UNSUP-THEME-02, DD-THEME-05). (6) **Slice 3:** with custom themes, the gallery is in two groups, *Yours* then *Focus Notes*, each a labelled group of options under its heading in the one grid (so the arrow keys still move across it). The theme files are read again whenever the theme manager opens: a new one shows, a changed one shows its new colors, and a slot whose file is gone goes back to its default. A theme's options are a ⋯ menu button above the gallery for the theme showing ("Options for Sepia evening"), not a button inside each tile, because a button inside a list box's option is a nested control screen readers and axe reject: *Duplicate* (any theme, saved as a numbered copy and chosen), and for one of yours *Show file* (the system's file manager) and *Delete*, which removes the file, puts the slot back to its default, and offers *Undo* in a toast, which writes it back and refills the slot. A custom theme chosen in a slot already syncs through *Sync appearance* (THM-05): the slot's ID goes to `settings.json`, and the file is in the private repository. **Not yet:** *Rename*, export, filter chips, the ignored tokens said in the manager, named schemes (THM-09), import (THM-10), generation (THM-11), and shuffle (THM-12). (7) **Checked:** `packages/core/test/custom-themes.test.ts`, the journey `m8-custom-themes.spec.ts` (keyboard only for choosing, axe; with deliberately hard-to-read colors showing, every axe rule but color contrast; the groups, a file added while running, Duplicate, Delete and Undo). | Proposed | [Theme manager](../design/views/theme-manager.html#as-built), [Theming → Custom themes](../experience/theming.md#custom-themes-p1), D-108 |
| D-174 | **The landing page is visual first, in the manner of apple.com's product pages (owner, 2026-10-16; amends D-167 and D-168 for the landing page).** The owner rejected a landing page of prose and lists: "Nobody reads. It has to be visual first." (1) **Hero:** one headline of at most 6 words in large display type, one line of at most 14 words, *Download* and *Try the demo*, and a large screenshot of the app in focus mode, as wide as the page allows. (2) **At most 5 featured sections,** each an optional eyebrow, a headline of at most 5 words, one line of at most 14 words, and a large screenshot (or a short loop, if motion sells it). (3) **One grid of small tiles,** an icon and at most 4 words each, for secondary points. (4) **A closing call to action** of one line. (5) **Banned:** prose paragraphs and bulleted prose. The questions, *Is it for you?* and the explanations move to the help centre, behind one *Learn how it works* link. (6) **Budget:** at most 120 words of visible text outside the navigation and footer, and no text block over 20 words, checked by the site's builder; VVS ≥ 80 (D-127). (7) **Type and layout:** the system's display sans (`font.display`: Segoe UI Variable Display, SF Pro Display, then the system's UI face), never the note font, on a display scale of tokens (`font.siteDisplay`, `font.siteHeadline`, `font.siteLead`), centred sections with generous space, through the site's components (SiteText, SiteFigure, SiteGrid). (8) **Screenshots** come from the app's own window (`webContents.capturePage`, never the screen) with a curated demo workspace, light and dark, through the `site` catalogue (D-169); every image has alt text, a width and a height, so nothing shifts. | Proposed (the owner reviews the preview) | [Product site and help → The landing page](../product/site-and-help.md#the-landing-page), [Writing help articles](../design/help-writing.md) (HLP-01, HLP-04, HLP-08) |
| D-175 | **Sorting the Files tree by name or by last change (WP-8.4, FIL-11, first slice, proposed).** (1) **The control:** the Files header's sort button (⇅), a menu with *Name* and *Last changed*, the one in use checked; also two commands, *Sort files by name* and *Sort files by last change*, in Spotlight. The choice is per device, kept in `state.json` as `filesSort` (absent means Name). (2) **Order:** folders stay first in both. By Name, as before: folders, then notes, each by file name (numbers in order). By Last changed: notes newest first, and a folder by its newest note (an empty folder last), ties by name; the roots stay where they are. (3) **When it moves:** the order comes from each note's modification time as the shell read it when it listed the workspace, or when a change from outside arrives; the app's own saves don't move rows, so the note being written never jumps under the pointer or the caret while the panel is pinned beside it, and nothing is computed per keystroke (PRF-01, PRF-14): the order is worked out only when the tree, the open folders, the filter, or the choice change. (4) **Not yet:** the changed time on rows ("2 h", Files → Anatomy); duplicating (FIL-11's other half) is already built (D-162). (5) **Note history (FIL-13, second slice):** ⋯ → Note history, the Go menu or Spotlight opens a 380 px side sheet (IA-VIEW-HISTORY) on the open note's versions from Git, read only when it opens (`git log --follow`, so a rename doesn't cut the history), 50 at a time: *Show older versions* reads the next page from the oldest one read (`--skip` doesn't work with `--follow`). Rows are grouped by day, newest first, each with its time and author; an agent's commit shows the agent's name and an *Agent* label from its `Focus-Notes-Agent` trailer. ↑/↓ move, Enter shows a version below the list, block by block, with the blocks that differ from the note now marked *Changed* (the conflict resolver's VersionPane), with *Restore this version* and *Back to current*; Esc closes. Restore puts that text in the note as a normal save, so the cadence commits it as a new version and the newer ones stay in history (DD-HIST-01); it's announced, and the toast's *Undo* puts back the text it replaced. The main process reads only a commit ID and a Markdown path in the note's own repository. Waits are named in the sheet: "Reading versions…", "Reading older versions…", "Reading that version…" (wait inventory). Nothing runs per keystroke: the list and the comparison are built only when the history or the version shown changes. **Not yet:** showing the version in the canvas itself, a one-line summary of each change, and *Undo this change* on an agent's row. | Proposed | [Files](../design/views/files.html), [Files and sharing](../experience/files-and-sharing.md), DD-FILES-09, [Secondary views → Note history](../design/views/secondary-views.md#note-history) (FIL-13) |
| D-176 | **The site's screenshots are desktop scenes, kept free of banding, and themes are a selling point (owner, 2026-10-16; amends D-169 and D-174).** The owner saw banding in the gradients, and the page's background and the app's window were too alike. (1) **Every screenshot is a scene:** a 1920 × 1080 desktop with the app's own window centred on it at its real 1× size (1440 × 900, about 75% of the desktop's width, with the wallpaper around it; its drawn corners and shadow composited with their alpha). The owner first saw a 2560 × 1440 desktop and found the window too small on it. (2) **The desktop** is "Photo by Cristina Gottardi on Unsplash" (Unsplash License, free to use, no attribution required), cropped to 16:9 from the original and resized once with Lanczos to 1920 × 1080, kept losslessly in `site/media/source/` and credited anyway in the footer, on the privacy page and in `site/media/source/CREDITS.md` (a non-code asset; `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` lists code). Dark scenes dim it to 72%. (3) **No banding:** `tools/site/scenes.py` composites in floating point and writes each scene once per width, 1920 and 960, as AVIF 4:4:4 at quality 90 and as WebP at quality 94 for browsers without AVIF. SiteFigure serves them through `<picture>` with `srcset`, `sizes`, explicit width and height, and the hero at high fetch priority. Quality wins over size: the hero is about 140 KB at 1920 and 40 KB at 960. (4) **Light mode in every scene** (the owner: "the app looks better in light mode"), whatever the visitor's own setting. **The hero never uses focus mode:** it shows the whole note at full contrast. **No scrollbar in any scene:** each is captured with the pointer away and no scroller scrolling or hovered, the design system's resting state, in which its minimal scrollbar is hidden (D-73); no capture-only style. Dark mode is an option: the Themes section's scene has a small *Light / Dark* choice under it (SiteSceneToggle), two radio buttons that switch the image with CSS alone, since the site has no script; a labelled group, operable from the keyboard. (5) **Themes** are a featured section (*Make it yours.*) with the theme panel's gallery. | Accepted (owner, 16 Oct) | [Product site and help → Screenshots](../product/site-and-help.md#screenshots) (HLP-01, HLP-04) |
| D-177 | **The demo at /demo/ is the web app's demo build on sample notes kept in the visitor's browser (owner, 2026-10-16: "get the demo page ready"; WP-10.6).** `apps/web` built with `--mode demo` to `dist-demo/`; `tools/site/build.mjs --demo` places it at `/demo/` in place of `site/demo.md`'s placeholder, and `pages.yml` builds it from the same commit as the app. Its own IndexedDB database (`focus-notes-demo`), so the owner's notes, `/n/` and the vault are never read or touched; nothing is fetched from the site. First visit seeds about ten sample notes (the site screenshots' curated workspace plus a welcome note and a formatting tour) under a Private and a Public root; Reset demo empties the demo's database and seeds again. Opens light until the visitor chooses. The banner is catalogue components in the account corner, tokens only: three small Buttons, *Download* (primary), *Reset demo* (secondary) and *Demo: notes stay in this browser* (subtle). A fresh start (the first visit, or the first after Reset demo) opens the welcome note at its top with the caret at the start of its title. Save to GitHub, sharing and the vault shown with explanations are queued. Tests: `apps/web/test/demo.test.ts`, journey `apps/web/e2e/demo.spec.ts` (persist after reload, Reset demo, no other origin, axe light and dark, 390 px). | Accepted (owner, 16 Oct: "The demo should be live") | [Product site and help → The demo](../product/site-and-help.md#the-demo) (WP-10.6, HLP-08, PRV-01, SHR-01) |
| D-178 | **PRF-14's control-response journey measures a warm runner: one throwaway launch and round of first uses come before the measured launch (DEF-24; amends D-151 (13)).** On CI the first app launches on a fresh Windows runner pay for cold OS disk and font caches. The Long Animation Frames trace showed every spike as one frame with no script attributed and almost no rendering (288 ms with 235 ms blocking; 70 ms with 6 ms blocking), and later launches in the same job had nothing over 29 ms. So `m5-snappy.spec.ts`'s input-to-next-paint test passed or failed by where the shard split put it: first in journeys-windows (4) it failed (56 to 296 ms, main with test-only changes included, 72 ms); sixth or seventh it passed (40 to 48 ms). The test now launches once, uses the toolbar, Files, Spotlight and Settings once, logs those numbers as `[D-178] cold`, holds them to a loose ceiling (input to next paint p95 ≤ 300 ms over the cold round, about three times the 56 to 120 ms cold first uses CI showed; the one 296 ms outlier was a shard-first run of the same kind) so a real cold-start regression fails the test while a fresh runner's variance passes, quits, and then measures exactly as before: a fresh launch with the warm-up on (FOCUS_NOTES_WARMUP=1), first uses through fifth. The gated budget is unchanged: input to next paint p95 ≤ 50 ms. A real budget for the cold first launch (PRF-10's territory) is a separate, later decision; the 300 ms ceiling is a regression check, not that budget (throughput review, 2026-10-16). | Accepted (orchestrator, 2026-10-16) | [Performance and quality](../architecture/performance-and-quality.md) (PRF-14, PRF-10); D-151 (13); [Gate evidence → DEF-24](gate-evidence.md) |
| D-179 | **Multiple windows, first slice: a note in a window of its own (WP-8.5, DSK-06, UC-CANVAS-04, proposed).** (1) **Opening:** Spotlight's alternative action (<kbd>Mod</kbd>+<kbd>Enter</kbd>) on a note, a heading, or a match opens that note in a new window; so do the quick action *Open in new window* (available on the desktop, saying why elsewhere) and the command `note.openInNewWindow` for the open note (saved first). The window it was opened from keeps its note. *New window* (<kbd>Mod</kbd>+<kbd>Shift</kbd>+<kbd>N</kbd>), *Reopen closed note*, and *Close note / window* stay reserved for the next slice. (2) **The window:** created by the same path as the first (`createWindow`), so it has the same hardened preferences, shows on its first themed frame and never before it was painted (DSK-09, D-149), and is drawn by the app with its own frame: `AppWindowFrame` keeps a `CustomWindow` (or macOS title bar) per window, keyed by its page, and every window channel (drag, snap, minimize, maximize, keys, pointer, state) reaches only the sender's own. It opens cascaded 32 px right and down from the window it came from, its normal size, back at the work area's corner when it would leave it (`cascadePlacement`). (3) **State per window:** the page is given its note (`note=` in its address) and opens it rather than the last one; it never changes which note the app reopens at launch (the core drops `lastNotePath` for such a window, and the main process keeps the file's when it saves `state.json`, `stateFromWindow`). Everything else in `state.json` is shared, last writer wins, as before. (4) **Every window hears** sync's state and changes on disk, so a window with the same note open reloads it, or keeps its edits and asks (D-92); and saves go through the DEF-24 (renumbered DEF-26 on main) check, so two windows on one note never write over each other: the second to save is told the note changed. (5) **Closing:** each window flushes its own note before it closes (its own `app:flushed` answer); a workspace switch closes the windows opened for notes and restarts the first; the first closing with others open hands its role (setup, links, the first run) to the next. Per-window listeners (scheme, compositing) are removed when a window closes. (6) **Not yet:** remembering which windows were open across launches, Mod+Shift+N, and the macOS Window menu. | Proposed | [Desktop app](../experience/desktop-app.html), [Desktop and Git](../architecture/desktop-and-git.md), [Spotlight](../experience/spotlight.html) |
| D-180 | **Gates match what changed; publishing the owner asked for is never held by unrelated desktop defects (the owner: "I explicitly wanted the site live, there's no prerequisite for the app to be perfect before that"; amends D-113).** (1) **Why:** asked at 15:27 to put the site live, it went through four full Windows desktop CI cycles (about 40 minutes each), and each failed on a different desktop defect already on main (PRF-14's cold runner, DEF-24 → DEF-26's save race, DEF-25's slash menu, DEF-27's table save), none of them in the site. The site and web checks were green throughout; it went live at 17:40, more than two hours late. (2) **Site, docs and web-only changes** (`site/`, `tools/site/`, `documentations/`, `apps/web/`, the site-only components) merge on `pnpm check`, typecheck, unit tests, the site and web journeys and, for anything published, a security review; the desktop journeys don't gate them. (3) **Desktop and shared app code** keep the full Windows gate (D-113); a defect found there that's also on main is quarantined explicitly with scope, owner and expiry, not used to hold unrelated work. (4) **The go-live commit** `e9fb359` (site, demo, the app-instance rule, site CSS scoped out of the app, D-178) fast-forwarded main with its site and web checks and three of four Windows journey shards green; the fourth failed only `m1-tables.spec.ts:108` (DEF-27), which it doesn't touch. (5) **CI to follow:** a path filter in `ci.yml` so a site-only change runs only the site and web jobs. | Accepted (owner, 16 Oct) | [AGENTS.md](../../AGENTS.md), [Gate evidence → CI stability](gate-evidence.md#ci-stability-def-13-to-def-27), D-113, D-152 |
| D-181 | **Help is a section of the Slate docs (owner, 2026-10-16; amends D-167 and D-168).** The owner: "We already use Slate for documentation, why create another system for the help center? Help is just another section in the overall documentation." (1) **Where:** `documentations/help/`, a Help page and one page per section (the eight from D-168), Slate pages like every other: one H1, a TL;DR, registered in `docs-manifest.json`, in the writing guide's voice. Its address is `/documentations/#help/index.md`. (2) **The site's own help system is gone:** the helpHome, helpCategory and helpArticle views, their view models, `site/help/` and their tests. The site keeps the landing, download, privacy and demo pages. (3) **`/help/` redirects:** the builder writes `/help/` and `/help/<section>/` as small pages with a meta refresh and a plain link to the docs' pages (no script), from the docs' manifest; the site's *Help* and *Learn how it works* links go straight there, and the site check confirms every docs page a link names exists. (4) **Learn more links** (WP-10.7) resolve to the docs' Help pages (`helpDocsPath`). (5) Search is the docs' own. | Accepted (owner, 16 Oct) | [Product site and help](../product/site-and-help.md#the-help-centre), [Help](../help/index.md), [Writing help articles](../design/help-writing.md) (HLP-02, HLP-03) |
| D-182 | **Tables as the owner asked (proposed; built in slices).** (1) **A table fills the text column (Fit to column):** `width: 100%` and `table-layout: fixed`, so a new table has equal columns that span the column, as in Microsoft Loop; text wraps in its cells. Measured locally, a 200 × 10 table whose every cell wraps types at p95 about 60 ms against 30 to 40 ms before (narrower columns wrap more); typing elsewhere is unchanged. (2) **Insert on any inner border:** within a 12 px band centred on an inner column or row border (`table.insertZone`), anywhere along it or on the handles' side, the border highlights at once (no 150 ms wait) with the + at its top end (columns) or left end (rows). A press on the + inserts in that frame (PRF-14); a click without moving on the highlighted line inserts too, through a 4 px hit band (`table.insertLineHit`) so a click near a cell's edge still places the caret; the keyboard keeps the menus' insert commands. The controls are fixed over the note, so hovering shifts nothing. The pointer moving onto the controls keeps the offered border. The cell menu's anchor is kept inside the room popovers have (the window less their padding), so the menu never runs past a small window's edge (D-111). (3) **Resizing a column:** a drag on the border between two column headers (cursor `col-resize`, over the insert zone's width) resizes the column on its left, drawn in the frame of each move through the CSS object model and saved once on release (one undo step), never under 4 characters (`COLUMN_MIN_CH`); Fit to column, its right-hand neighbour gives or takes the difference; Bleed to edge, the table grows. Below the header row the highlighted border's drag resizes too and its click inserts; on the header row a click inserts nothing, so a double-click there is unambiguous: it resets the column to its aim, its longest line held to 30 to 75 characters. Esc during a drag puts it back. Keyboard: Alt+Shift+→/← widen and narrow the caret's column by 2 characters (in a table they share the key with Expand and Shrink selection, which keep it elsewhere; the editor keymap now reads modifiers in one order, so the two never shadow each other), and *Widen column*, *Narrow column*, *Reset column width* in the column and cell menus. Widths follow their columns through inserts (a new column takes its neighbour's width), moves, and deletes. (4) **Fit to column / Bleed to edge:** a table attribute; Bleed widens the table's frame to the note's area (`100cqi` of the scroller, less a pinned Files sheet and the gutters), centred on the text column, with the table `max-content`, columns aiming for at most 75 characters (`table.bleedMeasure`), so it's never stretched; with every width set, it's their sum. Switched from the table and cell menus (checked) and two commands. (5) **In the Markdown:** an HTML comment on the line before the table, `<!-- table: layout=bleed widths=24,40,- -->` (widths in characters of the note's font, `-` for none), written only when not the default; it's invisible on GitHub, survives other editors as a comment, and a comment that isn't on the line before a table is left as it is. Corpus `27-table-layout-widths.md`. (6) **Where:** the table's node view, colgroup and widths are the design system's (`table-view.ts`); the editor keeps the attributes, the commands, and the Markdown. | Proposed | [Tables](../design/components/tables.html) |
| D-183 | **Spacing shows what belongs together (owner, 2026-10-16: "Paragraph spacing should be to indicate what belongs together, for proper visual chunking"; "spacing between paragraphs should never be lesser than line height"; amends D-78).** In the note's em, so it scales with Mod+= and Mod+-, the same in the editor and on published pages: (1) **between blocks** (paragraphs, lists, quotes, tables, code) the paragraph gap, `font.blockGapLines` lines of the note's text, never less than one (Compact 1, Comfortable 1.25, Airy 1.5; at line height 1.5, Comfortable is 1.875 em, up from 1.5 em); (2) **before a heading** the gap plus more the bigger the heading (h1 2.0 em more, then 1.5, 1.0, 0.75, 0.5, 0.25), so the space before each level is larger than the next level's and larger than the gap, and two paragraphs are never further apart than two headings; (3) **after a heading** 0.35 em (`layout.headingAfter`), the smallest space of all, so what follows reads as nested under it; (4) nothing above the first block. Applied with adjacent-sibling rules in EditorSurface and NoteContent; each heading's margin is computed in the note's em. `layout.blockGap` (a fixed em) is replaced by `font.blockGapLines`. A unit test holds the invariants for every spacing setting and every line height the Reading tab allows. | Accepted (owner) | [Foundations → Type](../design/foundations.md), [Tokens](../design/system/tokens.md), [Theme manager → Reading](../design/views/theme-manager.html) |
| D-184 | **Downloads are the landing page's last section, and a plain version is the latest release (owner, 2026-10-16: "No need for a separate page"; "The download detail can be at the bottom of the page"; "The releases page should be updated with the latest build"; amends D-167 and D-128).** (1) **No download page:** `site/download.md` is gone; the landing page ends with a `download` section (an eyebrow, a headline, a tile per system with its file, *Download from GitHub* to `releases/latest`, and a caption that the builds aren't signed yet and list their checksums). Every *Download* on the site, in the navigation, the hero, the closing and the demo's banner, goes to `#download`; the old `/download/` redirects there. (2) **Latest release:** GitHub's `releases/latest`, which the site, the app's About and the READMEs link to, skips pre-releases, so while every release was one it was a 404. `release.yml` now drafts only suffixed versions (`v1.2.0-beta.1`) as pre-releases; a plain version, stage exits below 1.0.0 too, is a full release and becomes the latest. The rolling `preview` stays a pre-release (D-129). `v0.0.2`, from the newest green `main`, is the first. | Accepted (owner) | [Product site and help](../product/site-and-help.md), [Distribution](distribution.md) |
| D-185 | **A document minimap, modeled on Notion's floating table of contents (owner, 2026-10-17: "We need a doc minimap… indicator bars representing the headers in the doc… on the right side with clearance for the scrollbar. Current block is highlighted… Hovering on it expands it to a full chapter navigation", with Notion screenshots; amends UI-01's "only the document and the trigger").** (1) **When:** two or more top-level headings (Notion's threshold), and room between the text column and the scrollbar for a 32 px rail plus 8 px and 16 px gaps; otherwise hidden, never covering text. It stays in focus mode, which is on by default (a first draft hid it there, which would have hidden it from nearly everyone). (2) **Rail:** one 2 px bar per heading, evenly spaced (16 px, shrinking to fit 70% of the visible height, down to 4 px, then sampled with the current one always drawn), right-aligned, 20/16/12/8 px by depth below the shallowest heading; muted, the current one in the text color. (3) **Current section:** the caret's while the note has focus and the caret is in view; otherwise the last heading at or above the reading zone's top line (UI-11); at the end of the note, the last heading in view; above the first heading, none. (4) **Card:** opens after 150 ms of hover in the rail's place without taking focus, closes 200 ms after the pointer leaves the rail and card, or on Esc (which then does nothing else), per WCAG 1.4.13; lists every heading indented 12 px per level, two lines at most, the current one accented and `aria-current="location"`. (5) **Jump:** caret to the heading's start, focus in the note, the heading scrolled to the reading zone's top in one step, as Spotlight's Go to heading does; no smooth scroll, which fights the reading zone and paints every page of a long note. (6) **Keyboard:** *Show contents* (Spotlight; macOS Go menu) opens the card with focus on the current heading; arrows, Home, End, Enter, Space, Esc, Tab. (7) **Semantics:** a `nav` landmark named Contents with a disclosure button and a list of buttons (APG disclosure navigation); buttons rather than links because a choice moves the caret and the editor has no anchors. (8) **Performance:** headings read 150 ms after the last change, layout only on scroll, resize and opening; nothing at rest. Nothing is stored in the note; a setting to turn it off is a follow-up if wanted. Built as WP-8.8 (UI-13). **Amended by the owner, 17 October:** scrolling with the wheel or the scrollbar makes the heading in view current even with the caret still in view, until the caret moves again; and a jump glides over 200 ms with the reading zone's own scroll animator (D-156, at once with reduced motion), the caret aimed just inside the band so the reading zone doesn't cut it short, instead of one instant step. | Proposed | [Document minimap](../design/components/document-minimap.html), [Requirements](../product/requirements.md), [Main UI](../experience/main-ui.html) |
