| D-186 | **The document minimap's card can be pinned open (the owner: "Have an option to pin the expanded minimap", proposed).** (1) **The control,** as pinned Files (D-161): a pin `IconButton` on the contents card's first row, at the end of the note's first heading (whose text stays clear of it) (`pin`, then `pinOff`; name and tooltip *Keep contents open*, then *Unpin contents*; `aria-pressed`), and the command `outline.togglePin` (*Keep contents open*, checked when on) in Spotlight, the ⋯ menu's View group and the macOS Go menu; no default shortcut. A pointer press on the pin leaves the caret in the note. (2) **Pinned is the same card,** in the rail's place (the rail goes), never a docked sidebar: shown while the note has two or more headings at its own width (the same as unpinned; pinning never resizes it, the owner, 17 October), and **it keeps a column** (the owner, 17 October: "the vertical column is reserved"): the text column is laid out left of it, as beside pinned Files (D-161), gliding over `motion.sheet` with the caret's line held; never under it. Only where the text would be narrower than `pinnedColumnMin` does the rail come back. Without the room, the rail and its hover card return, the pin still pressed. (3) **It stays** through the pointer leaving, Esc (which returns the caret to the note, as in pinned Files), a jump to a heading, and another note opening; the current heading follows the caret and the scroll, the list scrolling only to keep it in view. In the tab order after the note; *Show contents* focuses its current heading. (4) **Unpinning** from the card turns it into a hover card that closes as the pointer leaves (or on Esc or Tab with focus in it). (5) **Kept per device** in `state.json` (`outlinePinned`). | Proposed | [Document minimap](../design/components/document-minimap.html), [Requirements](../product/requirements.md), D-161, D-185 |
| D-187 | **Files and the contents dock rather than pin (the owner, 17 October: "change the concept from pinning to docking where the panels get flushed to the left and right edge giving clear visual indication that the available viewport space in the middle is for the file content"; built).** Supersedes D-161 (2) and (3) ("pinned is the same sheet … never docked") and D-186 (2). (1) **Docked:** Files flush to the window's left edge, the contents flush to its right, each from the window's top edge to its bottom (the owner, 17 October: "edge to edge till top edge"), their content starting below the title band (`layout.titleBar`, 44 px), which stays the window's drag region; the window's minimize, maximize and close stay shown at the top of the docked contents, on it; opaque `color.surface`, no shadow or float inset, a hairline on the inner side, the window's own corner rounding clipping them. The note's area, its scrollbar with it, lies between them (the scroller's margins, `pinnedClearVar` and `pinnedRightVar`), the text column centred in it; docking and undocking glide over `motion.sheet` with the caret's line held, as D-161 did. (2) **Resizable** from the inner edge: a `separator` handle (`Resize Files`, `Resize contents`), dragged, or focused and moved with ← → (Shift: four steps of 16 px), Home and End; a double-click resets. Widths are kept per device (`filesWidth`, `outlineWidth`). (3) **Minimums (pragmatic choices, the owner unavailable to confirm):** each panel 200 to 480 px, default 280 (`layout.dockFiles`, `layout.dockOutline`, `layout.dockMin`, `layout.dockMax`); the note's area at least 400 px, gutters included (`layout.dockContentMin`). A panel can't be resized past what leaves the note its minimum. (4) **Narrowing:** where the window can't hold both panels and the note's minimum, Files undocks first, then the contents; an undocked panel behaves as it does undocked (Files closes, and opens as a floating sheet that closes as a note opens; the contents go back to the rail and its hover card), its docking kept. Widening docks them again, Files reopening without taking the caret. The canvas arbitrates (`allocateDocks`), telling core whether Files fits (`files.setFits`). (5) The controls keep their names in code (`files.togglePin`, `outline.togglePin`) and read *Dock Files* / *Undock Files* and *Dock contents* / *Undock contents*. | Proposed | [Files](../design/views/files.html), [Document minimap](../design/components/document-minimap.html), [Requirements](../product/requirements.md), D-161, D-185, D-186 |
| D-188 | **Files says where the notes come from (the owner, 17 October: "in the draggable area we should show the name of the repo and the github username currently active… a way to open the local folder… After a separator, the rest of the files browsing experience").** (1) **First in Files, above a hairline:** GitHub's mark, the notes repository's name (`owner/name` when the signed-in account doesn't own it), and `@login` below it in muted caption type; a folder without a GitHub repository shows the folder's name with a folder icon, and the browser, which has no local folder, shows nothing. (2) **Docked, it leads from the window's top edge with the sheet's padding above and below it** (the owner: "We need more padding around the top header area… We don't have to fit it in the titlebar area"); the part in the title band stays the window's drag region: the text drags the window, its button doesn't; on macOS it starts clear of the traffic lights (`window.trafficLightRoom`). (3) **One icon button, Open local folder**, opens the workspace's folder in Explorer or Finder (`files.revealFolder`, the shell's `workspace:reveal`, nothing else). (4) The repository is read from the clone's own `.git/config` origin, with no Git process and no network, in `workspace:info`. The Sheet gained a `band` slot. | Proposed | [Files](../design/views/files.html) |
| D-189 | **Ways out and links in Files, and the link editor gives way (the owner, 17 October).** (1) **The link editor closes when you click elsewhere in the note** ("if I click elsewhere on another text, the add-link popover should be dismissed"): a press in the note area outside the editor runs `link.dismiss`, which only closes it, so the click places the caret or starts a selection as it would anyway; Esc and Cancel are unchanged. (2) **Recently deleted has an obvious way back** ("it's not obvious how to get back to files"): while the list shows, the footer reads *Browse files* with a folder icon (a plain button whose words say where it goes, not a pressed toggle whose name changes), and the header's *Files* becomes a back chevron and a button, still the sheet's h2, so the list's day headings keep their outline. (3) **The repository's name opens it on GitHub, and @login the account's profile**, through the shell as every web link (`files.openRepo`, `files.openProfile`); where nothing can open links (the browser without a shell), they stay plain text. Text and Heading gained an optional `press` event: a button that still reads as the text, with the controls' focus ring; Text is underlined on hover, a Heading only muted, because a heading is never underlined (the owner, 25 October: "Do not use underline for heading"). | Proposed | [Files](../design/views/files.html), [Text formatting](../design/components/text-formatting.html) |
| D-190 | **Crash recovery is isolated from the workspace (owner, 2026-10-18: "We need better crash recovery logic, it should be isolated also so that it doesn't mess up existing files and folder structure"; amends D-105 (3), proposed).** After the recovery journal recreated four notes under names held during title renames, and committed them. (1) **Rule:** recovery never creates, writes, renames, moves or deletes a file or folder in the workspace by itself; only the person's save (with D-92's check) or a choice writes recovered text. (2) **Identity:** each open note has a draft ID (a ULID) and every handover a sequence number; the path, front-matter `id`, base hash, file ID and Git `HEAD` are fields of the draft, updated in the same queue on every rename. (3) **Store:** one atomic draft file per note in `<app data>/recovery/<workspace key>/`, replacing `recovery-journal.jsonl`, which is migrated once and deleted; SQLite was weighed and left until drafts need queries. (4) **Launch** matches each draft, reading only: same path as it was, then front-matter `id`, file ID, Git renames, a unique content match; matched notes open with the text as unsaved edits, changed ones open the resolver, and the rest wait in Files → Recovered for 30 days (200 MB at most). (5) Private notes' drafts are encrypted with `safeStorage`. (6) Benchmarked against VS Code, Zed, JetBrains, Obsidian, Word, macOS, Sublime Text, Vim, Emacs and Logseq (18 October). (7) A hotfix first: restore never creates a file, late text follows a rename, and file names drop `&#x20;`. Accepted 25 October (the owner: "Build the complete crash recovery spec"), after the journal recreated notes again on v1.0.0 (DEF-34). | Accepted | [Crash recovery](../architecture/crash-recovery.md), [Performance and quality](../architecture/performance-and-quality.md#reliability), [Requirements](../product/requirements.md) (QLT-06), [Implementation plan](implementation-plan.md) (WP-8.9) |
| D-191 | **Backup by repository on Files' section headings (the owner, 18 October).** "Folder creation and file creations are another aspect that needs to be backed up … we can create folders with no files in it and we want to save that." Private and Public in Files are each their own repository (the `private/` submodule and the notes repository), so each section's heading row carries, at its trailing end, that repository's backup icon: a real button with the per-note icon's looks and states (waiting, backing up, backed up, offline, problem, D-132), named "Private notes backup: Backed up at 10:36 AM", its own tab stop, pressed on pointer-down. It opens a card modeled on the per-note one: the section's name, its state, the repository (owner/name when it's on GitHub), the last backup, what waits in counts ("2 folders, 1 note waiting for backup"), and **Back up to GitHub**. **What waits** is counted in the repository's `notes/` folder only: files not committed (whoever made them), folders with nothing in them (Git can't see those, so each one counts), and what commits not pushed yet changed. **Back up to GitHub** gives every empty folder a `.gitkeep`, commits everything waiting in `notes/` as one commit (`Create folder notes/Empty`, or `Back up 2 folders and 1 note` with each change listed), then runs the app's own backup, so private is pushed before the pointer moves (D-07). It runs through the task tracker as `repoBackup.<repo>` (waiting class, measured as `focus-notes:repoBackup.private` and `.public`), says *Backing up your changes to GitHub* in the card while it runs, announces *Public notes backed up*, and toasts a problem. **Findings:** a folder made in the app was already committed (`Create folder …`, its `.gitkeep`) and pushed in the background with its Files batch (FIL-05, D-97); what was never backed up were folders and files made outside the app (Explorer, Finder, agents), because the app commits only what it wrote, and empty folders, which Git can't see. The manual backup is the person's consent to commit those; it never touches files outside `notes/` (a README, code someone keeps there). Absent where there's no Git: the browser app and folders that aren't repositories show no icon. | Proposed | [Files](../design/views/files.html), [Feedback and dialogs](../design/components/feedback-and-dialogs.html), [Perceived performance](../architecture/perceived-performance.html), [Requirements](../product/requirements.md) |
| D-192 | **Open containing folder in the Files row menu (owner, 2026-10-18: "Right clicking a file or folder should also give the option of \"Open containing folder\""; builds the planned *Show in Finder / Explorer*).** (1) **Named as the owner asked**, the same on every system: *Open containing folder*. (2) **Where:** the row menu of a note (after Open), a folder (after New note and New folder), and a section, read-only places included; not for a selection of several. <kbd>Shift</kbd>+<kbd>Alt</kbd>+<kbd>R</kbd> (<kbd>⌥⌘R</kbd> on macOS, the Pro layer, as in VS Code) anywhere in the Files panel acts on the focused row. (3) **What it does:** the main process checks the path is inside one of the workspace's roots as the file system resolves it (no control characters, dot folders, symbolic links or short names leading elsewhere) and still exists, then `shell.showItemInFolder`: the folder that holds it opens in Explorer or Finder with it selected; a section shows its notes folder. **Revised (owner, 2026-10-18: "Open containing folder is only for files; Open in File Explorer or Open in Finder, and the equivalent for Linux, should be for folders"):** a note keeps *Open containing folder*; a folder or a section gets *Open in File Explorer* (Windows), *Open in Finder* (macOS) or *Open in file manager* (Linux), `files.openFolderItem` over `workspace.openFolder`, which checks the path the same way, refuses anything but a directory, and opens it with `shell.openPath`. The one key acts on whichever the focused row is. Gone since: a toast, "Couldn't open the folder. It may have moved or been deleted." (4) **Desktop only:** the bridge's `workspace.revealItem` exists only there, so the browser has no item. (5) **Copy, Copy as path, Copy text** (owner, 18 October: "Copy text content (For files)", "Copy as path", "Copy file/folder (This is the same as file explorer/finder copy)"): *Copy* puts the selected notes and folders on the system's clipboard as files (<kbd>Ctrl+C</kbd> / <kbd>⌘C</kbd> in the tree, as in Explorer and Finder; ⌘C then ⌥⌘V stays the planned move), through each system's own tool because Electron's clipboard holds no file list: `Set-Clipboard -LiteralPath` in PowerShell on Windows, AppKit's pasteboard through `osascript` on macOS, GNOME's `x-special/gnome-copied-files` on Linux; each tool started by absolute path (D-166 (5)), the paths in an environment variable, never in the command, at most 32,000 characters (a Windows environment variable's limit); a section isn't copied whole; through the task tracker ("Copying…"). *Copy as path* (<kbd>Shift+Alt+C</kbd> / <kbd>⌥⌘C</kbd>, VS Code's keys) copies full paths, one per line, without quotes. *Copy text* copies one note's Markdown without the app's `id` line, the open note's from the editor, unsaved edits included; it works in the browser too. `clipboard:write` now takes up to 4 million characters, a note's length. Journeys copy into the memory clipboard. | Proposed | [Files → Row menu](../design/views/files.html#row-menu), [Keyboard shortcuts](../experience/keyboard-shortcuts.md) |
| D-193 | **Right-clicking text on the desktop opens the OS's menu, with the OS spell checker's suggestions (the owner, 18 October: "Dictionary spelling errors are highlighted but I'm unable to right click or act on them… Use OS provided one"; amends DD-FB-05).** Misspelled words were underlined, but Electron shows no menu of its own and the shell handled none, so a right-click did nothing. (1) **The menu:** a right-click in the note or any editable text opens the OS's native menu: up to six suggestions, *Add to dictionary* (the OS's own dictionary, through the session), then Cut, Copy, Paste and Select all as Electron roles; selected read-only text gets Copy. The app's own menus (the title bar band, Files rows, table cells) still win, and a key (Shift+F10, the Menu key) still opens the block menu. (2) **Windows:** Electron 41 and later underline words but report none to `context-menu` (electron/electron#54729; `webFrame`'s checks have no dictionary there, #28684), so the preload names the word under the pointer and the shell asks Windows' Spell Checking API (`ISpellChecker`, the same checker and dictionary that underline it) through one hidden Windows PowerShell helper, started in the background after the window shows. Its script comes on its input, never from a file, and answers take a few milliseconds; until it's ready, or if it can't start, the menu shows the edit actions alone, never a wait. A chosen suggestion is typed over the word, so it saves and undoes like typing. Remove the helper when Electron's fix lands (electron/electron#52085). (3) **Not covered:** a misspelled word in a table cell (the table's menu wins), menus opened by key, plain inputs (edit actions only) and Linux (Chromium's dictionaries download, which the app blocks). (4) Journeys read the menu from the log, never opening it over the owner's screen (D-141). (5) **The helper starts only when no Git command is starting or running** (`whenGitIdle` in `git/run.ts`): Git runs on a worker thread (D-151 (5)), and on Windows a process started on another thread at the same moment can inherit a Git command's pipes. The long-lived helper then held Git's output open, the call never finished, and its repository's queue stalled: Resolve opened nothing, and pushes never came (`m3-conflicts`, `m5-sharing`). As a backstop, a Git command's output is closed 2 s after it exits (`EXITED_PIPE_GRACE_MS`), so no inherited handle can stall sync. | Proposed | [Feedback and dialogs](../design/components/feedback-and-dialogs.md#context-menu) (DD-FB-05) |
| D-194 | **macOS and Linux get their own key names and words (the owner, 19 October: "make this work perfectly on Mac and Linux. Mind the shortcuts and UI text copy").** (1) **Keys on macOS** are drawn in the glyphs its own menus use, not in Windows words: <kbd>↩</kbd> Return, <kbd>⌫</kbd> Delete, <kbd>⌦</kbd> Forward Delete, <kbd>⇥</kbd> Tab, <kbd>↖</kbd> <kbd>↘</kbd> Home and End, <kbd>⇞</kbd> <kbd>⇟</kbd> Page Up and Page Down, and read aloud in words ("Command Delete"); Escape stays "Esc", as its key cap reads. Windows and Linux keep their words (Enter, Esc, Ctrl+Shift+N). Spotlight's footer names its keys the same way ("↩ to open · ⌘↩ for the alternative" on macOS), and the cheat sheet finds "cmd b" there. (2) **Git's install command** under *Having trouble?* is the system's own: `winget` on Windows, `xcode-select --install` on macOS, and on Linux `sudo apt install git` under "Or install it with your package manager". Journeys expect each system's form. (3) **A menu item's words stay apart:** its label, shortcut and reason are separated by spaces that don't render, so the part a short window leaves showing is still words of its name (WCAG 2.5.3; axe flagged "Copy public link" on Linux, whose clipped reason fused "link" and "Only"). | Proposed | [Keyboard shortcuts](../experience/keyboard-shortcuts.md) (KEY-05) |
| D-195 | **The layout holds with every OS's UI font and window manager; journeys measure what the owner sees, not one OS's metrics (cross-platform CI, 19 October).** macOS and Linux journeys failed where the system UI font (SF, DejaVu Sans) is wider or its line box taller than Segoe UI, and where the CI window manager clamps windows. (1) Product: the segmented control's inline padding is `space.s` below 600 px, so "Works for" fits a 480 px window; the find bar's replace row and the Files and Find in notes sheets' header rows wrap instead of overflowing; unpinned from the dock, the contents card stays a hover card until the note has its width back (with a wider font the rail had no room while docked, so the card closed at once). (2) The visual-quality padding check measures text by its line box (the parent's line-height), not its glyphs, so a font's ascent and descent don't read as uneven padding; tolerance stays 1 px. (3) Journeys size windows from the measured reading column (70ch of the UI font) where the rail needs room; the step "without room the rail returns" is skipped where no window has room for the rail but not the dock (Linux's font). (4) Journeys that resize or cascade windows scale to the work area (macOS clamps a window's height to it), ask for full transparency where they check the blur, and run axe once exit animations are done. (5) Performance: the input-to-next-paint p95 of 20 uses is the nearest-rank 19th, not the 20th (the maximum); the web pages' INP counts event entries with an `interactionId` only, as the INP metric does. (6) `FOCUS_NOTES_E2E_UI_FONT` loads a given font as the UI font in journeys, to check another OS's metrics on Windows. | Proposed | [Performance and quality](../architecture/performance-and-quality.md) |
| D-196 | **macOS and Linux gate every merge, as Windows does (the owner, 19 October: "make this work perfectly on Mac and Linux"; amends D-112).** D-112 let macOS and Linux run and report in CI without failing the workflow (`continue-on-error`) until the Windows app was accepted. Both had failed for days unseen, while every run showed green. With the platform parity pass (D-194, D-195 and D-193's macOS journey), every journey passes on all three, so `ci.yml` drops `continue-on-error`: a failure on any system fails the run, and `main` fast-forwards only to a commit green on all three. Windows still runs first and its journeys stay sharded; the budgets still gate on Windows alone and report on macOS (runner start times swing too far to gate on). A failure that shows only on macOS or Linux is fixed or quarantined in [Gate evidence](gate-evidence.md), as for Windows (D-113). | Proposed | [Agentic loop](agentic-loop.html#throughput), [Gate evidence](gate-evidence.md) |
| D-197 | **The boundary check reads style files, style writes and token reads from the syntax tree, so token-only styling is enforced, not assumed (design-system audit DS-A01, 19 October).** The audit fed probes through `tools/check-boundaries.mjs` that it accepted: `padding: 13` and `borderWidth: 3` in a `.css.ts`, `transitionDuration: "1s"`, a plain `.css` file with a hex colour, `element.style.setProperty(…)` in app code, and another component's tokens read through `import { tokens as designTokens }`. The check now parses each file with TypeScript. (1) **Raw values:** in a `.css.ts`, every string and template part is checked for hex colours, colour functions, and non-zero lengths and durations in any CSS unit (`s` included); a bare non-zero number is rejected on any property except counts and flex ratios (`flexGrow`, `flexShrink`, `flex`, `order`, line clamps, grid lines). Zero, `1em`, percentages such as `100%` and `inset(50%)`, token references and `calc()` of tokens stay valid. (2) **Plain CSS:** handwritten `.css` is rejected everywhere; a file could be allowed only by listing it with its reason (none is). Generated token stylesheets are build output and not walked. (3) **Style writes outside `packages/ui`:** assigning to `.style` or any of its members (`cssText` included), `style.setProperty` and `removeProperty`, `Object.assign(el.style, …)`, `setAttribute("style", …)`, `setAttributeNS` and `toggleAttribute` of `style`, and `attributeStyleMap` writes. Reading a style stays allowed. Inside `packages/ui`, measured geometry, slider percentages and validated custom-theme colours written at run time stay allowed, as before. (4) **Token ownership:** every binding of the `tokens` export (aliases, namespace imports, variables, destructuring) resolves to a token path; a component reads only `cmp.<its own name>` in any of its files, a computed key is rejected as unverifiable, and style files keep the semantic-group and layout-group rules. The one existing site this flagged, `VersionPane`'s visually hidden label (`width: 1, height: 1`), now reads its own `srOnlySize` token (`{border.hairline}`, exactly 1 px in every mode), as `TextField`, `Shortcut` and `LiveRegion` do. `tools/test/check-boundaries.test.mjs` holds every probe as a failing case and the legitimate values as passing ones. | Proposed | [Design-system audit](../design/system/audit.md#ds-a01-p1-token-only-styling-is-not-fully-enforced) |
| D-198 | **Every declared state is covered, or is a named gap (design-system audit DS-A02).** A contract's `states` were documentation: `ui:check` read only enum values, skipped hosts, and accepted a state nothing reached. Now (1) a fixture says which states it renders (`covers`; one without it renders `rest`); (2) a contract can name the journeys that reach states no static fixture can (`journeys: [{ spec, covers }]`), and each spec must exist; (3) a state nothing reaches is listed in `uncovered` with its reason, reported as a known gap and never counted as coverage, and listing a state there that a fixture or journey covers fails; (4) a host names its journeys instead of being skipped. A state name that the contract doesn't declare fails, as does a declared state with no fixture, journey, or reason. Interaction-only states (hover, pressed, focus, transitions) are gaps until a journey checks them, not fixtures with pretend props. **List items too (DS-A04):** a list prop's read must hold its kind's items, not just any array. Each kind's item shape (`ITEM_SHAPES` in `packages/ui/src/items.ts`) is typed from the data types list props already use (`CommandItem`, `MenuSection`, `TreeItem`…), so it can't drift from them; `ui:check` requires the view model's item schema to declare every required field with its kind, and checks nested lists (a menu section's items are command items). A `string[]` bound to Toolbar's lists now fails, naming the field. View models that declared opaque `{ type: "object" }` items now declare them (note blocks share `BLOCK_LIST_SCHEMA`). | Proposed | [Design-system audit](../design/system/audit.md#ds-a02-p1-fixture-coverage-does-not-cover-declared-states), [check.ts](../../packages/ui-runtime/src/check.ts) |
| D-199 | **A component's props are checked against its contract at compile time (design-system audit DS-A05).** Registries were `Record<string, ComponentType<any>>`, so renaming a React prop or changing its type only failed when a view rendered. Now every registry (`registry.ts`, `reader.ts`, `share.ts`, `site.ts`) is built with `defineRegistry` ([register.ts](../../packages/ui/src/register.ts)), which derives each contract's runtime props (`ContractProps`): a message is the resolved `string` (never the authored `{ t }` reference), an enum is its value union, a token is its group's names, an icon is an `IconName`, a list is its kind's item shape, each event is an `on…` handler taking its payload (plus `value` when it binds), and slots are `children` or arrays. A component that lacks a contract prop, requires a prop its contract never sets, or types one differently fails `pnpm typecheck`, naming the keys; a type-level test proves a renamed or retyped Button prop breaks compilation. A contract can say `requiresId` when the component names its parts from its id (ResultList, FileTree), and `ui:check` requires the id. **One busy policy (DS-A06):** every control with a `busy` prop (Button, IconButton, ItemCard) uses the shared `useBusy` ([busy.ts](../../packages/ui/src/behaviors/busy.ts)), which keeps no timer or state: `busy` makes the control inert at once (`aria-disabled`, press ignored, so a repeated press does nothing) and sets `aria-busy` and `data-busy="on"`; its words are `busyLabel` when given; with `busyLabel` and no `busy` it is `data-busy="done"` (the tracker's done words, pressable again). The Spinner replaces the icon in the same cell (the control keeps its size), shown by the control's style only after its `cmp.*.busy.signDelay` token (`wait.signDelay`), so quick work shows nothing; a busy control is no longer dimmed (its sign is the Spinner and the words). Under reduced motion the timing is the same and the Spinner stands still. The tracker's mapping is `busyProps` ([tasks.ts](../../packages/core/src/tasks.ts)): busy from `ack`, words from `on`, done words while `done`, nothing after a failure, and the latest task for a target wins, so pressing again while it says done starts over. React Aria's buttons drop an `aria-busy` prop, so it never reached the DOM before; the policy sets it through a ref. | Proposed | [Design-system audit](../design/system/audit.md#ds-a05--p2--contracts-do-not-prove-implementation-prop-parity), [register.ts](../../packages/ui/src/register.ts) |
| D-200 | **The component catalog's implemented inventory is generated from the contracts, and every guidance row says whether it's implemented, composed, or planned (design-system audit DS-A07, 19 October).** The catalog page described 40 primitives, six hosts, 13 layouts, nine blocks, and 38 compounds and called itself complete; 44, four, 15, one, and three are registered, and the overview promised generated prop types, complete state fixtures, and pixel checks on every fixture. Now (1) `tools/component-inventory.mjs` writes the page's inventory (each component's tier, declared states, fixture count, and the states its contract lists as gaps) from `schemas/ui/catalog.v1.json` and `contracts.v1.json`, which `ui:check --verify` already keeps equal to the contracts; (2) each guidance row carries a status, *Implemented* (optionally *as a* another tier), *Composed*, or *Planned*, and `--check`, run by `pnpm check` (`check:generated`), fails when the block is stale, the TL;DR's counts differ, an *Implemented* row names something unregistered or in the wrong tier, a *Composed* or *Planned* row names a component registered in that tier, or a registered component has no *Implemented* row; (3) the human guidance (when to use, composition, accessibility) stays hand-written; (4) the overview's Enforcement table links each rule to its check, says what it doesn't cover, and labels what isn't enforced as planned: implementation prop parity (DS-A05), the shared busy protocol (DS-A06), and pixel comparison (DS-A03). `tools/test/component-inventory.test.mjs` holds each drift the audit found as a failing case. Generating the whole page was rejected: it would lose the guidance that makes the catalog useful. | Proposed | [Component catalog](../design/system/components.md#inventory), [Design system → Enforcement](../design/system/overview.html#enforcement), [Design-system audit](../design/system/audit.md#ds-a07-p2-the-narrative-catalog-and-enforcement-claims-have-drifted) |
| D-201 | **The desktop app carries the workspace template and pushes the instance's first commit itself (WP-5.10, DST-08; owner, 19 October: "the starting template can be packed as part of the app").** Amends D-98 (2) and (6). (1) **Why:** setup's *Copy the template* asked GitHub to generate the instance from `prabinpebam/focus-notes-workspace`, so every new instance depended on a repository in the owner's personal account being public, marked as a template, and current. (2) **Where it lives:** `apps/desktop/scripts/build-main.mjs` writes `out/main/workspace-template.json` beside the main process, inside the app's archive, from `templateResource` in `tools/release/template.mjs`: every file of the template as base64, sorted by path, with its version. A release sets `FOCUS_NOTES_TEMPLATE_ARCHIVE` to its own `focus-notes-workspace-<version>.tgz`, so the app carries exactly the template the release attaches, `.focus-notes/app.json` pinned to that web bundle's version and SHA-256; a development build reads `templates/workspace/` with its `0.0.0` placeholder, which the *Deploy app* workflow refuses. It's one JSON file in the archive, not an `extraResources` folder, so packaging can't drop `.gitignore` or `.gitattributes`, and development and packaged builds read the same path. The main process checks it before use (plain relative paths, no `..` or `.git`, no names equal ignoring case, `.focus-notes/workspace.json` present); a missing or bad one fails the step as *templateMissing* ("This copy of Focus Notes is missing its starter notes. Reinstall Focus Notes, then try again."). (3) **The step:** with no repository by that name, the app makes an **empty public repository** (`auto_init` off, so there's no README commit to merge), and when the repository has no `main` and no default branch head, writes the template into a scratch folder in the system's temp folder, commits it as *Start my Focus Notes* on `main` (`symbolic-ref`, not `init -b`, for older Git), pushes it, and deletes the folder. The step order is unchanged (account, instance, private, deploy key, download, link, vault, Pages, push), and after this step the instance looks as a generated one did, so download clones it as before and Pages is never turned on for an empty repository. It stays idempotent and retryable (D-98 (4)): a retry finds the repository and pushes again only while it's still empty; an empty repository of that name the person made is filled the same way; a repository with `.focus-notes/workspace.json` (made in the browser from the public template, or by an earlier run) is used as it is; anything else is *notInstance*. (4) **The `workflow` scope:** GitHub's generate API needed no scope for workflows, but a push that adds `.github/workflows/*` does. The app's own sign-in already asks for it (`gh auth login --scopes workflow`, Git Credential Manager's `repo gist workflow`). A classic token's scopes come back in `X-OAuth-Scopes`: when that header lists scopes without `workflow`, the step stops as **workflowScope** before making anything ("Your GitHub sign-in can't add workflows. Sign in again and allow them."); fine-grained and app tokens send no header, so nothing is blocked up front, and a push GitHub refuses with "refusing to allow … to create or update workflow" gets the same problem. Try again runs the step again after signing in. (5) **Unchanged:** the template guard (D-157) runs before the repository is made, again before the first push, and on every other route; the public template repository is still published by each release (D-137) for people who start in the browser, but setup no longer calls it, and `GitHubApi.generate` is unused. (6) **Rejected:** cloning the template into the download folder and making the first push part of *Save to GitHub* (Pages and the deploy key's secret would be set on an empty repository, and a stopped setup would leave one that isn't an instance); keeping generate with the bundle as a fallback (two paths to test, and the dependency stays). | Proposed | [Desktop and Git → Creating a workspace](../architecture/desktop-and-git.md#creating-a-workspace), [First run](../experience/first-run.md), [Distribution](distribution.md), [Requirements → DST-08](../product/requirements.md) |
| D-202 | **The desktop app is also offered in the Microsoft Store, as an MSIX package beside the NSIS installer (PKG-12, WP-5.11).** The owner asked for a real Store release; NSIS stays the direct download. (1) **Package**: electron-builder's `appx` target, a separate command (`pnpm --filter @focus-notes/desktop run dist:store`, `apps/desktop/scripts/dist-store.mjs`), never in `win.target`. Its file is named `.msix`, because Microsoft's `msstore` command line publishes only `.msix`, `.msixbundle` or `.msixupload` files. Its tile and logo images are drawn from the same source as `icon.ico` by `tools/release/store-assets.mjs`, which `pnpm check` keeps current. (2) **Identity in one place**: `build.appx` in `apps/desktop/package.json` holds obvious placeholders (`Placeholder.FocusNotes`, `CN=00000000-…`); the repository variables `MSSTORE_IDENTITY_NAME`, `MSSTORE_PUBLISHER` and `MSSTORE_PUBLISHER_DISPLAY_NAME` override them, and a release build refuses the placeholders (`--require-identity`). They come from Partner Center's Product identity and aren't secrets. (3) **Command line**: an app execution alias, `focus-notes.exe` (`build/appx-extensions.xml`), because the package's folder is read-only and moves with each version, so nothing can be added to `Path`. The alias passes the words as typed, so in the Store build a first argument that isn't a switch runs the command line (`cliArgv`, `store-build.ts`); `--cli` still works everywhere, and no argument opens the app. `isStoreBuild()` (`process.windowsStore`) is the one test: Settings → Agents shows *installed with the app* and no Install button, the agent snippets name the alias, and *Install* never touches `Path`. (4) **Links**: electron-builder declares `focus-notes:` in the manifest from `protocols`, so `setAsDefaultProtocolClient` doesn't run in the Store build. (5) **Data**: MSIX redirects the app's AppData writes to the package's private copy; the alias and the processes the app starts (`git`) share the package's identity and see the same copy, and uninstalling removes it. Notes live in the folder the user picks (outside the package), so they stay. Nothing else writes beside the executable: the audit found only the Path edit and the link registration. (6) **Building**: `release.yml`'s `store` job builds and checks the package on every release, with the identity from the variables and no secrets (PKG-04); without the variables, or for a version that starts with 0, it still builds and checks, as a short-lived artifact, never an asset: the Store refuses a package version whose first section is 0 (and keeps the fourth for itself), so Store releases start at 1.0.0, M5's version. CI packs and checks it on Windows too. (7) **Submitting**: a separate `store.yml`, run when a release is published (or by hand with a tag), in the `release` environment. When the secrets `MSSTORE_TENANT_ID`, `MSSTORE_CLIENT_ID`, `MSSTORE_CLIENT_SECRET`, `MSSTORE_SELLER_ID` and the variable `MSSTORE_PRODUCT_ID` exist, it downloads the release's `.msix`, checks it against `SHA256SUMS`, and submits it with Microsoft's `msstore` (`microsoft/microsoft-store-apppublisher`, pinned by SHA). Otherwise, and for pre-releases and 0.x releases, it stops with a notice. It's separate because `release.yml` holds no secrets, and it runs only once the owner has published the release. The Entra app is the owner's own publishing credential, used only by this workflow, never by the app or a user's instance, so PKG-06 still holds. (8) **Signing**: none. The Store signs MSIX packages itself; the package built here is unsigned and is for the Store only, not a download. (9) **The owner's steps**: the developer account, reserving the name, the first submission and the age rating stay manual, because the Store's API can't make a first submission. They're listed in [Microsoft Store](microsoft-store.md); the listing is generated from the product site (`store/`). (10) **The first Store release is v1.0.0, held to an indie release bar** (20 October; the owner: "we don't have to be extremely strict about everything; set an acceptable level that's suitable for an indie developer"). The bar is a checklist in [Stage gate evidence → Version 1.0 for the Microsoft Store](gate-evidence.md#version-1-0-for-the-microsoft-store): every requirement's tests and journeys green on all three systems, a way to report problems from the app, no design-system violations, the budgets on CI, no lost notes in the corpus and fault-injection journeys, the Electron and dependency checks with a security review of every network or release change, the privacy statement live, axe and keyboard journeys, the Store package and a release dry run, Microsoft's certification and a 10-minute smoke test by the owner, a complete listing, and a way to stop a bad release. The owner reviews it and decides whether it's v1.0. The rest of the [release definition of done](definition-of-done.md#release-definition-of-done) (the soak and the 1,000 fault-injection rounds, the moderated usability test, the screen-reader, international and install-matrix audits, the runbook rehearsal, approved baselines) applies from the next minor release. (11) **The Store's screenshots are generated, never taken by hand**: the `store` review catalogue runs the product site's scenes on CI at an exact 1920 × 1080 drawn window (D-141: a real window is resized only there), and `tools/release/store-screenshots.py` sets each, with its own shadow, on an opaque 2560 × 1440 backdrop, light behind a light window and dark behind a dark one, as the PNGs in `store/screenshots/`, numbered in the order `store/listing.json` names them. Rejected: replacing NSIS with MSIX, which would force the Store's sandbox and update path on every download; the msstore Electron configurator, which runs `npm install` against public registries; and submitting from `release.yml`, which would put secrets in it. | Proposed | [Microsoft Store](microsoft-store.md), [Distribution → What a release produces](distribution.md#what-a-release-produces), [Distribution → Signing](distribution.md#signing), PKG-12 |
| D-203 | **Send feedback: a problem or an idea becomes a GitHub issue from inside the app (WP-5.12, QLT-07; amends D-140 (7)).** The owner asked for feedback people can send without leaving the app, so problems reach him and get fixed. (1) **Where:** a *Send feedback* button pinned at the foot of Settings' category list, so it shows on every category, the one Settings opens on included (Tabs' new `footer` slot, right under the list; it closes Settings, and closing the dialog goes back there); ⋯ → *Send feedback…* after *Settings…*, on the desktop and in the browser; Spotlight (*bug*, *issue*, *problem*, *idea*, *suggestion*, *report*, *GitHub* find it); and the macOS Help menu's *Send Feedback…*. *Report a problem…* stays in About, Spotlight and the Help menu, and now opens the same dialog with Problem chosen. (2) **The dialog** (IA-VIEW-FEEDBACK) is a view document of its own, `feedback.view.json`, so the browser hosts it without Settings, in a new medium Dialog size (`m`, the 640 px DialogPanel already has): *Problem* or *Idea*; *Title* (required, at most 120 characters) and *Details* (at most 5,000), whose placeholders follow the kind; *Attach diagnostic report*, a toggle button rather than a checkbox (the catalog has no checkbox; Settings' switches are toggle buttons), on for a problem and off for an idea; whenever it's on, the report's preview is open below it: the exact redacted text that will be posted, read-only and scrolling, and the never-included line (*Preview* folds it, and opening it again builds it afresh); a profile card, the avatar and @login with *Posts publicly to prabinpebam/focus-notes*, or GitHub's mark with *Continues on GitHub* when signed out and in the browser; then *Cancel* and *Send*, or *Continue on GitHub*. Send is unavailable until the title has text and an attached report has been shown; Enter or Send before then says why (announced), and Title is marked required. While sending, Kind is read-only like the fields. Closed after being opened from Settings, by Esc, Cancel or Done, it gives focus back to Settings' *Send feedback*. (3) **Sending (the desktop, signed in):** the window sends only `{ kind, title, details, attachReport }`, checked strictly (types, lengths, the app's own page). The main process builds the issue for a **fixed** repository, never one from the window: the title as typed, trimmed; a body of the details, `---`, one line *Focus Notes ‹version› · ‹OS› ‹release› (‹arch›)*, the redacted report (D-140) in a `<details>` block only when attached, and then exactly the text the person last previewed, which the main process kept when it built the preview (none previewed, nothing attached goes), without the report's *Nothing was sent.*, and a hidden marker `<!-- focus-notes-feedback: kind=…; app=…; platform=… -->`; labels `bug` or `enhancement` and `from-app`, which GitHub drops for people who can't push. It posts with the signed-in token, which never leaves the main process, and answers only the issue's number and address, or a problem code. The address is shown only when it's an issue of that repository. It's a named wait (the tracker's `focus-notes:feedback.send` measure, as every tracked wait is named); the fields can't change meanwhile; closing the dialog doesn't stop it, and the result arrives as a toast. One send at a time, and 30 s after a success before the next, against double posts; 5 s after a failure before *Try again* goes (`wait`), and at most 5 issues an hour from one run of the app (`limit`, with *Continue on GitHub*). (4) **Failures:** offline, a timeout or a 5xx is `network` (*Try again*, the draft kept); 401 is `expired` (*Sign in again* through the account flow, or *Continue on GitHub*); 403, 404, 410, 422, rate limits and abuse detection are `refused` (*Continue on GitHub*). A fine-grained token that covers only the person's own repositories is refused here, which is why Continue is always offered. (5) **Continue on GitHub** opens `issues/new` with the title, the details and the summary line, and the labels. The address stays under 6,000 characters: longer details are cut with a note to paste the rest, and the whole details go on the clipboard. An attached report, the one previewed, goes on the clipboard, never in the address (D-140 (7)), with *Report copied. Paste it into the issue.* The dialog closes and keeps the draft. It's the only route when signed out and in the browser, which never writes to GitHub (D-80, PKG-05). (6) **The draft** stays in the window's memory while the app runs and is cleared after a send; it's never written to disk, so the data inventory has no new item for it. (7) **The issue's own words** (the summary line, the cut note, the marker) are fixed English for the maintainers who read it, as the report's are (D-140 (5)). (8) **Triage:** `.github/workflows/feedback-labels.yml` adds `bug` or `enhancement` and `from-app` to an issue opened with the marker, through `actions/github-script` pinned by SHA, with `issues: write` only, reading the body in JavaScript and never in a shell or a `${{ }}` expression. (9) **The preview is the report itself (amends D-140's "the window only gets the summary"):** an issue is public, so the person sees exactly what's posted before sending. Only this dialog's preview receives the redacted text, from `feedback:preview`; About keeps its six cards. The who-and-where card comes first, above Kind, so it stays in view with the preview open whatever the window's height. Rejected: About's six cards as the preview (what's posted could differ from what's seen); Attach off by default for a problem (the expanded preview keeps the dialog at VVS 85). Rejected: a feedback service or a token of the project's own (the person posts as themselves; nothing of the project's stands between them and GitHub, D-55); the report in the address (D-140 (7)); a checkbox component for one switch; a second Report a problem flow beside this one. | Proposed | [Secondary views → Send feedback](../design/views/secondary-views.md#send-feedback), [Security and privacy → Data inventory](../architecture/security-and-privacy.md#data-inventory), [Performance and quality → Diagnostics](../architecture/performance-and-quality.md#diagnostics), QLT-07 |
| D-204 | **The menu in text matches the theme on Windows and Linux, and has the OS menu's full set of actions everywhere (the owner, 20 October: "Is there a way to style the system provided menu so that it matches the theme", then "Do Option 2 and handle Mac OS properly" and "Make sure that we get the same set of functionality that the OS menu provides"; WP-5.13, EDT-14; amends D-193).** (1) **One model:** `packages/core/src/text-menu.ts` builds the items for every platform from Electron's `context-menu` params, so the drawn menu and macOS's stay in parity and are unit-tested per platform. (2) **Windows and Linux:** the app draws the menu (`TextMenu`, over `CommandMenu`) in the theme, every theme, light and dark, and forced colors. Main stays the authority: it sends the page a checked model (the place, at most 30 entries, at most 6 suggestions, no link address) and takes back an item id, once, from its own window; then it acts: replace or learn, `webContents` cut, copy, paste, `pasteAndMatchStyle`, delete and select-all, the editor's Undo and Redo (run as the app's `edit.undo`/`edit.redo`, so its history stays right; Chromium's own flags don't see it, so the page disables them from the editor's history), Windows' emoji panel, and *Copy link*/*Open link* (web and mail links only, through the app's guarded opening). Items, in order: suggestions (or *No suggestions*, disabled) and *Add to dictionary*; Undo, Redo; Cut, Copy, Paste, Paste as plain text, Delete; Select all; Copy link, Open link; Emoji (Windows). Each shows its real keys as plain text in the interface font, as every app menu and Windows' own menus do (*Ctrl+X*, *Delete*, *Win+.*, from the command registry's key names, so the spoken names match: *Win Period*); no key caps. An unavailable item's keys dim with it; in forced colors the focused item is in Highlight and HighlightText and unavailable ones in GrayText. Read-only text with a selection gets Copy alone. The menu answers within a frame: if Windows' spell checker hasn't answered by then, it opens with *Checking spelling…* and fills in; the suggestions are announced, and focus moves to the first if it was on the row they replaced. A right-click outside the open menu only closes it, as a system menu does; the selection goes back to the note, or to a field (Find, a rename) with its own start, end and direction. Main closes any open menu first on each right-click, numbers them and drops answers from any but the latest, keeps the misspelled word with the open menu, sends only suggestions the page accepts, and forgets the menu when the page reports it closed (`textMenu:closed` with its id). *Open link* and the page's links share one guarded opening (`openWebLink`). (3) **macOS:** the system's menu stays, in macOS's words: the suggestions (or *No Guesses Found*), *Learn Spelling*, *Look Up “word”*, Undo and Redo (the editor's history, always enabled: the native menu can't ask the page), Cut, Copy, Paste, *Paste and Match Style*, Delete, Select All, the link items, a *Speech* submenu and *Emoji & Symbols*. It follows the app's appearance: `nativeTheme.themeSource` is "light" or "dark" from Appearance, "system" when Appearance follows the OS (and the OS's changes still reach the page then). (4) **Left out:** *Ignore spelling*/*Ignore all* (Electron has no session-only ignore; adding and removing a word could leave it in the OS dictionary after a crash), the browser's items (search the web, print, inspect, translate, writing direction) and the Unicode and IME items of old Win32 edit controls (no clear use in a note), Select all in read-only text (it would select the whole window), Emoji on Linux (no system panel). (5) Keys still open the block menu (<kbd>Shift</kbd>+<kbd>F10</kbd>, the Menu key), and the app's own menus still win. (6) Journeys read the drawn menu in the page; on macOS they read the log (D-141); the emoji panel, the browser and the computer's clipboard and dictionary are stood in for. | Proposed | [Feedback and dialogs → Text menu](../design/components/feedback-and-dialogs.md#text-menu), EDT-14 |
| D-205 | **Work runs on three agent kits: Slate, agentic-craft and agent-orchestration (the owner, 24 October: "set up the other 2 also as sub module and let's update this project to be run with these kits. Resolve any conflicts").** (1) **Pinned:** `agentic-craft/` and `agent-orchestration/` join `slate/` as Git submodules at the root, at the commits Loushing lists (`299dbae`, `2064b11`). (2) **Installed, not edited:** each kit's installer copies its skills and agents into `.github/skills/` and `.github/agents/` and keeps a facts section in `AGENTS.md`. The copies belong to the kits; project specifics live only in the facts sections, and `node tools/agent-kits.mjs` (in `pnpm check`) fails when a copy differs from its pinned kit. CI doesn't fetch submodules, so the check skips there, like the Slate runtime check. (3) **Who owns what:** agentic-craft owns what to build and what success means; agent-orchestration owns how work runs; Slate owns the docs. The project's roles (orchestrator, builder, verifier, the reviewers, docs-keeper) stay, and load the kits' skills. (4) **Conflicts:** the project's rules win over the kits' general ones, in the order set in `AGENTS.md` → Agent kits. The chain, IDs and six design-system layers are the project's; attended and unattended map to *work with me* and *keep going unattended*; the CI merge gate stays (D-113, D-196); the agent may merge and publish as `AGENTS.md` allows. (5) **Replaced:** the project's `throughput-reviewer` becomes the kit's, which reads the same log and facts from `AGENTS.md`. The kit's `design-system-auditor` (tokens, layers, states, accessibility, copy) runs beside the project's `design-system-reviewer` (VVS, view documents, baselines). (6) **Fixed on the way:** the orchestrator no longer restarts the owner's app after a merge (D-122), and the verifier gates on all three platforms (D-196). | Accepted | [AGENTS.md](../../AGENTS.md), [The agentic loop → Roles](agentic-loop.html#roles), `.github/agents/`, `.github/skills/` |
| D-206 | **Changes that don't touch the desktop app never wait on desktop CI (the owner, 24 October: "Did you really need to run full e2e for [adding the kits]? You are wasting time and token … Be optimal about time, change config and hooks if you have to"; amends D-113, extends D-180).** Adding the kits sat about 90 minutes on three full CI runs, the journeys among them, and on fixes unrelated to the request, while the owner's prompts queued. (1) **CI decides by path:** a `changes` job in `ci.yml` turns on the desktop build, budgets and journeys only when something under `apps/desktop/`, `packages/`, `templates/`, `schemas/`, `tools/release|bench|dev/`, the root manifests or lockfile, `.npmrc`, `.nvmrc` or `ci.yml` changed. `pnpm check`, typecheck, unit tests and the web journeys always run. (2) **The agent decides by the same list:** docs, the site, `AGENTS.md`, agent files, skills, the kit submodules, and repository checks and their tests are committed straight to `main` after their fast local checks, with no work branch and no CI wait. Desktop and shared app code keep the merge gate (D-113, D-196). (3) **Unrelated red stays out of the task:** a new advisory or a flaky journey met on the way is reported in one line and queued, not fixed inside the task, unless it blocks landing it. | Accepted | [AGENTS.md → Working modes](../../AGENTS.md#working-modes-and-throughput-d-113), `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `.github/agents/verifier.agent.md` |
| D-207 | **One stacking order for the whole interface: menus and popovers open above the side panels (the owner, 24 October: "Menu and other popovers should go above the side panels. Do an audit of Z-index overall and have a framework and fix any Z-index issues").** The audit found the link editor and the format toolbar (`Positioner`, at `layer.contextual`) opening under the Files and Find all sheets (`layer.panel`); every menu, popover and picker (`MenuButton` and the cell, block, text and file menus built on it, `PopoverPanel`, `DatePicker`, Snap Layouts) tied with the sheets at `layer.panel` and sat under a dialog's scrim, so the share row's menu in Settings opened behind the dialog. The scale is now ten layers: canvas 0, mask 1, contextual 2, trigger 3, panel 4, **anchored 5** (new: surfaces pinned to the selection or caret, above every panel and below a modal dialog), dialog 6, **popover 7** (new: menus, popovers and pickers opened from any control, a dialog's included), toast 8, tooltip 9. The rule: a component takes its role's layer through its own `cmp.*.layer` token, never a number, and a surface sits above whatever it opens from. The Trigger's morph panel keeps `layer.panel`: it orders only inside the trigger's own layer. `tools/test/layers.test.mjs` enforces the order and the mapping; `check-boundaries` already rejects a raw `zIndex`. The `Layer` layout's variants are unchanged, since views don't place these surfaces themselves. | Accepted | [Tokens → Layer order](../design/system/tokens.md), `packages/tokens/src/semantic/structure.json`, `tools/test/layers.test.mjs` |
| D-208 | **Content on its way shows a shimmering placeholder in its shape (the owner, 24 October: "We need shimmer implementation for loading state, in menus, modals and dialogs and other UI where it's applicable").** A new `Skeleton` primitive (`shape` line, tile or avatar; `width` full, long, medium or short) replaces the still bars ItemCard drew for PRF-13. (1) **Motion:** a gradient overlay moves by `transform` only, `motion.shimmer` (1.5 s) per pass for `motion.shimmerPasses` (3) passes, so it stops within 5 s (WCAG 2.2.2); it runs the other way in right-to-left text. `motion.shimmer` is 0 with reduced motion and the overlay is removed; in forced colours the shape is a plain GrayText fill. (2) **Meaning:** a placeholder is always `aria-hidden`; the words that already said what's loading stay as the one status, and known headings stay visible. An empty or failed result replaces the placeholders. (3) **Where:** ItemCard skeletons (first run, the repository picker); the spelling menu's *Checking spelling…* row, through a `loading` flag on any menu item; Settings' account check, sharing, agents and About while they load, and the share dialog; Send feedback's report; the history sheet's first load (`vm.history.loading`); and Recently deleted. Indeterminate waits without a known shape keep Spinner or ProgressBar. `tools/test/shimmer.test.mjs` holds the timing, the reduced-motion value and the transform-only rule. | Accepted | [Perceived performance → Patterns](../architecture/perceived-performance.md), [Components](../design/system/components.md), `packages/ui/src/primitives/Skeleton/`, `packages/tokens/src/component/skeleton.json` |
| D-209 | **Journey windows on the owner's computer are invisible (the owner, 24 October: "Don't launch test window above other Windows").** Shown without activating (D-104), a journey's window still sat on top of the owner's apps. With `FOCUS_NOTES_E2E=1` and not on CI, every journey window (and the snap preview) is drawn at opacity 0: it takes no clicks, shows nothing, stays unoccluded so Chromium keeps its frames running, and `webContents.capturePage` still captures its content. CI, screen-level journeys (`FOCUS_NOTES_E2E_ALLOW_SCREEN=1`) and `FOCUS_NOTES_E2E_VISIBLE=1` (to watch a run) keep windows visible. | Accepted | `apps/desktop/src/main/window/journey-window.ts`, `apps/desktop/src/main/window/frame.ts`, [AGENTS.md](../../AGENTS.md) |
| D-210 | **Import files into a folder (the owner, 24 October: "We also need to have an import option that imports supported files like md, txt, docx, html etc. Import are available as an option in folder menu").** A folder's and a writable section's row menu offer **Import files…** after New note and New folder, on the desktop. (1) **The shell reads:** the main process shows the open dialog (several files: `.md`, `.markdown`, `.txt`, `.docx`, `.html`, `.htm`) and returns a handle; reading is a second call, so the *Importing…* sign shows only while files are read. A file over 10 MB, one it can't read, and any past 200 are left out and named in the toast. UTF-8 (with or without a byte order mark) and UTF-16 text are read. (2) **Conversion:** Markdown and text are kept as they are, with `\n` line ends and no front matter `id` (an import is a new note); text is read as Markdown, as a paste is. Word documents become HTML in the shell with mammoth (BSD-2-Clause), images dropped; HTML and Word's HTML become Markdown in the renderer with the paste pipeline (`htmlToMarkdown`: DOMPurify's paste allow-list, an inert document, the HTML reader), so scripts, styles, unsafe links and images never reach a note. (3) **Notes:** the title is the first H1, else the file's name, added as the H1; a taken file name is numbered (`planCreateNote`), nothing is replaced, and all the notes are one batch and one commit, selected, with Undo removing them. The bundle import (*Import notes…*, FRE-10) is unchanged. | Accepted | [Files](../design/views/files.html), [Requirements](../product/requirements.md) FIL-15, `apps/desktop/src/main/import-files.ts`, `packages/core/src/files-app.ts` |
| D-211 | **Windows only until v1 is in the Microsoft Store (the owner, 24 October: "I want the Windows thing first. I'm waiting to publish V1 at Microsoft store. Drop the Mac and Linux version for now").** Pauses D-196. CI's `test` job, the nightly build and the release's desktop installers run on Windows only; macOS and Linux each ran every journey in one 25–30 minute job, which set the pace of every merge, and their failures (D-204, DEF-31, DEF-32 on Linux) held Windows work back. Their steps stay in `ci.yml` and the code stays cross-platform, so bringing them back is adding the runners to the matrices. The merge gate is Windows: `test (windows-latest)` and the four journey shards. Revisit once v1.0.0 is published in the Store. | Accepted | `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `.github/workflows/release.yml`, `.github/workflows/nightly.yml`, [AGENTS.md](../../AGENTS.md), [Microsoft Store](microsoft-store.md) |
| D-212 | **A missing notes folder is never silently re-created (the owner, 24 October, after deleting his notes folder with the app closed: "I don't see any dialog or msg that says, can't find the folder … it has automatically created the folder with the same name again but with just the default content. It doesn't sync the content also").** At start the desktop app checks the folder named in its workspace record before Git, the watcher or any note. A missing folder, or one on a drive that isn't connected, opens the recovery view (IA-VIEW-RECOVER): what's missing, when it was last backed up, and *Download your notes again*, *Sign in to GitHub again*, *Find the folder…* and *Start with an empty folder*, each saying what it will do. Nothing is created or downloaded before a choice; a folder put back opens by itself. Only the first run makes a folder and its welcome note. The record (`workspace.json` in the app's data folder) keeps `version: 1` and gains optional fields: `kind` (`github`, `local`, `synced`), `remote`, `login`, `lastSync` and `syncService`; a record without them is inferred from the folder. | Proposed | [Where your notes live](../experience/notes-folder.md), [Requirements](../product/requirements.md) FRE-11, FRE-12, [Information architecture](../design/information-architecture.html) DD-IA-07 |
| D-213 | **A folder out of step with its notes on GitHub shows an alert and syncs (the owner, 24 October: "If the folder/repo is out of sync, it should indicate with an alert icon with detail card on hover and give you the option to sync manually or it should start syncing automatically. We can't have the folder automatically created and not synced with the remote content at all").** Writing never waits on an account or sync problem. An alert icon on the save state, the Files header and the affected section opens a card (on hover, focus or press) that says what differs and offers *Sync now*, *Sign in again* or *Switch account*. Sync starts by itself when nothing here could be lost: a folder holding only the untouched welcome note downloads the recorded notes. A folder with notes written there syncs on *Sync now*: the notes on GitHub are downloaded beside it, the person's notes are added as private notes (same name, numbered), every file is checked, and the old folder goes to the Recycle Bin. Backup pauses, with the alert, when more than 20 notes or more than half of them were removed outside the app. A folder with no notes on GitHub says "Offline mode · saved on this computer". The owner's case today (a folder with the recorded name, no `.git`, a known account) is inferred as out of sync. Settings → Account shows the account Git signs in with in every folder, fixing DEF-33. | Proposed | [Where your notes live → Out of sync](../experience/notes-folder.md#out-of-sync), [Requirements](../product/requirements.md) FRE-13, [Feedback and dialogs](../design/components/feedback-and-dialogs.md#save-and-sync-status) |
| D-214 | **People choose where the notes folder lives (the owner, 24 October: "User should also have the option of deciding where they want their local folder to be and it should not be hardbound to the documents folder automatically").** Setup's *Just use this computer for now* adds a *Where should your notes live?* step (default `Documents\Focus Notes`, the next free name, with *Change…*); *More options* keeps *Choose another folder…*. Settings → Workspace shows the folder with *Show in File Explorer*, *Move…* and *Open another folder…*. *Move…* renames on the same drive; across drives it copies, counts and checks every file, can be cancelled until the switch, then asks *Send the old folder to the Recycle Bin?*. *Open another folder…* takes any folder of Markdown notes, read in place. | Proposed | [Where your notes live → Choosing and moving the folder](../experience/notes-folder.md#choosing-and-moving-the-folder), [First run](../experience/first-run.md), [Requirements](../product/requirements.md) FRE-14 |
| D-215 | **A folder another service keeps in sync is a supported notes folder (the owner, 24 October: "a user might want to keep this folder in their own cloud solution like google drive, Onedrive or iCloud and the sync is done by that and not by Github").** OneDrive, Google Drive, iCloud Drive and Dropbox folders are recognised by their paths and the OneDrive environment variables, and the corner names the service ("Saved here · OneDrive keeps it in sync"). GitHub backup is off in such a folder and `.git` isn't made there: a Git folder in a synced folder is a known source of broken repositories. *Use it anyway* after a warning allows it. New GitHub folders go to `%USERPROFILE%\Focus Notes\<name>` when Documents is synced. Online-only notes aren't downloaded to list or search them, a save the service holds is retried for up to 5 s, and the service's conflict copies show as ordinary notes. P1, built in M5 with D-214. | Proposed | [Where your notes live → Folders another service keeps in sync](../experience/notes-folder.md#folders-another-service-keeps-in-sync), [Requirements](../product/requirements.md) FRE-15, [Risks](risks.md) R-22, R-23 |
| D-216 | **Starting answers at once on Windows; a splash only in the portable (the owner, 25 October: "Let's have a splash screen that shows up immediately and make it bearable to wait for the actual app to launch… How can I test the installed app launch speed").** Measured on the owner's machine: the installed app (the same files as `release\win-unpacked`) shows its window and caret in about 0.4 s once Windows has it in memory, and about 3.5 s on the first start, of which about 3.1 s pass before the app's own code runs (Windows loading and scanning the executable). The portable takes 7–8 s on every start, because electron-builder's portable launcher deletes and unpacks the whole app into a temporary folder each time. So no splash window inside the app (it would show after the slow part and flash for a moment); the portable's launcher shows `build/portable-splash.bmp` (the icon on Paper's canvas, drawn from the icon's design) while it unpacks. `pnpm bench:launch` times any build from outside through `FOCUS_NOTES_LAUNCH_LOG`, a file the app appends clock times to, and nothing else. The installer is the fast path the Store build follows. | Proposed | [Desktop app → Start and close](../experience/desktop-app.html#start-and-close), [Requirements](../product/requirements.md) DSK-10 |
| D-217 | **Closing the window keeps Focus Notes in the tray on Windows (the owner, 25 October: "Can we also do a thing where closing minimize to systray so that it's extremely fast to open again").** On by default. Closing the main window hides it at once, its changes are saved as on any close, and a backup runs; the app keeps running with an icon in the notification area. A click on the icon, the Start menu, the taskbar or a link shows the same window again, in front. The icon's menu has *Open Focus Notes*, *Keep running when closed* (the switch) and *Quit Focus Notes*; signing out of Windows and shutting down quit as before. The first time, a notice says the app is still running. `tray.json` in the app's data folder keeps the switch and the notice. Windows opened for a note close normally. Journeys keep the old close unless they opt in (`FOCUS_NOTES_E2E_TRAY=1`), and never show the icon (D-141). A switch in Settings and starting at sign-in can follow. | Proposed | [Desktop app → Start and close](../experience/desktop-app.html#start-and-close), [Requirements](../product/requirements.md) DSK-11, [Security and privacy → Data inventory](../architecture/security-and-privacy.md#data-inventory) |
| D-218 | **The portable's splash looks like the app and says what's happening (the owner, 25 October: "The splashscreen should have rounded corners just like the app, same value. Also show the title of the app and any ongoing process in subtle text below it").** Amends D-216. The splash window is cut to the window's radius (`radius.window`, 20 px) and shows *Focus Notes* under the icon and one muted status line: *Starting…*, *Unpacking the app… N%* (whole percents from the unpacking), *Opening your notes…*. It stays until the app's window has shown, read from the launch log (`FOCUS_NOTES_LAUNCH_LOG`, set to a temporary file when it isn't set), the app ends, or 30 s pass; it never takes the focus. electron-builder's portable template can't be replaced by configuration, so `patches/app-builder-lib@26.15.3.patch` (a `pnpm patch`) makes it include a script beside the splash image when there is one, and leaves it as it was otherwise; the installer is unchanged. `scripts/portable-splash.mjs` writes both the bitmap and `build/portable-splash.nsh`, and `check:generated` keeps them current. Light theme only; Segoe UI. The patch has to be redone when electron-builder is updated. | Proposed | [Desktop app → Start and close](../experience/desktop-app.html#start-and-close), [Requirements](../product/requirements.md) DSK-10 |
| D-219 | **Crash recovery's choices beyond its spec (WP-8.9, building D-190 and Architecture → Crash recovery).** (1) **Told once, by window:** `found()` is told to the first window that asks after a launch, and again to a window whose renderer was replaced (the crash cover's restore), never to another; other asks get nothing. The shell keeps in memory which window owns each draft (its web contents); a draft a window keeps is never found by another while it lives, and `keep`, `saved` and `discard` from another window are ignored. Drafts told to a window are adopted by it and their sequence starts again at 0, so the window, which counts from 1, isn't dropped. (2) **The old journal's notes from other folders:** at the first launch with the draft store, the journal's unsaved notes inside the open workspace become drafts there (sequence 0, their last base) and the ladder decides; a note outside it can't wait for its own folder, since the journal held no workspace, so it becomes a Recovered draft of the open workspace named by its file name; the journal file is then deleted. A journal entry already older than 30 days goes like any Recovered draft. (3) **The app's own batch writes:** a batch that rewrites a note with a draft (links after a rename) deletes the draft when its text is now on disk, and otherwise moves its base to the new text, so the next launch matches it instead of calling it changed. (4) **Renames outside the app while it runs:** the watcher's report of a note gone and one appearing with the same file ID moves its draft (notes only; a folder renamed outside the app is left to the launch ladder). (5) **Closing without saving** deletes that window's drafts only. (6) **Unreadable drafts:** a draft file that can't be read is kept as long as a Recovered draft would be (30 days from its last change), then deleted; one encrypted with a key store that's gone stays out of every list. (7) Diagnostics keep the report's existing "unsaved text for N note(s), B bytes" line, now the drafts' count and size; matched, changed and recovered are counted in the log. | Accepted | [Crash recovery](../architecture/crash-recovery.md), [Requirements](../product/requirements.md) QLT-06 |
| D-220 | **How the window puts recovered text back (crash recovery, the renderer side).** Choices the spec leaves open, under D-190. A note gets its draft ID at its first unsaved edit and keeps it through renames and moves the app makes; each handover counts up, and a save confirms the count taken when it started (or the latest one when the text didn't change in between). Recovered text reaches the editor as the `note.recover` action (`{ markdown, key }`), sent only to the editor built for that note, and replaces the text as one undo step: the editor adopts the recovered source so its blocks keep their formatting, and one undo reads back exactly the text on disk. If the person typed in the open note before the answer came, what they typed is saved first and then compared with the recovered text: equal drops the draft, different opens the resolver. A matched note that isn't open is read again when it's opened; if the disk changed since launch it goes to the resolver like a changed note. The resolver has a third kind, *recovered*, labelled **Recovered** and **On disk**, with *Keep recovered text*; the version not kept goes where the outside-change flow puts it (DD-FB-04), and replacing the unsaved text discards its draft. Files says *unsaved* on every note with recovered text waiting, matched or changed, until it's opened. `recovery.review` opens the first changed note, else the Recovered list when it exists (`files.showRecovered`); it isn't offered in Spotlight. The toast speaks of the app, never as *we* (Words): *Kept 2 drafts that couldn't go back in their notes.* Moves made outside the app (seen by the watcher) don't carry a waiting draft along; it stays under the old path. | Accepted | [Crash recovery](../architecture/crash-recovery.md), [Requirements](../product/requirements.md) QLT-06, WP-8.9 |
| D-221 | **Files → Recovered (crash recovery S3, WP-8.9).** Choices the spec leaves open, under D-190. **Where:** Recovered shows in place of the tree, like Recently deleted (D-189): a footer button *Recovered · N*, only on the desktop and only while there are drafts, which becomes *Browse files* while the list shows; Recently deleted and Recovered never show at once. It's listed when the launch has sorted what the last session left, and again after each draft goes; with none left it closes and the tree comes back. **Rows:** each draft is one pressable card (its title, *Was in “folder” · date time · N characters*, and a *Kept until ⟨date⟩* badge); opening one shows its text read-only, as plain blocks (the resolver's version pane), with *Save as a new note…*, *Copy text* and *Discard*, and back leads to the list with that row focused. **Save as a new note…** uses the Move to… picker, limited to the draft's own section (Private or Public) so saving never changes who can read the text; the folder it was in is chosen when it's still there, else the top of its section. It's written as a new note through the workspace's normal create, under a free name (the shell's create never writes over a file), without the note ID in its front matter (the new note gets its own); the draft is deleted only once the write is confirmed, and there's no Undo (it would delete the only copy). A failed write keeps the draft and says so. **Discard** asks first (*Discard this draft?*, naming its length; it can't be undone). **Commands:** *Recovered drafts* (`files.showRecovered`) is in Spotlight on the desktop; the rows' own steps aren't. **Signing out and removing the workspace** already delete this computer's drafts (Crash recovery → Retention); their confirmations add *N unsaved drafts on this computer will be deleted too* when there are some (removing, once it can go ahead). | Accepted | [Crash recovery](../architecture/crash-recovery.md), [Requirements](../product/requirements.md) QLT-06, WP-8.9 |
| D-222 | **Crash recovery's proof and the choices it surfaced (WP-8.9 slice D, under D-190).** (1) **Removing the workspace or signing out** deletes the open workspace's drafts (the store's `removeAll`), then the app's `recovery/` folder only if it's left empty, so another workspace's drafts are never deleted with this one; the data inventory lists `recovery/` as removed by both and by *Delete data folder*, and `recovery-journal.jsonl` only as an old file read once and deleted (the journal is migrated, never written; a clean quit removes nothing). (2) **Matching at launch reads the workspace once:** the first rung that looks through the workspace reads every note once for all three keys (front-matter `id`, file ID, text hash), 32 files at a time, and the drafts' ladders run together, each on its own queue. Only reads (R1). Found by the bench: one note at a time in three passes took 116 ms for 20 drafts in 10,000 notes and 29.8 s when a note had moved; now 10.7 ms and 3.4 s. (3) **The worst case is reported, not budgeted:** `fn:recovery.match` has the spec's 30 ms budget (`QLT-06.recoveryMatchMs`) for drafts whose notes are where they were; when a note was moved or deleted outside the app every rung reads the workspace, which the bench reports as `QLT-06.recoveryMatchMovedMs` (about 3.4 s with 10,000 notes). It runs off the first frame; the note on screen is never held by it. `node tools/bench/run.mjs --only "QLT-06"` runs that scenario alone. (4) **The rename storm** runs 40 rounds on CI and 8 locally (`FOCUS_NOTES_STORM_ROUNDS`, replayable with `FOCUS_NOTES_FAULT_SEED`), and types only once the new note's own editor is open, since *New note* writes the empty file before it opens the note. | Accepted | [Crash recovery](../architecture/crash-recovery.md), [Security and privacy → Data inventory](../architecture/security-and-privacy.md#data-inventory), [Requirements](../product/requirements.md) QLT-06, WP-8.9 |
| D-223 | **The Microsoft Store's screenshots: a smaller window at twice the scale, a cascade of themes and fonts, and the setup with GitHub (owner, after v1.0.2; amends D-202 (11)).** The owner asked for screenshots at a smaller window width, one composition of up to four windows cascading in different themes and fonts, and a few of the setup with GitHub. (1) **Window:** the `store` review catalogue now runs its own journey, `e2e/store-catalog.spec.ts`, with the app's window at 1120 × 720 drawn at twice the scale (`--force-device-scale-factor=2`, so 2240 × 1440 device pixels), resized exactly on CI only (D-141). `tools/release/store-screenshots.py` scales each window to fit the 2560 × 1440 canvas inside a 120 × 90 margin, so the writing reads about 1.75 times as large as in the old 1920 × 1080 window, and refuses a capture of another size unless `--preview` (a local run). (2) **Cascade:** one note, focus mode off, in four windows from the back to the front: Dusk with the monospaced font, Sepia with Atkinson Hyperlegible, Harbor dark with the sans-serif font, and Paper with the serif font, each at 0.5625 of a single window's size, stepped from the top left to the bottom right on the front window's (light) backdrop. (3) **Setup with GitHub:** a new install against the journeys' fake GitHub, already signed in to Git as Ada with her picture: Welcome, Connect (*Continue as Ada*), Your notes (*Create my notes*), Creating held mid-way at the deploy key (4 of 9), and the canvas with the Backup popover. No real account or network is used. (4) **Scenes and captions:** ten, the Store's most: the note, the cascade, Spotlight, Files, Themes, then the five setup scenes; Ink alone is dropped, since the cascade shows the dark themes. Each has a caption in `store/listing.json` (`screenshots.captions`), checked against Partner Center's 200 characters and shown in `store/listing.en-US.md` in upload order.
