The site's scenes keep `site-catalog.spec.ts`; both share `e2e/scene-capture.ts`, and the setup's fakes are shared with the FRE catalogue in `e2e/fre-fakes.ts`. | Accepted | [Microsoft Store](microsoft-store.md), D-202, PKG-12 |
| D-224 | **Screenshot code never waits on the desktop journeys (the owner, 26 October: "Why does it take so long to generate a few screenshots and merge it? … something is fundamentally broken"; amends D-206).** The Store screenshots were ready in minutes, then their code sat about 50 minutes on the full Windows journey suite, because the capture spec lives under `apps/desktop/e2e/` and an unrelated flaky editor journey failed on every retry. (1) **CI drops capture-only files before the path test:** the screenshot catalogues (`apps/desktop/e2e/*-catalog.spec.ts`), their helpers (`scene-capture.ts`, `fre-fakes.ts`) and the Store screenshot and listing scripts (`tools/release/store-screenshots.py`, `store-logos.py`, `store-listing.mjs`). None ships in the app, and they run for real on `review-captures`, which is their check. `store-assets.mjs` stays gated, because its images go into the package. (2) **The agent follows the same list:** such changes, with `store/` and `tools/review/`, go straight to `main` after `pnpm check`, the desktop typecheck and a local capture. (3) **Deliver first:** screenshots, builds and release files are handed to the owner as soon as they're checked; any merge happens after, never before. | Accepted | [AGENTS.md → Working modes](../../AGENTS.md#working-modes-and-throughput-d-113), `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, D-206, D-223 |
| D-225 | **The Store's logo images, and no Xbox (the owner, 26 October: "I need posters also … What if someone connects keyboard to Xbox and want to use the app there").** (1) **No Xbox:** the package targets `Windows.Desktop` and runs as a full-trust desktop app (`runFullTrust`), which Xbox doesn't run, so the listing reaches Windows 10 and 11 PCs only. Microsoft requires the poster and box art only for Xbox; for apps they aren't the main logo but may show in search and collections. (2) **Images:** `tools/release/store-logos.py` draws them from the icon's own geometry (`icon.mjs` `render`), so they match the package's tiles, into `store/logos/`: the 300, 150 and 71 px Store display images, the poster (1440 × 2160) and the box art (2160 × 2160). The poster and box art put the icon, "Focus Notes" in Segoe UI Variable Semibold and "A calm writing app." on the screenshots' light backdrop, all inside the top two thirds, where the Store may lay text over the rest. The package's own logos are unchanged. | Accepted | [Microsoft Store](microsoft-store.md), D-223, D-224 |
| D-226 | **The Store's 16:9 Super hero art (the owner, 26 October: "I need this also").** `tools/release/store-screenshots.py` also writes `store/hero/super-hero-3840x2160.png`: the four-theme cascade from the screenshots (D-223) on the same light backdrop, at 3840 × 2160 with wider margins, and no product title or other added text, as Microsoft requires. The windows are centred, since some Store layouts crop the top and bottom. It shows at the top of the listing on Windows 10 and 11 when there's no trailer. | Accepted | [Microsoft Store](microsoft-store.md), D-223, D-225 |
| D-227 | **One work folder beside the repository (the owner, 26 October: "My working folder is a mess with too many folders and worktrees … put all focus notes related work in a consolidated folder and not as peer to the main folder").** `E:\Projects\side-projects` also holds the owner's other projects, so Focus Notes keeps just two folders there: `focus-notes` (the repository) and `focus-notes-work`, which holds `worktrees\<branch>`, `preview\` (the Preview, its scheduled task and desktop shortcuts moved with it), `release\<version>\`, `review\<label>\` (`fetch-captures.mjs` writes there), `logs\builder-progress.log`, `handoffs\`, `scratch\` and `archive\`. On 26 October 84 worktrees and 25 leftover folders were removed; none held a commit missing from GitHub, and the one uncommitted edit and two probe specs went to `archive\uncommitted-2026-10-26\`. A worktree is removed with its local branch once the branch lands. `close-test-apps.ps1` looks for test apps in the repository and `focus-notes-work\worktrees`, and candidates in `focus-notes-work\preview\candidates`. Amends D-122's and D-146's paths. | Accepted | `AGENTS.md`, `tools/review/fetch-captures.mjs`, `tools/dev/close-test-apps.ps1`, D-122, D-146 |
| D-228 | **The Store package carries its own Git (the owner, 27 October: "Option B it is", after certification of 1.0.2 failed under policy 10.1.5, *The product promotes acquiring software outside the Store*, found at "Install the helper app"; amends D-22 and D-20).** (1) **What:** GitHub Desktop's own Git build, dugite-native `v2.56.0-2` (Git 2.56.0.windows.4, Git Credential Manager 2.9.1, Git LFS 3.8.1), unchanged, at `resources\git` in the MSIX: about 105 MB in 375 files unpacked, 47 MB compressed. It's pinned by tag, asset and SHA-256 in `apps/desktop/build/git-bundle.json`. `tools/release/git-bundle.mjs` downloads it from the GitHub release, refuses any other hash, unpacks it to `apps/desktop/vendor/git/` (ignored by Git) and runs it once as a check; `dist-store.mjs` adds it to the package and won't build without it, and `store-manifest.mjs` fails a package that lacks it. It's a release file on GitHub fetched at build time, not a package from a registry, so the package-source policy doesn't route it, and the app itself makes no new connection. (2) **Which Git runs:** a packaged app with `resources\git\cmd\git.exe` always uses it, even when Git is installed, as GitHub Desktop does, so the Store build never depends on other software; otherwise `git` from `PATH`. In a development run only (`electron .`), `FOCUS_NOTES_GIT_BUNDLE` can name a bundle folder for local checks, as an absolute path on this computer; a packaged app ignores it, and a relative path or a network share is refused. Every Git call goes through it (saving and sync, setup, sign-in, the command-line tool and MCP), and the sign-in helpers' `PATH` starts with its `cmd` folder, so Git Credential Manager's own sign-in finds it. The person's own Git settings, hooks and credentials still apply: the bundle's system settings are Git for Windows' defaults (`credential.helper=manager`, `core.autocrlf=true`), and Windows Credential Manager, which keeps the sign-in, is the same one the terminal's Git uses. (3) **Nothing to install:** with Git bundled, no screen, command or link offers to install or get Git, Git Credential Manager or the GitHub CLI. The install buttons, the winget command and *Get the GitHub CLI* are hidden (`vm.firstRun.canInstall`), the copy command is disabled, the shell refuses the `git`, `gcm` and `gh` pages, and a test holds every view node that opens them to `canInstall`. A bundled Git that won't start says "Focus Notes can't start Git" and *Repair Focus Notes in Settings → Apps*; a sign-in helper turned off in the person's own Git settings says so, and the app never changes their settings. The GitHub CLI is still used when it's already installed. Diagnostics say the Git is the one that came with Focus Notes. (4) **Only the Store package:** the installer and the portable stay small and keep using the system's Git (D-22); the resolver works for any packaged build, so they can opt in later. (5) **Licensing:** Git and the MSYS2 runtime are GPL-2.0, an exception to D-20 because they're separate programs the app starts, not linked code; GCM, Git LFS and dugite-native are MIT. `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` lists them under *Programs in the Microsoft Store package* with the exact source of each, and the bundle's own `LICENSE.txt` ships beside it. (6) **Keeping it current:** `node tools/release/git-bundle.mjs --latest` names a newer dugite-native release, and every Store release checks it (R-26). (7) The resubmission is 1.0.3, the next version the Store accepts. | Accepted | [First run → Getting Git](../experience/first-run.md#getting-git), [Microsoft Store](microsoft-store.md#git-in-the-store-package), [Distribution](distribution.md), PKG-13, WP-5.17, R-26 |
| D-229 | **Alt+Enter is full screen too (the owner, 28 October: "We also need a shortcut to a true fullscreen functionality, like Alt+Enter").** On Windows and Linux <kbd>Alt</kbd>+<kbd>Enter</kbd> runs `window.fullScreen` as well as <kbd>F11</kbd>, as in Windows games, media players and Windows Terminal; it's the same full screen (the whole display, no corners, margin or shadow, the band hidden until the pointer reaches the top edge). It's an alternate, so menus keep showing <kbd>F11</kbd> and the cheat sheet lists both. macOS keeps <kbd>⌃⌘F</kbd> only, because <kbd>⌥↩</kbd> is a text key there, and the web build binds neither (the browser keeps <kbd>F11</kbd>; ⋯ uses the Fullscreen API). No editor, table or Spotlight key uses <kbd>Alt</kbd>+<kbd>Enter</kbd>, and <kbd>Ctrl</kbd>+<kbd>Alt</kbd>+<kbd>Enter</kbd> (Replace all) still needs <kbd>Ctrl</kbd>. | Accepted | [Keyboard shortcuts](../experience/keyboard-shortcuts.md), [Desktop app](../experience/desktop-app.html#window-behavior), DSK-04 |
| D-230 | **Print (the owner, 28 October: "We need print as a capability … We can let the standard stuff be handled by the printing controls from the OS and the print driver").** (1) **The system's dialog:** ⋯ → *Print…*, the File menu and Spotlight print the open note through the system's own print dialog (the browser's on the web), which keeps the printer, paper, orientation, scale, copies, pages and *Save as PDF*. The app has no print settings of its own. (2) **No shortcut:** <kbd>Mod</kbd>+<kbd>P</kbd> stays Quick open, as in VS Code and Notion. (3) **The page:** the note's blocks, read-only, on the Paper palette whatever the theme on screen, on white, at 12 pt (16 px) whatever the zoom, with 0.75 in margins (`cmp.printPage.*`). Front matter never prints, and the note's title names the job, so *Save as PDF* suggests it as the file's name. (4) **Page numbers:** `n / N` in the bottom margin's center, in the interface font and the muted text color; off by default and on every page when turned on (amended by D-231). (5) **Page breaks:** the pages flow on their own. They never break just after a heading or inside a table row, a list item or a quote's paragraph, and a paragraph keeps at least two lines on each side of a break. Where the writer wants a new page, a line with only `<!-- pagebreak -->` (or `<!-- page-break -->`), or an empty `<div style="page-break-after: always"></div>` or `break-before: page` as Typora and Obsidian write it, starts one; on screen and on published pages it's the note's own text, shown as written. (6) **The screen doesn't change:** the paper layout is a hidden layer (`PrintPage`) shown only to the printer, through the task tracker's *Preparing to print…*, and gone when the dialog closes; a failure toasts, and cancelling says nothing. (7) **Not yet:** headers and footers with the title or date, a print preview, images (notes don't render them yet), PDF export with its own options, and printing several notes at once. | Accepted | [Print](../experience/print.md), SHR-12, WP-8.12, [Perceived performance](../architecture/perceived-performance.md) |
| D-231 | **Page numbers when printing, off by default (the owner, 28 October: "page numbers should be off by default and should have an on/off option").** Amends D-230 (4). (1) **The option:** *Page numbers when printing*, a checkbox right after *Print…* in ⋯ → Note, the File menu and Spotlight (`print.pageNumbers`); off by default, as in Typora, Craft and Ulysses, and kept on this device (`printPageNumbers` in the app state, like the docked contents). (2) **When on:** `n / N` at the foot of every page, page 1 included, since the writer asked for them (Typora, Markdown PDF and Craft number page 1 too); when off, no page carries a number. (3) **How:** the PrintPage layout's `pageNumbers` prop picks the named page `fn-print-numbered` (the margin and the number) over `fn-print` (the margin only), both written by the tokens build. | Accepted | [Print](../experience/print.md), SHR-12, WP-8.12 |
| D-232 | **Paragraph focus (the owner, 29 October: "highlight the entire current paragraph rather than just the current line").** Replaces the seed's visual-row focus unit without editing the seed. The full-width band covers the nearest text block: a paragraph including wraps and hard breaks, a heading, or a code block. In lists, quotes and tables it covers the containing paragraph, not the whole container; adjacent table content at the same height remains bright because the mask still spans the canvas. A selection covers complete endpoint paragraphs and everything between them. Dimming, toggles, persistence, frame batching, live resize, reduced motion and print behavior stay unchanged. | Accepted | [Focus mode](../experience/focus-mode.md), CP-FOCUS-01, SC-WRITE-02, UC-FOCUS-02, FOC-01 to FOC-06, WP-1.7 |

## Rewrite with AI decisions (30 October 2026)

The owner's request is to specify, not implement. The foundation choices below are proposed implementation contracts; only the named owner gate can approve real egress. Long-form [engine](../architecture/rewrite-engine.md), [providers](../architecture/rewrite-providers.md), [history](../architecture/rewrite-history.md) and [experience](../experience/rewrite-with-ai.md) refine them without implying delivery.

| ID | Decision and reason | Status | Source and affected pages |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| D-233 | **F1: desktop first, Windows first; external integration client only.** Core owns state/commands, desktop main owns API transport/key storage, ui/views own controls, editor owns mapping/apply/decorations. Focus Notes never installs, downloads, embeds, launches, hosts or executes models/model processes; local endpoints are separately managed and already running. Browser rewrite is excluded until its secure boundary is proven. | Proposed | Owner rewrite request and client-only correction, 2026-10-30; D-112, D-211; [Engine](../architecture/rewrite-engine.md), [Providers](../architecture/rewrite-providers.md), RWR-25, RWR-48 |
| D-234 | **F2: protected in-place preview, never an implicit save.** Canonical original is durable before Send. Hold target autosave/ordinary commits through review until Accept/Discard. Ordinary recovery contains original plus independent edits, never generated fragments; complete variations stay in isolated history. Leaving discards only the mapped target and retains outside edits/history. Accept creates one canonical undo event; projection navigation/Discard before Accept is not a canonical edit. This supersedes review autosave so unaccepted text cannot reach a remote. | Proposed | Owner asks for inline rewriting and undo, 2026-10-30; coordinator safety clarification and canonical/projection correction, 2026-10-30; D-190; [Engine](../architecture/rewrite-engine.md), RWR-23, RWR-24 |
| D-235 | **F3: completed variations are version-control commits in isolated local-only history storage.** Accepted text follows existing guarded save and normal commits; no amend/reset/rebase/force-push. Do not prescribe workspace side refs: external push --mirror could publish them. The history contract must prove isolation even against that operation. Commit messages contain neither prompt nor note text. | Proposed | Owner variation/version-control request, 2026-10-30; coordinator recon safety finding, 2026-10-30; D-23, D-133; [History](../architecture/rewrite-history.md), RWR-61 to RWR-66 |
| D-236 | **F4: disabled until setup; bring your own external OpenAI-compatible API.** No Focus Notes server/account/telemetry or silent fallback. One client adapter shape supports cloud and independently running user-attested local services without installing/downloading/embedding/launching/hosting/executing models or runtimes. ai-integration is planning guidance only, no scaffold or kit installation. This recommends a contract, not a live-tested service. | Proposed (owner) | Owner request and client-only correction, 2026-10-30; PRV-01, D-01; [ai-integration method](https://github.com/prabinpebam/ai-integration); [Providers](../architecture/rewrite-providers.md) |
| D-237 | **Mod+K rewrites; link.edit moves to Mod+Shift+J.** This chord is free in the current shortcut catalogue and core registry. Mod+Shift+K is table-row deletion and Ctrl+Alt risks AltGr, so neither is safe. Spotlight retains its scoped Mod+K actions. One-time device hint, menus, cheat sheet, Help and tests migrate at feature release, not with this spec. This is a deliberate local departure from the common Mod+K link precedent to honor the owner's direct key request. | Proposed | Owner explicitly requests Ctrl+K, 2026-10-30; D-16; [Shortcut catalogue](../experience/keyboard-shortcuts.md), core format/keymap/Spotlight registries checked 2026-10-30; KEY-08, EDT-13 |
| D-238 | **Consent names the destination, model and scope.** Private local use requires locality attestation, private remote use per-request approval; changing the profile invalidates consent. Local URLs do not prove on-device inference. No provider is silently enabled, and shared private text follows the same policy. | Proposed (owner) | CP-SAFE-01, PRV-01, D-236; [Providers](../architecture/rewrite-providers.md), RWR-43 |
| D-239 | **Bound cost before sending.** Recommend 64 KiB input, 8,192 output tokens, one request at a time, 20/day/profile; confirm cloud requests above 8 KiB and configured monetary threshold. Unknown price is unknown, never free. No automatic billable retry. Owner approval is required for pricing sources and actual caps before a real adapter is enabled. | Proposed (owner) | No accidental spending derived from optional BYO provider, D-236; [Providers](../architecture/rewrite-providers.md), RWR-44 |
| D-240 | **Source-aware mapping preserves bytes outside the confirmed target.** Exact splices where provable; otherwise preview expansion to a whole top-level source block, or refuse. Hashes guard apply; front-matter ID is untouched. Model output is untrusted supported Markdown, never active HTML or a trigger for fetches. | Proposed | EDT-10 and R-03; owner's selected-text request, 2026-10-30; [Engine](../architecture/rewrite-engine.md), RWR-21, RWR-22, RWR-27 |
| D-241 | **Rewrite joins existing save and conflict lanes.** Checkpoint acknowledgement precedes stream. Guarded apply observes afterWrites and exact-content checks; crashes and cancellation restore safely without erasing outside edits. New files are atomic, collision-safe, new-ID and same-visibility. A history success is not a successful note save. | Proposed | D-190, D-221, D-222; CP-SAFE-01; [Engine](../architecture/rewrite-engine.md), RWR-23, RWR-28, RWR-29 |
| D-242 | **Fixture-first delivery, then a separate owner gate.** Engine, prompt, fresh effect and local history are visible using an offline fake before choosing a service. Real adapters depend on recorded destination/auth/privacy/consent/cost and Store/privacy-claims approval. Rollback disables new sends, not writing or history recovery. | Proposed | Owner says not to block spec on provider selection; D-206; [Implementation plan](implementation-plan.md#mai), RWR-45, RWR-46 |
| D-243 | **Fresh text is ephemeral, accessible decoration.** Recommend 1.5 s hold and 2.5 s ease-out fade; all frames meet 4.5:1. Selection/caret/spelling/focus band retain priority; reduced motion, forced colors and effect-off show regular text. Review status is the non-color signal. No generated style enters Markdown or clipboard. | Proposed | Owner gradient request, 2026-10-30; D-232, A11Y-01; [Design](../design/rewrite-with-ai.md), RWR-08, FOC-07 |
| D-244 | **Bounded local history, not workspace publication.** Recommend 20 variations/session, 30 days, 100 MiB/store; never evict unresolved original checkpoints. Private stores remain isolated; non-Git/unborn folders use durable device-local storage without initializing Git. History implementation must prove mirror-push exclusion and explain that deleting a ref is not secure object erasure. | Proposed | CP-SAFE-01, D-235, D-190; [History](../architecture/rewrite-history.md), RWR-63 to RWR-66 |

## Rewrite engine proposals (30 October 2026)

These are specification choices, not implemented behavior. Real provider, privacy, consent and cost approval remains an owner gate. D-249's detailed [limits](../architecture/rewrite-providers.md#limits-and-errors) supersede D-239's provisional numeric defaults; D-247's [retention](../architecture/rewrite-history.md#retention) tightens D-244's variation cap from session to note, retaining its 100 MiB/domain store limit. Prompt input remains 4,000 code points/16 KiB to match RWR-03/RWR-04; all instruction tokens count toward context/cost rather than a hidden smaller prompt limit.

| ID | Decision and source | Status | Affects |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| D-245 | **Source-aware target mapping and exact byte splice.** Add plain-text leaf source spans; exact partial targeting only when unambiguous and structurally safe, otherwise visibly expand to the complete top-level source block. SHA-256 and exact UTF-8 comparison prove untouched prefix/suffix bytes, BOM/EOL and front matter survive. Existing `packages/markdown/src/index.ts` preserves top-level source blocks but has no inline map; its serializer can canonicalize separators near changed blocks. Source: EDT-10, D-70 and current parser/serializer recon, 30 October 2026. | Proposed | [Rewrite engine → Selection mapping](../architecture/rewrite-engine.md#selection-mapping), RWR-21, RWR-22, EDT-15, WP-AI.1 |
| D-246 | **Hold target-note autosave and ordinary commits through review.** Sending, streaming and review render a protected projection; canonical original plus independent edits are kept for recovery, never generated fragments. Accept alone creates one ordinary undoable replacement; leaving, switching, closing and quitting default to Discard, preserve outside edits/complete variation and save canonical text through the existing guarded lane. The five-second close handshake cannot accept silently. Source: QLT-06/D-190, D-92 and existing 60-second ordinary backup cadence. Amends the initial D-234 review-autosave hypothesis. | Proposed | [Rewrite engine → Save and checkpoints](../architecture/rewrite-engine.md#save-and-checkpoints), [Crash recovery](../architecture/crash-recovery.md), RWR-23, RWR-24, RWR-28 |
| D-247 | **No-remote app-local Git history, not workspace side refs.** Plumbing commits use `refs/focus-notes/rewrite/v1/<opaqueNoteKey>/<sessionUuid>` in separate userData public/private bare stores; private blobs/index are OS-encrypted. No alternates, remote, workspace parent/index or branch mutation. Undo/redo appends states; retention deletes unpublished session refs. A namespace inside the workspace could escape via external `push --mirror`; isolated stores also handle non-Git/unborn folders without initializing them. Source: [Git commit-tree](https://git-scm.com/docs/git-commit-tree), consulted 30 October 2026, PRV-01/SEC-06 and current private-first Git sync boundary. Refines D-235/D-244. | Proposed | [Rewrite history](../architecture/rewrite-history.md), RWR-61 through RWR-66 |
| D-248 | **Untrusted note/output, not an agent.** Separate instruction/data fields; no model tools, secret access, output-triggered network, imports or executable HTML. Parse bounded Markdown, reject unsupported/unsafe constructs and require complete validated output before review/Accept. Prompt framing is not a security guarantee. Source: SEC-01/PRV-01, existing raw-Markdown/safe-link behavior, and ai-integration `security.md` at `1613a55775f3dd06f157a73a7b2db0ddc0f68ea7` (offline review, 30 October 2026). | Proposed | [Rewrite engine → Output validation](../architecture/rewrite-engine.md#output-validation), [Rewrite providers](../architecture/rewrite-providers.md), RWR-27, SEC-09 |
| D-249 | **Narrow default-off BYO external API contract.** Client only: no model/runtime installation, download, embedding, launch, hosting or execution; local endpoints are independently running. Recommend `chat-completions-text-v1` with reviewed route capabilities, numeric loopback-only local v1 (LAN deferred), no fallback, safeStorage references, per-use private-remote consent and strict unknown-charge blocking. Proposed admission defaults: USD 0.10/operation, USD 1/day; no automatic inference retries. Explicitly entered keys may pass transiently through a cleared key-entry field; stored plaintext never returns, avoiding an unproven native-dialog dependency. Source: owner's client-only correction, 30 October 2026; D-21/PRV-01, [Electron safeStorage](https://www.electronjs.org/docs/latest/api/safe-storage), consulted 30 October 2026, and pinned ai-integration offline method/proof table. No real provider/model/destination approved. | Proposed (owner) | [Rewrite providers](../architecture/rewrite-providers.md), RWR-41 through RWR-48, PRV-05, SEC-09 |

## Licensing policy (D-20 details)

| Class | Licenses | Rule |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Allowed | MIT, ISC, BSD-2-Clause, BSD-3-Clause, 0BSD, Apache-2.0, Unlicense, CC0-1.0, and OFL-1.1 for fonts (D-29) | Can ship. Keep their notices in a generated `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES`. |
| Case by case | MPL-2.0, CC-BY-4.0 (icons and other assets), CC-BY-3.0 (the EFF wordlist, D-52) | Needs a recorded decision here. |
| Not allowed | GPL, LGPL, AGPL, SSPL, BUSL, any proprietary or paid license (including the paid Tiptap bundles) | CI fails the build. |

A license check runs in CI from M0 onward. Development-only tools follow the same list, unless their output never ships. Tools the user installs themselves (Git, Git Credential Manager, GitHub CLI) aren't shipped, so they aren't covered. The one exception is the Microsoft Store package's own Git (D-228): Git and the MSYS2 runtime (GPL-2.0) ship there as separate programs the app starts, never linked into it, with their notices and the exact source of each in `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md`.

## Changing a decision

A change starts here, then follows [the articulation chain](../product/spec/chain.md): update the affected promise, scenario, or user-can first, then the views, components, and requirements that trace to it.
